To see this, put d=V^T(z_0-zstar). The condition zstar in Kpolar is
exactly d>=fpot and the squared norm of V G^(-1)d is the displayed
quadratic. Coercivity supplies the minimum. This proves the radius
identity without choosing a vertical minimizer.

For clarity the associated perspective has variables tau,F,d and is

    sup 2F_n-2tau c_n,
    tau>=0, F_l-F_k<=tau C_kl, F_k>=tau K_k,
    d>=F, d^T G^(-1)d<=tau.                        (3.7)

A legal fpot maps to tau=1/r(fpot), F=tau fpot and d=tau d*, with d*
minimizing (3.6). Conversely a feasible point with tau>0 gives a legal
fpot=F/tau and r(fpot)<=1/tau. Its strictly positive numerator in (3.4)
makes its score at least the objective in (3.7). Thus the two suprema
agree when the legal set is nonempty. At tau=0 positive definiteness
forces d=0, and F>=0 with F<=d forces F=0. Hence (3.7) ALWAYS includes
zero and equals the zero-adjoined supremum of legal scores. It must not
be equated to the conventional supremum of an empty initializer set.

For arbitrary nonnegative pair multipliers lambda_kl and zero-row
multipliers nu_k, put

    rho=2e_n+out(lambda)-in(lambda)+nu,
    kappa=2c_n-sum lambda_kl C_kl+sum nu_k K_k.

Here e_n is the terminal coordinate vector and out/in are row/column
sums. If rho>=0 and kappa>0, expansion gives

    rho^T fpot-kappa
      =2(fpot_n-c_n)
       +sum lambda_kl[C_kl-(fpot_l-fpot_k)]
       +sum nu_k(fpot_k-K_k) >=2(fpot_n-c_n).

Vrho is in the positive cone. For the true closest polar point,
rho^T fpot<=sqrt(r(fpot))*||Vrho||. Completing the scalar square gives

    2(fpot_n-c_n)/r(fpot) <= rho^T G rho/(4kappa).   (3.8)

Both signs and the terminal half-square are fixed in these identities.
No strong-duality premise is required. The identical derivation applies
to the two-vertex relaxation with its inherited constants and its own
two-halfspace polar.

## 4. New radial constants, derived for damping two

Uniform elementary bounds for m>=1536 are

    N>=2^16, Kmix>=N/2, 12/5<c<121/50,
    2/5<a<5/12, beta>999/1000, 4/3<T<27/20.        (4.1)

For c one can use 7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 and epsilon<1/6000.
The estimate a>2/5 follows from beta>999/1000 and c<121/50;
a<5/12 follows from beta<1,c>12/5. The inequalities for N and Kmix
follow by doubling from 2^m>=2m+2.

For l=0,1 write H_l=1+b with b=a or beta. The root identity is

    (1-rho_l)(rho_l+b)=2(1-b)E_l rho_l/T.          (4.2)

Its quadratic has negative constant term and a positive value at one,
so 0<rho_l<1. Therefore rho_l/(rho_l+b)<=1/(1+b).
From the exact energies and eta<=2/N^3,

    N E_0<1+3/N, N E_1<4,
    N(1-rho_0)<(9/14)(1+3/N)<13/20<2/3,
    N(1-rho_1)<6epsilon<1/1000.                   (4.3)

For a lower estimate in (4.2), use rho_0>999/1000, 1-a>7/12,
rho_0+a<17/12, N E_0>1, and T<27/20. This gives

    N(1-rho_0)>(280/459)(999/1000)>121/200.

Substituting the NEW x,y of (2.3), not the old law, now gives

    13/5<N(1-x)<53/20<3,
    2<N(1-y)<2001/1000<3.                          (4.4)

For example N(1-x)=N(1-rho_0)+2rho_0; its lower bound exceeds
2+121/200-4/(3N)>13/5. Put theta=x^2y and

    zeta=-(N/4)log(theta).

Integrating 1/(1-u) on [0,u] proves u<=-log(1-u)<=u/(1-u).
Consequently

    9/5<zeta<7301/[4000(1-3/N)]<11/6.              (4.5)

The last rational comparison follows from 194>132000/N.
Set r=m-2, Lq=2^r=N/4, i=Lq-1 and

    s=sigma_i=theta^(Lq/4)=exp(-zeta/4),
    t=sigma_n=theta^(N/4)=s^4.

Useful completely rational consequences are

    63/100<s<16/25, t>5/32, s^3<27/100.            (4.6)

Here is a nonnumerical exponential check. The tail of exp(u) beginning
with its quadratic term is at most (u^2/2)/(1-u/3) for 0<u<3.
At u=11/24 this gives

    exp(11/24)<35/24+121/976=4633/2928<100/63.

The first four terms of exp(9/20) already exceed 25/16. Apply (4.5)
to obtain the bounds for s. Also 32*63^4>5*100^4 proves t>5/32;
(16/25)^3<27/100 proves the last assertion. These are symbolic rational
inequalities, not evaluated trajectories or numerical sweeps.

For u_k=(k+1)/N the exact ratio sigma_k/exp(-zeta u_k), according as
k mod4 is 0,1,2,3, is

    theta^(-1/4), y^(-1/2), x^(-1/2)y^(1/4), 1.

The bounds (4.3) and the lower bound for 1-rho_0 imply rho_0<rho_1,
so x<y<1. In particular x^(-1/2)y^(1/4)>x^(-1/4)>1. By (4.4),
-log x,-log y<4/N, so each displayed logarithm is between zero and 5/N.
Using exp(u)<=1/(1-u) shows

    0<=sigma_k-exp(-zeta u_k)<8/N.                 (4.7)

At quarter bridge indices qLq-1 it is exactly zero and sigma=s^q.

## 5. Mechanism1: original short cycles and top boundary cycles

For an adjacent level-l step of ratio R_l and radius sigma, direct
expansion of (3.1) gives its two-cycle sum

    C_(k,k+1)+C_(k+1,k)
      =-T sigma^2[R_l^2+(H_l-2)(B_l^adj/T)R_l+1-H_l]. (5.1)

For l=0,1, R_l is strictly between zero and the positive root rho_l;
the other root is negative, so the bracket is strictly negative. For
l>=2 the ratio is one and the bracket is (2-H_l)(1-B_l^adj/T)<0.
Here H_l>2 and E_l>0. This proves positivity for EVERY adjacent cycle,
including the low-level nearly tight cycles, without assuming legality.

Now use the actual top boundary indices

    a0=N/2-1, b0=N/2, c0=N/2+1,
    H=H_(m-1), q0=sigma_(a0)=sigma_(b0),
    sigma_(c0)=x q0,
    Btop=B_(m-1)^adj=T-2-eta(N-2m),
    Bshort=B_0^adj, delta=2/(N-1)-2eta.

The xor labels show B_(a0,c0)=Btop+delta. From (4.1)-(4.4),

    -1<Btop<-3/5, 0<Bshort<T<3/2,
    3/(2N)<delta<3/N, x>3/4, H-1>N/5>18.

For arbitrary increasing three consecutive indices with increments
d=v_b-v_a and e=v_c-v_b, cancellation of P_a in (3.1) gives

    C_ac-C_ab-C_bc=-<(h_a-1)v_a+v_b,e>.

At the chosen boundary this is the exact scalar

    DeltaC=q0^2[(H-1)((1-x)Btop-x delta)+T-x Bshort]. (5.2)

Since x delta>1/N and T-x Bshort=T(1-x)+2xE_0<9/N,

    DeltaC<-q0^2(H-1)/(2N)<0.                     (5.3)

Also |DeltaC|<q0^2[(H-1)6/N+9/N]<q0^2 H/2.

Crucially, (5.3) alone is NOT a negative directed cycle. To check the
two orientations of the genuine triangle, first the adjacent pair
(a0,b0) has sum q0^2(H-2)(T-Btop)>2q0^2(H-2).
Together with (5.1) for (b0,c0) and the bound for |DeltaC| this gives

    C_(a0,c0)+C_(c0,b0)+C_(b0,a0)
      =DeltaC+pair_(a0,b0)+pair_(b0,c0)>0.          (5.4)

For the other orientation the nonadjacent pair sum divided by q0^2 is

    (H-2)(T-x(Btop+delta))
       +H_0(Btop-x Bshort)+T(1-x^2).

The first term exceeds (4/3)(H-2), the second exceeds -5 and the
third is positive. Thus pair_(a0,c0)>0. Hence

    C_(a0,b0)+C_(b0,c0)+C_(c0,a0)
      =pair_(a0,c0)-DeltaC>0.                     (5.5)

This establishes the claimed checked-cycle status. It says nothing
about unexamined directed cycles; the search is explicitly inconclusive
for unrestricted relative potentials.

## 6. The ONE explicit relative-potential law and its failed original row

Freeze, before testing, the following closed finite dyadic formula:

    p_0=0,
    p_k=sum_(j=0)^(k-1) [
          sum_(b=0)^(j-1) h_b sigma_b
                      (sigma_(j+1) B_(b,j+1)-sigma_j B_bj)
          +sigma_j sigma_(j+1)B_(j,j+1)-sigma_j^2 T],
    fpot_k=p_k+gamma(p).                            (6.1)

All quantities in (6.1) are given explicitly in (2.2)-(2.3) using xor,
popcount, powers and finite sums; gamma(p) is the finite formula (3.5).
This is p_k=sum_(j<k)C_(j,j+1), not a recurrence that was run or a
feasibility optimizer. No alternative relative law is fitted afterward.

Every forward adjacent row is an equality. Every backward adjacent row
passes by (5.1). Every zero row passes by (3.5). Nevertheless the
original row a0->c0 has residual

    fpot_(c0)-fpot_(a0)-C_(a0,c0)
       =-DeltaC>q0^2(H-1)/(2N)>0.                  (6.2)

Consequently no common shift repairs (6.1). This precisely located
failure is the authorized trigger for mechanism2. Since the tested
triangles in (5.4)-(5.5) are positive, we do not promote it to a
potential-independent obstruction. There is no legal full initializer
from this law, and no terminal payoff is claimed for it.

## 7. Mechanism2: exact pair data with all chronological prefixes

Retain the pair i=Lq-1,n=N-1 from Section4. Its angle and Gram are

    B=B_in=T-3N/[2(N-1)]+4eta-eta N,
    Gpair=[[s^2T,stB],[stB,t^2T]].                  (7.1)

It is positive definite. Directly from its formula and N>=2^16,

    -1<B<0, 9/8<T+B<7/6.                          (7.2)

For example T+B=7/6+2/N^2-3/[2(N-1)]-eta(N-4).
The negative correction is bounded by 3/N+2/N^2<1/24 for the lower
bound; for the upper bound use 2/N^2<3/[2(N-1)].

Here are finite closed expressions for every needed prefix, using the
new x,y. For 2<=j<=m define the polynomial

 Pcal_j(z,w)=
 [H_0 z+xH_1 z^2w+xyH_0 z^3w]
       product_(b=0)^(j-3)[1+w(theta z^4)^(2^b)]
 +sum_(l=2)^(j-1)H_l w^l(theta z^4)^(2^(l-2))
       product_(b=0)^(j-l-2)[1+w(theta z^4)^(2^(l-1+b))].             (7.3)

Products with upper index below lower index are one. This polynomial is
exactly sum_(k<2^j-1) h_k sigma_k z^(k+1)w^pop(k). To prove this, the
three low positions k=4q,4q+1,4q+2 have radii theta^q,x theta^q,
xy theta^q and popcounts pop(q),pop(q)+1,pop(q)+1. A high-level position
k=(2q+1)2^l-1 has radius theta^((2q+1)2^(l-2)) and popcount l+pop(q).
Binary expansion of q expands exactly the products in (7.3).

**Source correction:** the old report's count for level one was
floor((k+1)/4), and its low-position coefficient at k=4q+2 was xH_0
rather than xyH_0. Both omit the actual level-one step at position
4q+1. The correct count is floor((k+2)/4), derived in Section2;
formula (7.3) and the ratio table in Section4 correct it explicitly.
None of this
round's estimates imports a value from the old polynomial. The old
damping4 result is not reverified here; this discrepancy is surfaced
for its author's review.

Put

    U_j=Pcal_j(1,1), M_j=(partial_z Pcal_j)(1,1)-U_j,
    Z_j=(partial_w Pcal_j)(1,1).

These respectively sum h_k sigma_k, k h_k sigma_k, pop(k)h_k sigma_k
over k<2^j-1. Write U=U_r,M=M_r,Z=Z_r. Define

    b_i=T-2(Lq-1)/(N-1)+2eta r-eta N,
    b_n=T-2+2eta m-eta N,
    Ji=b_i U+2M/(N-1)-2eta Z=<P_i,w_i>,
    Jpre=b_n U+2M/(N-1)-2eta Z=<P_i,w_n>,
    Jn=b_n U_m+2M_m/(N-1)-2eta Z_m=<P_n,w_n>.

These identities follow from k xor i=i-k for k<i, and k xor n=n-k.
For the reverse row, put d=2Lq/(N-1) and

    Ei=sum_(q=0)^3 s^q[Ji-qdU+2eta pop(q)U]
        +H_r s T
        +H_(r+1)s^2[T-d+2eta-eta N]
        +H_r s^3[T-2d+2eta-eta N]=<P_n,w_i>.        (7.4)

Indeed in each quarter interior k=qLq+j, j<Lq-1, its radius is s^q
sigma_j and k xor i=(q+1)Lq-1-j. The three bridge indices qLq-1,
q=1,2,3, have xor against i equal to 0,Lq,2Lq. The first bridge is
an actual diagonal and contributes T, without a spurious -eta N.

Thus the four inherited constants, and the terminal constant, are

    Ki=sJi+s^2T, Kn=tJn+t^2T, c_n=tJn+t^2T/2,
    Cin=tJpre-sJi+stB-s^2T,
    Cni=sEi-tJn+stB-t^2T.                          (7.5)

Writing u=fpot_i and v=fpot_n, the exact pair domain is

    u>=Ki, v>=Kn, v-u<=Cin, u-v<=Cni.              (7.6)

These are original rows with original prefixes; no shortened two-step
trajectory is substituted. Its radius is
min_(d1>=u,d2>=v) d^T Gpair^(-1)d. Removing the other halfspaces can
only reduce the full closest-polar radius. Hence this domain with this
radius is indeed an upper relaxation of full legal scores.

## 8. Word mass and uniform prefix estimates

Set S=S_r. Exact quarter concatenation gives

    S_m=4S+2H_r+H_(r+1), D_m=8S+4H_r+2H_(r+1)+1.

We need the uniform inequalities

    S>10000, (2/5)S<H_r<(21/50)S,
    (19/20)S<H_(r+1)<(41/40)S,
    (23/2)S<D_m<12S, S_m<6S.                     (8.1)

For S use h_k>1 and S>2^r-1=N/4-1>=16383. With lambda0=1+sqrt(2),

 c(c-2)-beta=(2lambda0^2-2lambda0+1)epsilon+lambda0^2 epsilon^2
             >c epsilon.

The linear coefficient is 2lambda0+3>7 and c<5/2. Therefore
c-2-a>epsilon and 0<a/(c-2)<1. Formula (2.1) gives S<c^r, whence
H_r>aS>(2/5)S and H_(r+1)>beta S>(19/20)S. On the other hand

 H_r=(c-2)S-(c-2-a)Lq+c-1<(c-2)S<(21/50)S.

Here Lq>8m, verified at m=16 and preserved by doubling, ensures
epsilon Lq>2>c-1. Finally H_(r+1)=c H_r+1-c<(2541/2500)S<(41/40)S.
These inequalities and S>10000 prove both D_m bounds in (8.1).

The four bounds needed for feasibility of the explicit point are

    5S/6<Ji<9S/10, Jpre>-2S/5,
    Jn<S/3, Ei>3S/2, U<5S/6.                     (8.2)

All are proved next, including every tiny-character correction.
For k<n, direct substitution in (2.2) gives

    |B_kn-(-2/3+2u_k)|<3/N;

for k<i it gives

    |B_ki-(5/6+2u_k)|<3/N.                       (8.3)

For the first expansion the error is
1/N^2-2(1-u_k)/(N-1)+eta[2(m-pop(k))-N]. Its absolute value is
at most 3/N^2+2/(N-1)<3/N, using 0<=m-pop(k)<=m<N/2 and eta N<=2/N^2.
The second has -(2(1/4-u_k))/(N-1) in place of its middle term and
the same bound. Combine (4.7) and (8.3). Each product sigma_k B differs
from its exponential-times-affine expression by less than 16/N, since
the relevant affine factor has absolute value at most 4/3. Therefore
each prefix comparison below has error at most 16 times its physical
mass divided by N. The U comparison has error at most 8S/N.

Each quarter interior is exactly the original H_r clock. Its total
step mass is S and its h-weighted mean of u_k is (q+1/2)/4. Pairing
j+1 with Lq-(j+1) proves this: the two arguments have equal nu_2,
hence equal step weights. This uses clock symmetry, without resetting
or symmetrizing any gradient, radius, or state.

Let f(u)=exp(-zeta u)(5/6+2u) and g(u)=exp(-zeta u)(-2/3+2u).
On [0,1/4], f''=zeta exp(-zeta u)[zeta(5/6+2u)-4]<0.
On [0,1], g''=zeta exp(-zeta u)[zeta(-2/3+2u)-4]<0.
Both signs follow from zeta<11/6. A function with decreasing derivative
lies below its tangent at the mean and above its endpoint chord;
summing those affine bounds gives the following inequalities.

For Ji/S the chord lower bound is 5/12+2s/3>251/300; its tangent
upper bound is (13/12)sqrt(s)<13/15. Subtract/add 16/N. Since N>=2^16,
these imply 5/6<Ji/S<9/10. For Jpre/S the chord lower bound is
-1/3-s/12>-29/75; subtracting 16/N still exceeds -2/5. Convexity of
exp(-zeta u) places it below its endpoint chord, so U/S<(1+s)/2+8/N
<41/50+8/N<5/6. This proves three of (8.2).

## 9. Full terminal and reverse-prefix estimates

Apply the tangent bound for g on each of the FOUR original quarter
interiors. Their summed ideal contribution divided by S is at most

    sqrt(s)[-5+s+7s^2+13s^3]/12 < 2/15.           (9.1)

The polynomial in brackets is increasing. At s=16/25 it is
29923/15625<2. If it is nonnegative, also sqrt(s)<4/5; if it is negative,
its product is already less than 2/15. This proves (9.1) in both cases.

The three ACTUAL bridge contributions are exactly

    H_r(-s+5s^3)/6+H_(r+1)s^2/3.

The first coefficient is positive since s>63/100, and its numerator
is increasing there and is less than 7/10 at s<16/25. Also s^2<5/12.
Using (8.1), the two bridge terms divided by S are less than

    49/1000+41/288.

Together with (9.1) this is less than 13/40; the exact gap is 11/36000.
The error from (8.3)-(4.7) is less than 16S_m/N<96S/N. Since
96/N<1/120, we have Jn/S<13/40+1/120=1/3. No early negative quarter
was discarded in obtaining this upper bound.

For Ei use its EXACT formula (7.4), not a sign estimate for a changed
trajectory. Put A=1+s+s^2+s^3 and Q=s+2s^2+3s^3. Its interior sum is

    A Ji-d Q U+2eta U sum_(q=0)^3 s^q pop(q).

The last term is nonnegative, U>0, and d<1/2+1/N. From (8.2)'s
already proved Ji and U bounds, Q<3, and s>5/8, s^3<27/100,

 interior/S > (5/6)(A-Q/2)-3/N
             = (5/6)(1+s/2-s^3/2)-3/N
             >157/160-3/N.                       (9.2)

The first bridge in (7.4), divided by S, exceeds
(2/5)(5/8)(4/3)=1/3. The second exceeds
(19/20)(25/64)(4/5)=19/64, since T-d+2eta-eta N>4/5.
The third is positive, since T-2d+2eta-eta N>0. These two kernel
inequalities follow from T>4/3, d<1/2+1/N, eta N<=2/N^2.
Therefore

    Ei/S>157/160+1/3+19/64-3/N
          =1547/960-3/N>3/2.                     (9.3)

Equations (9.1)-(9.3) finish ALL estimates (8.2).

## 10. Explicit feasible point of the pair relaxation and its exact score

Choose exactly

    ell=5S/4, z_rel=ell(w_i+w_n),
    u=<z_rel,v_i>=ell s(T+B),
    v=<z_rel,v_n>=ell t(T+B).                     (10.1)

This is a point of the two-row relaxation, not a second proposed law
for all original potentials. We now check EVERY inequality in (7.6).

Zero row i: u/s=ell(T+B)>45S/32, whereas Ki/s=Ji+sT<9S/10+3/2.
Zero row n: v/t=ell(T+B)>45S/32, whereas Kn/t=Jn+tT<S/3+3/2.
Both follow from S>10000.

Forward row: divide u-v>=-Cin by s>0. Its left side is

    ell(1-s^3)(T+B)>(657/640)S,

using 1-s^3>73/100 and T+B>9/8. Its required right side is

    Ji-s^3 Jpre-tB+sT < (126/125)S+3.

Indeed Ji<9S/10, Jpre>-2S/5, s^3<27/100, -tB<1 and sT<3/2.
The difference 657/640-126/125=1485/80000>1/100, so this row passes
strictly since S>10000.

Reverse row: its right side divided by s is

    Cni/s=Ei-s^3 Jn+tB-s^7T>(141/100)S-5/2.

Use Ei>3S/2, Jn<S/3, s^3<27/100, tB>-1 and s^7T<3/2.
The required left side is ell(1-s^3)(T+B)<7S/6 because s^3>(5/8)^3
>1/5. Since 141/100>7/6 and S>10000, this row too passes strictly.

The closest-polar distance is exact, not a selected-minimizer bound.
The point z_rel=(ell/s)v_i+(ell/t)v_n is in the positive cone of the
two retained vertices. For every point y in their polar,

    ||z_rel-y||^2=||z_rel||^2+||y||^2-2<z_rel,y>
                 >=||z_rel||^2.

Zero is polar, so the closest point is zero and its squared radius is
2ell^2(T+B). In fact z_rel is also in the full original positive cone,
so the same nearest point is zero for the full original polar. This
does NOT make its proposed entire trajectory legal.

The pair's inherited formal terminal numerator is

    2(v-c_n)=2ell t(T+B)-2tJn-t^2T.

In particular the exact relaxed normalized score is

    Rpair=[2ell t(T+B)-2tJn-t^2T]/[2ell^2(T+B)],
    S Rpair=t[4/5-16 Jn/(25 S(T+B))
                         -8tT/(25 S(T+B))].       (10.2)

This expression keeps the original terminal half-square. From (8.2)
and (7.2), its bracket is greater than

    4/5-128/675-12/(25S)
       =412/675-12/(25S)>3/5.

Here t<1,T<3/2,T+B>1 bound the last subtraction, and S>10000 makes
12/(25S)<7/675. Using t>5/32 proves S Rpair>3/32. Then (8.1) gives
D_m Rpair>(23/2)(3/32)=69/64>17/16, proving (1.1).

Any pair-only upper certificate satisfying the signs in (3.8) must
bound this feasible point, and thus cannot give the requested upper
below 17/(16D_m). This conclusion does not rely on strong duality or
on knowing the optimum of the pair relaxation. It follows from an
explicit feasible point with its exact objective and distance.

## 11. Audit, background retrieval, and handoff scope

The changed radial exponent is 9/5<zeta<11/6, not the damping4 window.
Every chronological sum in Sections7-10 uses the NEW sigma_k and the
same original physical word, one enclosing m, all distinguished and
tiny characters, all bridges, and the terminal vertex. Both original
pair orientations and both zero normals are checked. The terminal
half-square and W=2R normalization are present in (10.2).

An important source discrepancy was caught in the level-one prefix
count and the finite dyadic polynomial: k=4q+2 has radius xy theta^q,
not x theta^q. The count is floor((k+2)/4). It is corrected in (2.3),
the ratio table in Section4, and (7.3). No prior unverified numerical or
symbolic score is a premise
here. The earlier damping4 source should be reviewed at that formula;
no fact is revoked, and this round makes no claim about whether that
source correction changes its separately derived score estimate.

Skills applied: query-memory, obtain-immediate-conclusions for the exact
potential interface, construct-counterexamples to audit the frozen
potential law, and search-math-results for difference-constraint
background. Matlas returned circulation-feasibility results from
arXiv1304.1810 Theorem5.1 and arXiv1302.3145 Theorem3.4; these were not
applicable premises for the explicit calculations here and were not used.
MIT 6.046J Lecture7 Section7.2.2 was read as background on telescoping
difference constraints:
https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/6-046j-design-and-analysis-of-algorithms-spring-2012/a9e76885a78c729f2375e14830caebf2_MIT6_046JS12_lec07.pdf
The required cycle implication is proved in
Section3, so no external theorem is used as a black box. This is the
previously audited fixed-Walsh/polar regime with one radial change, not
a new literature-dependent construction. No solver or shortest-path
algorithm was run or used to assert existence.

Resources: TWO substantive analytical mechanisms, ONE explicit full
relative law, ONE prescribed pair relaxation point, ONE changed radial
law. ZERO mathematical programs, numerical/arithmetic/trajectory or
recurrence replays, solvers, optimizers, benchmark DP, formal calls,
fact_submit, new fact IDs, installations, consults, or subagents. Text
retrieval, finite symbolic reasoning, persistence and administrative
hashes only. No TASK overwrite, lifecycle command, finalization,
revocation, shutdown, outward publication, Goal completion or automatic
renewal occurs. The allocated cap1 round returns its report and handoff.

Remaining mathematical question in this narrow lane: construct a legal
full potential law under damping two, or find a genuinely negative
original directed cycle. The checked forward-tight law fails, while
the prescribed pair alone cannot close the desired score threshold.
Neither fact decides full-itinerary feasibility, and neither resolves
the original unrestricted all-word exact-profile target.

# A fixed two-dimensional elliptic function with infinitely many legal copies

Status: complete unverified candidate. This is a positive cross-scale gluing
mechanism and calibration, not the unrestricted exact-profile result. The
alternating test below is already unstable on quadratics. No previous
unverified calibration is used as a premise: all core inequalities, cross
inequalities and the infinite realization are proved here. The accepted
interpolation convention and conjugate construction are fact1252b8823bb5a290.

## 1. Explicit statement

Set

    A=[[1/2,-4/5],[1/5,1/2]],     x0=(1,0),
    h=1001/1000,    b=1001,    R=-40982041/100000,
    mu=1/10000.

There is ONE everywhere-defined convex differentiable function F:R^2->R
with1-Lipschitz gradient, strong-convexity modulus mu, F(0)=min F=0, and
the following infinite GD trajectory for the fixed alternating steps h,b:

    x_(2k)=R^k x0,       g_(2k)=R^k g0,
    x_(2k+1)=R^k x1,     g_(2k+1)=R^k g1,

where

    g0=(1/2,1/5),
    x1=x0-hg0=(999/2000,-1001/5000),
    g1=A x1=(40991/100000,-1/5000).

Its values at these points are R^(2k) times respectively17/50 and1/10.
The initial norm is one. For every k>=0 and EVERY replacement final step
beta>=0 after the query at time2k+1, this same function satisfies

    2F(x_(2k+1)-beta g_(2k+1))
                   >= |R|^(2k)/4000000.                  (1)

The proof also supplies an explicit orthogonal test vector for each such
terminal query and verifies every maximal-completion anchor inequality.

## 2. A strict finite core, with all perturbation bounds

First use h_old=1 and keep f0=17/50,f1=1/10. The old next point and gradient
are x1_old=(1/2,-1/5), g1_old=(41/100,0). With
Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2, direct calculation gives

    Q_(0,star)=-3/200,       Q_(star,0)=-39/200,
    Q_(1,star)=-419/20000,   Q_(star,1)=-319/20000,
    Q_(0,1)=-519/20000,      Q_(1,0)=-219/20000.

Here ||g0||^2=29/100, ||g1_old||^2=1681/10000, and
||g0-g1_old||^2=481/10000, which verify the displayed residuals directly.

Now set epsilon=1/1000 and h=1+epsilon. We have

    x1=x1_old-epsilon g0,
    g1=g1_old-epsilon A g0,      A g0=(9/100,1/5).

All old vectors x1_old,g0,g1_old,A g0,g0-g1_old have norm at most1.
Thus ||x1-x1_old|| and ||g1-g1_old|| are at most epsilon. The two
residuals involving only query0 and the optimum are unchanged. Expanding
the bilinear and squared-norm terms bounds the absolute changes in the
other four residuals as follows:

    Q_(star,1):  epsilon+epsilon^2/2 <.001001,
    Q_(1,star):  3epsilon+3epsilon^2/2 <.003002,
    Q_(0,1):     2epsilon+epsilon^2/2 <.002001,
    Q_(1,0):     3epsilon+3epsilon^2/2 <.003002.

These estimates respectively use the expansions of ||g1||^2/2,
-<g1,x1>+||g1||^2/2, <g0,x1>+||g0-g1||^2/2, and
<g1,x0-x1>+||g0-g1||^2/2. Therefore the new core satisfies

    Q_(i,star),Q_(star,i)<=-delta,     delta=14/1000,
    Q_(0,1),Q_(1,0)<=-eta,            eta=7/1000.          (2)

Also ||x_i||<=1 and ||g_i||<=1 for i=0,1. For query1 this follows from
||x1_old||<3/5, ||g1_old||<1/2, and the preceding perturbation bounds.
Write y_i=x_i-g_i; then ||y_i||<=2.

## 3. Every cross-copy inequality, in both directions

For every integer k>=0 prescribe scaled core data

    X_(k,i)=R^k x_i,       G_(k,i)=R^k g_i,
    V_(k,i)=R^(2k) f_i,       i=0,1.

Add the optimum (0,0,0). Within one copy, all Q residuals are R^(2k)
times the corresponding core residuals. Now take two distinct copies
with absolute scale factors L>s>0; their signs can be either positive or
negative. Since |R|>400, we have L>=400s.

Define the positive optimum margins of any jet by

    delta_i^+=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
    delta_i^-=<x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2.

Both core margins are at least delta by(2), and scaling multiplies them
by the squared scale. The exact cross residual for a large jet u and
small jet v is

    Q_uv=-delta_u^- -delta_v^+ +<G_u,X_v-G_v>.

Consequently, regardless of signs and chosen core indices,

    Q_uv<=-delta(L^2+s^2)+2Ls,
    Q_vu<=-delta(L^2+s^2)+2Ls.                           (3)

No old/future plane or cross-copy inequality has been dropped.

We will need strong convexity as well. For any sampled jets define

    G_i'=(G_i-mu X_i)/(1-mu),
    V_i'=(V_i-mu||X_i||^2/2)/(1-mu).

Direct expansion of the interpolation residual gives the exact identity

    (1-mu)^2 Q_ij'
       =(1-mu)Q_ij+(mu/2)||(X_i-G_i)-(X_j-G_j)||^2.       (4)

Within one nonzero copy the right side is at most

    [-eta(1-mu)+8mu] L^2<0,

because the difference of the two core y vectors has norm at most4.
Against the optimum it is at most[-delta(1-mu)+2mu] L^2<0. For two
distinct copies, ||L y_i-(+/-s)y_j||^2<=8(L^2+s^2), so(3),(4) give

    (1-mu)^2 Q_uv'
      <=-[delta(1-mu)-4mu](L^2+s^2)+2Ls
      <=-(27/2000)(L^2+s^2)+2Ls<0.                       (5)

Here delta(1-mu)-4mu=.0135986>27/2000=.0135, and
2Ls<=L^2/200 because L>=400s. The same estimate holds for Q_vu'.
This proves ALL transformed interpolation inequalities for the entire
countably infinite set of scaled jets and the optimum.

## 4. One global interpolant for the infinite set

For each transformed sample put

    y_i'=X_i-G_i',          d_i'=V_i'-||G_i'||^2/2,
    H_i'(v)=||v||^2/2+<y_i',v>-d_i'.

The optimum anchor is H_star'(v)=||v||^2/2. The transformed strict optimum
residuals proved above imply d_i'>0 on the two unscaled core jets. For the
kth copy, y_i' scales by R^k and d_i' by R^(2k). Hence, for each fixed v,

    <R^k y_i',v>-R^(2k)d_i' -> -infinity.

The same convergence is uniform on any bounded set of v. Therefore

    H'(v)=sup_i H_i'(v),       F'(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H'(v))     (6)

defines an everywhere finite continuous1-strongly convex H'. Locally it
is the maximum of finitely many anchors. Moreover H'(v)>=||v||^2/2,
H'(0)=0, and H' is minimized at0.

For each x the conjugate supremum has a unique maximizer g(x): existence
follows from the quadratic lower bound on H', and uniqueness from its
strong convexity. The optimality inequalities for two points x,y imply
<g(x)-g(y),x-y>>=||g(x)-g(y)||^2, hence ||g(x)-g(y)||<=||x-y||.
The two supremum inequalities also bound the remainder in
F'(x+d)-F'(x)-<g(x),d> between0 and
<g(x+d)-g(x),d>, which is at most||d||^2. Thus F' is differentiable
with gradient g and1-Lipschitz gradient. It is convex and has minimum0 at0.

All interpolation inequalities Q_ij'<=0 say that at v=G_i' the i-th
anchor attains the supremum in(6). Its gradient there is X_i. Thus
X_i belongs to the subgradient of H' at G_i'; conjugate equality gives
F'(X_i)=V_i' and grad F'(X_i)=G_i'. This checks every sample, including
the infinite collection. Finally set

    F(x)=(1-mu)F'(x)+mu||x||^2/2.                        (7)

It interpolates all ORIGINAL scaled jets, is mu-strongly convex, and has
gradient Lipschitz constant at most(1-mu)+mu=1. It is one fixed function,
independent of the horizon at which the trajectory is inspected.

## 5. The actual alternating trajectory

The characteristic identity for A is

    A^2-A+(41/100)I=0.

Since h+b=hb, we obtain the exact matrix identity

    (I-bA)(I-hA)=[1-(41/100)hb]I=R I.                  (8)

Indeed hb=1002001/1000 and 1-(41/100)hb=-40982041/100000. Starting
at x0, the h step reaches x1 and the b step reaches R x0. Repetition
of(8) proves the full trajectory in Section1. Equation(7) is the SAME
function at all these query times; no oracle or function is reset.

## 6. Uniform terminal payoff and all anchors

The complex representation A=T B T^(-1), with T=diag(1,1/2) and
z=1/2+(2/5)i, gives

    |det(x1,g1)|=(1/5)|1-hz|^2.

Both |Re(1-hz)|=999/2000 and |Im(1-hz)|=1001/2500 exceed2/5.
Thus |1-hz|^2>8/25 and |det(x1,g1)|>8/125>1/20. Because ||g1||<=1,
the distance from x1 to span(g1) is greater than1/20.

Let x=R^k x1, g=R^k g1 and let z_perp be the orthogonal projection of
x onto g-perp. Then ||z_perp||^2>|R|^(2k)/400. Strong convexity of(7)
and its minimizer0 imply, for every real beta,

    2F(x-beta g)>=mu||x-beta g||^2
                  >=mu||z_perp||^2>|R|^(2k)/4000000,

which proves(1). In particular the final gradient is not forced to follow
the linear operator beyond this last query.

For the finite prefix through this query, define the ordinary conjugate
anchors H_j(v)=||v||^2/2+<x_j-g_j,v>-(f_j-||g_j||^2/2). Since F is
an actual1-smooth interpolant, H_j<=F* for every query and optimum anchor,
as in accepted1252b8823bb5a290. Strong convexity gives
F*(w)<=||w||^2/(2mu). With w=mu z_perp we therefore have <w,g>=0 and

    2(f_j-||g_j||^2/2)+2<x-(x_j-g_j),w>-||w||^2
       =2<x,w>-2H_j(w)>=mu||z_perp||^2

for EVERY anchor. Hence the same lower bound is visible in the requested
orthogonal-terminal-vector formulation as well as in the fixed function.

## 7. What this proves and what it does not

The proof gives a concrete way to glue an infinite family of legal scaled
copies while retaining every crossing constraint and a common strong
convexity modulus. Strict optimum margins pay for the cross terms when
radial scale ratios are large. This is a sufficient condition with an actual
fixed realization; it is not lossless gluing of arbitrary boundary jets.

The alternating schedule already has huge quadratic error, for example
its pair multiplier at scalar curvature1/2 is
(1-h/2)(1-b/2)=-(999/2000)(999/2), whose magnitude exceeds one.
Therefore this example does not establish a new family where the quadratic
channel misses the benchmark. Its role is to make the positive elliptic
copy mechanism concrete. For the geometric ruler family at a dyadic
resonance, the still-missing step is a legal expanding finite core and
quantitative control of its rotated copies.

Matlas returned unrelated statements in arXiv:1812.04874 and2109.09377.
Neither is imported. No numerical solver, sweep or root search was used.

# Unsaturated terminal mass: full original coefficient interface and a source-capacity obstruction

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max4, continuation invocation92, guidance
52e9d99015702be8, plan7000802aea7e2692. This is one joint analytical draft.
The current TASK grants zero formal calls; no new statement in this report is
a verifier-accepted fact. Original target OPEN. W=2R throughout.

## 1. Result and scope

The general terminal-square and original-row flow interface is derived below
without imposing q=pi^2. The proposed constant-gradient Gram is correct when
all optimum-to-query multipliers vanish. When those multipliers are allowed,
the two additional terms are displayed explicitly in (3.1).

At q0=1/D, nonnegative multipliers and the actual tight Huber trajectory force
those optimum incoming rows to vanish. The constant-gradient restriction then
forces V+1=D*pi and pi^2<=q0. For the specified terminal distribution supported
only at l=n-1, this fixes pi=b/(S+b), strictly below sqrt(q0). It leaves real
freedom in the mixed initial-point/gradient coefficients; this freedom is not
present under saturation q=pi^2.

The ONE literal paid family tested at q=17/(16D) is

 M_trial = ||g_n-pi*x0+r^T g||^2
         + delta ||x0-u^T g||^2 + gamma ||dsrc^T g||^2
         + kappa ||x0+theta*dsrc^T g||^2 + xi ||dtail^T g||^2,       (1.1)

where theta is any real number, xi>=0, and all columns and coefficients are
specified in Section5. Its paid block has rank at most two, genuine mixed x0
terms, zero-sum gradient columns, and an adjustable terminal-three detail.
That detail can pay the elementary last-query column deficit of the baseline.

Nevertheless the ENTIRE family is excluded by an exact original source cut:

 a * sum_(j=1)^l lambda_(0,j) = mu0-P00
  = -2ab*k + a^2*b^2/T^2 - gamma/b^2 + kappa
                              - (kappa/b^2)(theta+b)^2
  < -3a/(4S) < 0.                                            (1.2)

Here P=sym(N Ltri), mu_i=lambda_(i,star), and k=(S-ab)/((S+b)T).
Every weight on the left must be nonnegative. Equation (1.2) holds for every
theta, every xi>=0, every compatible skew completion, and every enclosing
m>=1536. It is not the failure of a particular chosen array.

The whole unit-quadratic polynomial separately forces theta=0 or theta=-2.
Both survive all linear flow/skew compatibility equations and both fail (1.2).
The theta=-2 case explicitly spends the loose target in mixed terms. Thus the
failure is not an artifact of retaining only the q0 certificate search.

This excludes the displayed family, not all unsaturated pi, not all tail-source
certificates, and not the hierarchy itself. No primal lower bound or full W
upper bound follows. The missing hypothesis of accepted931b9653ce0f7201 remains
unproved.

## 2. Original rows and the general full coefficient interface

Normalize smoothness to1 and the optimum to x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Let h be
any positive word of length n>=2, let l=n-1, and retain the original formal
vectors x0,g0,...,g_l,g_n. The final vector g_n is not queried in the updates:

 x_i=x0-sum_(u<i)h_u*g_u,   0<=i<=n.
 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.

All distinct ordered rows involving star, queries, and terminal are available.
In the selected architecture only incoming-terminal multipliers are set to
zero. Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290, parts(b),(c), licenses that selection for
tight certificates and gives the exact terminal-square interface. It does
NOT license deleting either orientation of any queried-prefix row.

Write

 lambda_(n,star)=2pi, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,
 alpha_i>=0, pi>=0, pi+sum_i alpha_i=1,
 lambda_(i,n)=lambda_(star,n)=0.

Initially retain BOTH optimum directions:

 mu_i=lambda_(i,star)>=0,  nu_i=lambda_(star,i)>=0.

Let e be the n-dimensional all-ones column, e_i its coordinate columns, and
Ltri the n-by-n matrix with diagonal1, entry h_j in row i,column j when j<i,
and zero above the diagonal. Write g=(g0,...,g_l)^T; expressions involving its
columns mean the corresponding sums of Euclidean vectors and inner products.
Set

 y=e*x0-Ltri*g,  zeta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
 t=Ltri*e,  t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j,  S=sum_i h_i, D=1+2S.

Direct expansion gives

 Q_ij=zeta_i-zeta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>,
 Q_(i,star)=zeta_i-<g_i,y_i>,  Q_(star,i)=-zeta_i.

Define an n-by-n real matrix N from the original prefix multipliers by

 N_ij=-lambda_(i,j) for i!=j,
 N_ii=mu_i+sum_(j!=i)lambda_(i,j).

Exact cancellation of every f_i is equivalent to

 Ne=mu,             N^T e=2alpha+nu.                       (2.1)

Indeed the coefficient of f_i before subtracting terminal flow is prefix
outflow minus prefix inflow, namely (N^T e)_i-nu_i; the terminal outgoing
row contributes -2alpha_i. There is no unexamined reversed flow convention.
After this cancellation the prefix interpolation sum is

 2sum_i alpha_i*zeta_i - sum_ij N_ij <g_i,y_j>.

Define

 r=h-Ltri^T*alpha,
 r_i=h_i*(pi+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j)-alpha_i,
 V=e^T r=S-alpha^T t.                                     (2.2)

These identities follow by expanding column i of Ltri^T alpha and using
sumalpha=1-pi. Consequently

 x_n-sum_i alpha_i*y_i = pi*x0-r^T g.

Expanding the outgoing terminal rows now gives the COMPLETE identity

 M=q||x0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij
  =||g_n-pi*x0+r^T g||^2
   +(q-pi^2)||x0||^2
   +2<x0,(pi*r-mu/2)^T g>
   +g^T [sym(N Ltri)-r r^T] g.                            (2.3)

Here sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2. No gradient equality, rank condition, or actual-history
restriction was used in obtaining (2.3). Since g_n is a free formal vector,
M is PSD exactly when the entire prefix block in (2.3) is PSD.

For completeness this gives a full coefficient recovery interface. Suppose a
proposed residual is

 (q-pi^2)||x0||^2+2<x0,z^T g>+g^T K g,

where K is symmetric. Define

 P=K+r r^T, mu=2pi*r-2z, w=Ltri^(-1)e.

For a chosen nu>=0, all coefficient matches hold exactly when a skew matrix
A satisfies

 A^T=-A,
 A e=E:=P e-Ltri^T(2alpha+nu),
 A w=F:=mu-P w,
 N=(P+A)Ltri^(-1).                                       (2.4)

One must also impose mu>=0 and N_ij<=0 for EVERY i!=j. These are genuine
additional sign conditions, not consequences of PSD or of the moments.
The complete original array is then

 lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij (distinct queries),
 lambda_(i,star)=mu_i, lambda_(star,i)=nu_i,
 lambda_(n,star)=2pi, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,
 all incoming-terminal multipliers zero.                  (2.5)

There is an explicit entry formula, so (2.4) hides no coordinate deletion:

 N_ij=P_ij+A_ij
       -h_j sum_(v>j)(P_iv+A_iv) product_(j<u<v)(1-h_u).   (2.6)

The inverse used here has diagonal1 and, for v>j, entry
-h_j product_(j<u<v)(1-h_u). Multiplication by Ltri proves it by the finite
product telescoping identity; entries above its diagonal vanish.

Because w0=1 and w1=1-h0!=1, e,w are independent. The necessary and sufficient
linear compatibility conditions for the two skew moments in (2.4) are

 e^T E=0,  w^T F=0,  e^T F=-w^T E.                        (2.7)

An explicit sufficiency construction is useful. Put

 wbar=e^T w/n, cvec=w-wbar*e,
 fvec=F-wbar*E+(E^T w/n)e.
 Acan=(E e^T-e E^T)/n
          +(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/(cvec^T cvec).        (2.8)

The conditions imply e^T fvec=cvec^T fvec=0. Multiplying (2.8) by e and w
therefore gives exactly E and F. Every compatible skew is Acan+C with
C^T=-C, Ce=Cw=0. Formula (2.6) specifies all coefficients even for such a
completion. Nonnegativity of the recovered array remains a separate problem.

## 3. Constant-gradient screen and optimum incoming rows

Restrict only the queried formal gradients to a common vector G, keeping x0
and g_n independent. From (2.1),

 sum_i mu_i=2(1-pi)+sum_i nu_i,
 e^T N Ltri e=2alpha^T t+nu^T t.

The residual after the terminal square has the exact two-vector Gram

 [ q-pi^2,  pi(V+1)-1-(sum nu)/2;
   pi(V+1)-1-(sum nu)/2, D-(V+1)^2+nu^T t ].              (3.1)

Thus the suggested Gram is correct with nu=0. Under nonnegative multipliers,
its absence of the sum-nu term forces EVERY nu_i=0. In the general cone it is
incorrect to discard those optimum rows without this assumption or an argument.

For nu=0 write Y=V+1. The exact PSD region of this 2-by-2 restriction is

 q>=1/D,  0<=pi<=sqrt(q),
 (qY-pi)^2 <= (q-pi^2)(Dq-1),                            (3.2)

intersected with pi<=1 and the actual alpha moment constraints. To verify this,
the two diagonal conditions are q-pi^2>=0 and D-Y^2>=0. The determinant is
qD-1+2pi Y-qY^2-Dpi^2. Its product by q equals
(q-pi^2)(Dq-1)-(qY-pi)^2. Necessity of q>=1/D also follows by evaluating this
Gram on (D,1): the value is D(qD-1)-(Dpi-Y)^2. Conversely, for q>=1/D and
q-pi^2>0, the determinant condition forces the lower diagonal to be
nonnegative. If q-pi^2=0, (3.2) gives Y=1/pi and the lower diagonal is
D-1/q>=0. This proves the boundary cases as well as the interior.

For the fixed hierarchy below t0=1 and t_l=T=S-a. As alpha ranges over
nonnegative columns of mass1-pi, its moment range gives

 b+pi*T <= Y <= S+pi,  b=1+a.                            (3.3)

This is a necessary affine screen only. It is not a full-space certificate.

At q0=1/D the vanishing of nu is actually forced, rather than optional.
Use the legal scalar Huber function f(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=1 and
f(x)=|x|-1/2 otherwise, starting at x0=D. All queried points and the terminal
lie in the positive affine branch; all their gradients equal1. Every finite
ordered row and every query/terminal-to-optimum row is zero. The remaining
rows are Q_(star,i)=-(D-t_i)<0. The objective gap q0*x0^2-2f_n is zero.
Consequently M=-sum_i nu_i(D-t_i). PSD and nu>=0 force every nu_i=0.
Also the terminal square vanishes on this tight trajectory, so

 Y=D*pi, pi^2<=1/D.                                      (3.4)

The same conclusion follows directly from (3.2). Equations (3.3),(3.4) imply
pi>=b/(S+b), and also pi<=1/2. Thus concentrating alpha at the latest query
selects the smallest possible pi in this q0 affine screen.

For q>q0, no general vanishing claim for nu is made. The same Huber trajectory
only implies the necessary budget

 sum_i nu_i(D-t_i) <= D^2(q-q0).

## 4. Frozen tail source and the genuine unsaturated freedom

Fix the specified enclosing m>=1536, epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, and

 H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), h=H_m,
 n=2^m-1, l=n-1, S=sum h, D=1+2S,
 b=1+a, T=S-a, Ctot=S+b, Ctot+T=D.

Every child uses this SAME enclosing m. Put

 alpha=(1-pi)e_l, pi=b/Ctot, q0=1/D,
 delta=q0-pi^2=S(S-2ab)/(D*Ctot^2)>0.                     (4.1)

Then r_i=pi*h_i for i<l, r_l=a+pi, and V=a+pi*T=D*pi-1.
All coefficients in (4.1) are fixed; there is no alternative alpha search.

It is useful to state the COMPLETE q0 flow constraints, beyond the affine
screen. Write the residual prefix matrix as [delta,z^T;z,K]. Its constant
restriction vanishes at (x0,g)=(D,e). PSD of the full residual then forces
z^T e=-delta D and K e=-D z: a zero quadratic value for a PSD form forces its
bilinear product with every direction to vanish, as follows by varying a real
scalar in that direction. Equivalently every possible PSD residual at q0 has

 z=-delta*u, e^T u=D,
 K=delta*u*u^T+K0, K0>=0, K0 e=0.                        (4.2)

The Schur complement proves K0>=0; the displayed moments prove K0 e=0.
Conversely (4.2) makes the residual PSD with exactly that affine null direction.
Its remaining complete flow interface is

 mu=2pi*r+2delta*u>=0, nu=0,
 P=r r^T+delta*u*u^T+K0,
 A e=P e-2Ltri^T alpha,
 A w=mu-P w,
 N=(P+A)Ltri^(-1), N_ij<=0 for all i!=j.                 (4.3)

Compatibility (2.7), and every entry formula (2.6), remain required.
There is no imposed support restriction on finite multipliers in (4.3).

Under saturation q=pi^2, the residual x0 diagonal is zero. Full PSD then
forces its entire x0 mixed column to vanish, fixing mu=2pi*r. In contrast,
(4.2),(4.3) permit the additional vector 2delta*u in mu, with e^T u=D,
and a full PSD metric K0 on the e-orthogonal gradient directions. This is
real coefficient freedom. It neither follows from nor is excluded by the
saturated common-column report ba770750b559361d, read in full for this round.
That report's original Q orientation agrees with (2.1)-(2.3); its special
metric and common-column constraints have not been imported as necessities.

## 5. ONE explicit paid rank-two family

Define the physical column v by v_i=h_i for i<l and v_l=1, so e^T v=T. Freeze

 u=(D/T)v, r=pi*v+a*e_l,
 s=beta^(2^m-1)*c^(-m), gamma=a^2/T^2-s^2,
 q=17/(16D), kappa=q-q0=1/(16D),
 dsrc=(e0-e1)/b.

The last three physical steps are (b,gbar,b), where gbar=1+beta. On their
ORIGINAL indices define

 dtail=a*gbar*e_(l-2)+(1-a*beta)e_(l-1)-b*e_l.             (5.1)

Both columns have zero sum. The only family in this mechanism is (1.1), for
all theta real and xi>=0. In the requested paid-block coordinates this means

 ell=kappa*theta*dsrc,
 R=kappa*theta^2*dsrc*dsrc^T+xi*dtail*dtail^T.              (5.2)

Thus sumell=0, Re=0, and

 [kappa,ell^T;ell,R]
 =kappa*(1,theta*dsrc)^T*(1,theta*dsrc)
                   +xi*(0,dtail)^T*(0,dtail)

is literally PSD of rank at most two. No feasible but unspecified matrix R is
used. In particular theta=-2 uses a nonzero mixed block. The tail column is
included from the outset to allow last-query diagonal repair without changing
the source coordinates; it is not a second post-failure family.

All baseline signs can be proved at the stipulated threshold. The inequalities
sqrt(2)<10/7 and epsilon<=1/6144 give

 a>(7/17)(6143/6145)>2/5, while a<1/2; hence 1<b<3/2.

Every h_i>1, so S>2^m-1>64. Formula (4.1) proves delta>0.
To prove gamma>0, set vword=a/(c-2). Since c>1+sqrt(2) and a<1/c,
0<vword<1. Summing the two children, or substituting in their defining
recursion, gives

 1+S_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j.

Since c>2 and j>0 this is less c^j, hence T<S<c^m.
Also n>=8m for m>=6: it holds at6 and the inequality is preserved when m
increases by1. Put x=1/(4m). Since (1-x)^(-1)>1+x and the binomial expansion
gives (1+x)^(4m)>2, one has beta^(4m)<1/2 and beta^n<1/4. Therefore
s*T<beta^n<1/4<a, proving gamma>0. No recurrence or numerical calculation was
executed; these are symbolic finite inequalities.

Consequently (1.1) is a PSD polynomial for every theta and xi>=0. Whether
it equals a nonnegative combination of the required original rows is the
separate issue answered negatively below.

## 6. Full constant and unit-quadratic polynomial checks

For a common queried gradient G, e^T u=D and both difference columns vanish.
The complete trial polynomial restricts to

 ||g_n-pi*x0+V*G||^2+delta||x0-D*G||^2+kappa||x0||^2.     (6.1)

This is exactly the nu=0 affine Gram (3.1) at q=q0+kappa, with the specified
pi and V. Its terminal square still contains the original free g_n.
On the actual Huber trajectory x0=D*G,g_n=G, (6.1) is kappa D^2||G||^2,
which matches the original objective polynomial. No child is restarted.

The optimum incoming restriction is also forced within THIS paid family,
even though q>q0. Coefficient matching gives

 z=-delta*u+kappa*theta*dsrc,
 mu=2pi*r+2delta*u-2kappa*theta*dsrc.                      (6.2)

Its sum is 2pi*(Dpi-1)+2delta D=2(1-pi). The sum of (2.1) therefore gives
sum nu=0; nonnegativity forces nu=0. This is a family-specific consequence,
not a deletion from the unrestricted loose-target cone.

For the full unit-curvature quadratic f(x)=||x||^2/2, let
w_i=product_(j<i)(1-h_j). Then Ltri*w=e and g_i=w_i*x0. The hierarchy has
signed full product w_n=-s. To see its magnitude, note that the level at i
is the exponent of2 dividing i+1. Its total across i=0,...,2^m-2 is
sum_(j=1)^(m-1)(2^(m-j)-1)=2^m-m-1=n-m. Multiplying the factors
a*c^level=beta*c^(level-1) gives beta^n*c^(-m)=s.

At the original endpoints,

 w0=1, w1=-a,
 w_l=s/a, w_(l-1)=-s/(a*beta), w_(l-2)=s/(a^2*beta).

These follow from the literal last factors -a,-beta,-a. They prove

 dsrc^T w=1, dtail^T w=0,
 u^T w=D/T, r^T w=pi+s.                                 (6.3)

For u use sum_(i<l)h_i*w_i=1-w_l; for r use r=pi*v+a*e_l.
The terminal square in (1.1) consequently vanishes on the ENTIRE actual
unit-quadratic trajectory. Every original Q_ij, including optimum and terminal
rows, is zero on that trajectory. A certificate identity must therefore have
value (q-s^2)||x0||^2.

The baseline has exactly the correct q0 value, because

 delta*(1-D/T)^2+gamma=q0-s^2.                            (6.4)

Indeed 1-D/T=-Ctot/T and
Ctot^2-D*b^2+D*a^2=T^2; multiplying (6.4) by D*T^2 proves it.
The paid tail term vanishes, and the paid source square contributes
kappa*(1+theta)^2||x0||^2. Thus full polynomial matching forces

 theta*(theta+2)=0, namely theta=0 or theta=-2.            (6.5)

This is a necessary identity of the original polynomial, not a test of only
an aggregate moment. It does not decide any individual multiplier sign.

## 7. Complete linear flow constraints for the literal family

For (1.1), write

 Gamma=gamma+kappa*theta^2,
 P=r r^T+delta*u*u^T+Gamma*dsrc*dsrc^T+xi*dtail*dtail^T,
 mu=2pi*r+2delta*u-2kappa*theta*dsrc, nu=0.                (7.1)

The actual coefficient equations are exactly (2.4)-(2.7) with these P,mu and
the specified alpha. These formulas, followed by (2.6),(2.5), recover every
original coefficient for any compatible skew; there are no omitted rows.

One can check ALL scalar compatibility conditions, rather than assume them.
First

 e^T E=V^2+delta D^2-2(1-pi)T=0,

because V=Dpi-1, delta=1/D-pi^2 and (1-pi)T=S-V. Next (6.3),(6.4) give

 w^T F=pi^2-s^2+delta*(2D/T-D^2/T^2)-gamma
                                  -kappa*(theta^2+2theta)
       =-kappa*theta*(theta+2).

Finally P is symmetric and Ltri*w=e, so

 e^T F+w^T E=e^T mu-2alpha^T Ltri*w=0.

Thus for BOTH allowed values theta=0,-2, every compatibility equation holds
for every xi>=0. Formula (2.8) provides an actual real skew completion;
adding an arbitrary neutral skew does not alter the cuts below. This proves
that the scoped failure is specifically nonnegative original multipliers,
not a silently inconsistent choice of skew moments.

For explicit optimum-row signs set

 k=(S-ab)/(Ctot*T)=(1-pi*b)/T>0.

Since r=pi*v+a*e_l and u=(D/T)v, (7.1) simplifies to

 mu=2k*v+2pi*a*e_l-2kappa*theta*dsrc.                    (7.2)

The equality uses pi^2*T/D+delta=(1-pi*b)/D. At theta=0 all mu entries are
positive. At theta=-2 only source0 and source1 change:

 mu0=2kb+4kappa/b, mu1=2k*gbar-4kappa/b,
 mu_i=2k*h_i for 1<i<l, mu_l=2k+2pi*a.

One has k>1/(2S): Ctot*T<S(S+3/2), S-ab>S-3/4, and S>3 suffice. Hence
mu1>1/S-1/(8S)>0, because 4kappa/b=1/(4Db)<1/(8S).
All optimum outgoing signs pass at both unit-compatible theta values.
The terminal outgoing signs also pass: 0<pi<1 and alpha=(1-pi)e_l.
The forced optimum incoming coefficients are zero, and all incoming terminal
coefficients remain zero as selected at the start.

## 8. Original tail tests, including the repair actually afforded by xi

The last column of Ltri is e_l. Consequently the exact coefficient identity
P=sym(N Ltri) gives

 Pll=Nll.

The original last column and row capacities therefore require

 Pll-2(1-pi)=sum_(i<l)lambda_(i,l)>=0,
 Pll-mu_l=sum_(j<l)lambda_(l,j)>=0.                       (8.1)

The source paid square has no support at the last three indices, so

 Pll=Pll0+xi*b^2,
 Pll0=(a+pi)^2+delta*D^2/T^2.                            (8.2)

Define J=pi^2+delta*D^2/T^2. There is the exact identity

 J=2k-a^2/T^2.                                          (8.3)

For verification, put everything over Ctot^2*T^2. The numerator of J-2k is
b^2*T^2+D*S(S-2ab)-2Ctot*T(S-ab)=-a^2*Ctot^2.
Using (8.3) in (8.2) gives

 Pll0-mu_l=a^2*(1-T^(-2))>0.

Thus the last row capacity passes already, whereas the baseline last COLUMN
capacity fails. In detail, pi<b/S<3/(2S)<1/32 and delta<1/D give

 Pll0<(17/32)^2+D/T^2<1/2.

For the last inequality, D/T^2<3/S: after replacing a by1/2 it follows from
S^2-4S+3/4>0, valid at S>64. Also 3/S<3/64, and
(17/32)^2+3/64=337/1024<1/2. On the other hand 2(1-pi)>31/16.
Therefore the exact positive tail price is

 xi >= [2(1-pi)-Pll0]/b^2.                               (8.4)

The family explicitly permits such xi; this elementary tail mismatch has
been repaired within the chosen family, not ignored or confused with an
obstruction to every unsaturated certificate.

The next original tail pair also has a complete orientation check. Put
p=l-1. Since h_p=gbar,

 N_(p,l)+N_(l,p)=2P_(p,l)-gbar*Pll
   =gbar*[2k-a^2*(1+T^(-2))]
                  -xi*b*[2(1-a*beta)+gbar*b].            (8.5)

The left side must be nonpositive. Its right side is strictly negative for
all xi>=0: k<1/T<2/S, so 2k<4/S<1/16<a^2, and 1-a*beta>0. Hence this
last-pair necessary condition passes. It does not assert the individual signs.
These tests retain both orientations of each original row and the actual
last-three products; no terminal node has been substituted for a query.

## 9. Decisive original source cut for the ENTIRE family

The first physical step is h0=b=1+a. Column0 of Ltri has entry1 in row0 and
entry b in every later row. Since P=sym(N Ltri), its first diagonal obeys

 P00=N00+b*sum_(j>0)N0j
     =mu0+a*sum_(j>0)N0j.

Thus any nonnegative original multiplier array must satisfy the nonnegative
cut

 mu0-P00=a*sum_(j=1)^l lambda_(0,j)>=0.                   (9.1)

Every original queried destination is retained. There is no assumption about
support or choice of the completing skew, and mu0 is the ACTUAL outgoing
optimum multiplier fixed by the x0 mixed coefficients.

In (7.1), dtail0=0, r0=pi*b, u0=Db/T, and dsrc0=1/b. Therefore

 mu0=2kb-2kappa*theta/b,
 P00=b^2*J+(gamma+kappa*theta^2)/b^2.

Using (8.3) and completing the theta square gives exactly

 mu0-P00
 =-2ab*k+a^2*b^2/T^2-gamma/b^2
                   -kappa*(theta^2+2btheta)/b^2
 =-2ab*k+a^2*b^2/T^2-gamma/b^2+kappa
                   -(kappa/b^2)(theta+b)^2.              (9.2)

It is independent of xi. It is also independent of ALL remaining neutral
skew freedom. The paid x0 block can improve this source deficit by at most
kappa, regardless of theta.

Here is an explicit bound uniform at the stipulated enclosing threshold.
We proved k>1/(2S), b>1, gamma>0, S>64, 2/5<a<1/2. Also T>S/2. Hence

 -2ab*k < -a/S,
 a^2*b^2/T^2 < a*9/(2S^2) < a/(8S),
 kappa=1/(16D)<1/(32S)<a/(8S).

The second comparison uses S>36; the third uses a>1/4. Dropping only the two
nonpositive square terms in (9.2) gives

 mu0-P00 < -3a/(4S) <0.                                 (9.3)

Equations (9.1),(9.3) contradict one another for EVERY theta real and EVERY
xi>=0. In particular both theta values that pass the whole unit-quadratic
identity are excluded, even after paying (8.4) and even though all their
linear compatibility equations and optimum signs pass.

This is an exact nonnegative cut against the complete explicitly chosen
rank-two paid family. No enumeration, optimizer, numerical witness, or
particular-skew failure was used. There is no need to guess and test additional
arrays after this cut. No third family has been opened.

## 10. What remains and the status of the original problem

The reusable candidate interfaces are (2.1)-(2.8), the nu-corrected affine
Gram (3.1), its exact nu=0 region (3.2), and the q0 unsaturated form (4.2)-(4.3).
They are part of this JOINT UNVERIFIED draft, not accepted building blocks.
They can guide a future authorized verification or a new architecture.

The failure identifies a precise local deficiency. For this u, the baseline
source coefficient P00 exceeds the available mu0 by order1/S. The loose
payment kappa is too small to fix it with the frozen dsrc column. Arbitrarily
large PSD energy on the chosen last-three column does not change P00 or mu0.
A future different mixed column or different u could change this cut. This
report does not assert that either repair succeeds, and does not attempt one.

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is used at its exact original-row/terminal-reduction
scope. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the benchmark envelope and CONDITIONAL
implication from a full W<=17/(16D) bound. No such bound has been obtained here.
Accepted1e0bf3f41457d8e4 was acknowledged as the closed previous gate and is not
used as an unstated all-depth premise. Original PROBLEM.md is unchanged.

Retrieval and audit: TASK and latest master_guidance52e9d99015702be8 were read;
own local memory and relevant shared findings/facts were recalled using the
query-memory procedure. The complete shared coefficient report
ba770750b559361d was copied as textual context and read in full. The
initial broad retrieval output was truncated, so the report and the relevant
accepted statements/proof sections were read separately before reasoning.
Direct-proving and construct-counterexamples procedures were used to derive
and screen this single joint interface/family. One Matlas query returned
unrelated robust-mean/GNE/PL results, none imported. A public arXiv search
located the standard interpolation/PEP context, Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur,
https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.05666; no theorem from that paper is used as a proof
premise. All new identities and the scoped cut are derived in this report.

Resource accounting: substantive mechanisms2; mathematical programs0;
numerical/arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replays0; solvers/optimizers0;
benchmark DP0; formal calls/fact_submit0; installations0; consults0;
subagents0; new fact IDs none. Text parsing, copying, writing, and hashes are

revocation, shutdown, or Goal completion occurred. Return at cap1 without

END OF JOINT UNVERIFIED REPORT.
