# Saved full-dual structure and two paid optimum-profile reallocations

**JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical draft and numerical discovery.** Worker max,
ONE allocated max78 invocation (baseline77, ceiling78/attempted79). No
controller completion count is inferred. The original exact-profile problem
remains OPEN. No formal call, fact submission, new accepted fact, or full-class
upper bound is supplied. Two new explicit coefficient laws are examined.

The saved duals change the optimum profile, retain substantial incoming
terminal mass, and act positively on the tested finite-depth zero-displacement
spaces. They remain numerically defective. The two analytic laws below pay for
original optimum-row decrements and change that profile while preserving the
whole Huber contact equations exactly. The first fails on the actual first
original gradient. The second repairs that diagonal but cannot offset the
inherited all-depth negative action on a two-gradient zero-displacement wave.
These are scoped failures of the specified laws, not infeasibility of a full
PEP, a bound on W, or a resolution of the original minimax question.

## 1. Original problem and accepted inputs

For EVERY integer n>=0, EVERY physical word h in [0,infinity)^n, and L>0,
W_n(h) is the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over
all finite dimensions, convex differentiable L-smooth functions with an
attained minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, with the actual updates
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). In particular W=2R. The original benchmark is

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Every split k is an integer. The original target asks whether
lim_n B_n inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h)=1. Its equivalent lower-bound
quantifier is: for every positive eta there is a word-independent N_eta
such that EVERY actual n>=N_eta and EVERY nonnegative word h of length n
satisfies B_n W_n(h)>=1-eta. Neither the present diagnostic hierarchy nor
these two candidate laws restrict those target quantifiers.

The accepted inputs are fact1252b8823bb5a290 (full original interpolation
and certificate convention), fact04219554e41d9750 Sections10--11 (full
primitive/objective conventions and its unconditional benchmark), and ONLY
the scalar benchmark consequence in fact5fd396eba75651e7 Section10:

    B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250, m>=1536, tau=1/4.       (1.1)

The full interpolation fact and these exact accepted scopes were reread.
No seed hypothesis or restricted-family optimum is imported. A true full
certificate at q=21/[20(1+2S_m)] would imply a fixed benchmark gap
B_n W_n(h)<4977/5000. No such certificate is obtained here.

TASK.md, guidance f4f9450aef9e7697, and latest observed guidance
9a86c3435878b039 govern the same bounded allocation. Full shared reports
ad24079f80cd6a3a and 9ceef38b9c3f0498 were read as awareness. The latter is
NOT a correctness premise: all base coefficients and all mathematical
identities needed for the new argument are derived together below.

## 2. Bounded saved-array extraction: what actually changed

The frozen public inputs are ONLY calibration h=(3/2), H4(n15), H5(n31),
and H6(n63), each at its OWN enclosing m and tau=1/4. INPUT_MANIFEST.json
and EXECUTION_FREEZE.json preserve paths, bytes and SHA256 hashes. The raw
files are not changed. The source is the public small_tau_full_pep_20261001
index, not another worker's private memory. No H3 job, new word, trajectory,
hierarchy recurrence, benchmark DP, solver or optimizer was executed.

Every child used the documented public run_shared_math_python.sh launcher,
PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1, and single-thread numerical environment variables.
Each reserved30 accounted seconds and one job BEFORE importing NumPy, loading
raw arrays, doing algebra, diagnostics, or exporting. An EXTERNAL30-second
child timeout covered the entire child. The wrapper performed only
administrative file I/O, reservations and timing.

All original ordered rows were independently expanded from the SAVED
position coefficient vectors. With q in original units and original pair
weights lambda, the reconstructed matrix was q E00+sum lambda A_ij.
This matched the saved original matrix entrywise in the reported binary64
calculation. Every original incoming/outgoing terminal and optimum row was
retained. No eigenvalue clipping, matrix repair, rank truncation, support
threshold deletion or numerical-to-exact promotion occurred.

Calibration used the exact identity

    ||x0||^2/4-2f1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)
       =||x0/2-g0-g1||^2.

The exact row map, orientation, known flow2e1, original units and raw flow
checks passed on job1. Job1 nevertheless exited3 because an extra
entrywise-nearness requirement1e-5 failed: the saved approximate matrix
is4.70287706799688e-5 away from that exact square. The ONE correction slot
removed that unnecessary raw-certificate-matching requirement, retained its
measured discrepancy, and added original C/G eigenvalue defect checks at the
original report's1e-7 tolerance. The exact map/flow/scaling checks were
unchanged. The correction passed; this is a model calibration, not exact
feasibility. Initial code, freeze, failed outputs and CORRECTION.md are
preserved. No retry beyond that sole correction took place.

Exactly THREE feature families were preregistered and fitted in the ONE

and physical-prefix/step/level features of the optimum profile. No feature

## 3. Complete original-space algebra

Normalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. All n+2 vectors
(x_0,g_0,...,g_n) remain independent formal variables, including the
unqueried terminal gradient. Put

    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, S=sum h_t, D=1+2S, h_n=0,
    y_i=x_i-g_i,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Let K_ij, i!=j finite, be all finite-row multipliers, nu_i the
Q_(i,star) weights, and xi_i the Q_(star,i) weights. Diagonals are zero.
Value cancellation is exactly

    sum_j K_ij-sum_j K_ji+nu_i-xi_i=2*1_(i=n).         (3.1)

After this cancellation the full polynomial is

    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-sum_i nu_i<x_0,g_i>+C(g),
    C(g)=sum_(i,j) C_ij<g_i,g_j>.                     (3.2)

Define out_i=nu_i+sum_j K_ij. Every entry of C is

    C_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv,
    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv
                       +h_i[nu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (3.3)

Indeed half the incident row mass is out_i-1_(i=n), by(3.1).
Only a forward row sourced at i contributes -h_i K_iv to its diagonal.
For i<j the difference squares give the first term, rows out of i landing
after j give the second, and rows out of j landing at/before i or at the
optimum give the third. This derives the entire matrix, not a restriction.
For q>0 the exact initial Schur expenditure is nu nu^T/(4q).

In the analytic laws xi=0 and sum nu=2. Set e=(1,...,1).
Full flow then gives e^T C e=D. On the whole Huber contact tuple
(x_0,g)=(D,e), all finite and outgoing-optimum Q rows vanish, and

    M(q;D,e)=(qD-1)D.                                (3.4)

There is no incoming-optimum Huber price in either proposed law. Their
stronger derivative contact identity Ce=D nu/2 will be proved, not assumed.
The initial/common block is therefore [[q,-1],[-1,D]]. At21/(20D)
its determinant is1/20. This block alone does not establish full PSD.

## 4. The inherited base, rederived within this joint draft

Fix an integer m>=1536 and the SAME enclosing parameters at EVERY depth:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, D_j=2A_j-1,
    d_j=a*c^j, t_j=d_j/A_j, tstar=1/lambda.

The final h=H_m has n=n_m and D=D_m. These are mathematical definitions;
none of these recurrences was executed in this invocation. The finite
sum formula for masses is

    v_h=a/(c-2), A_j=v_h*c^j+(1-v_h)*2^j,
    t_j=(c-2)/[1+(1/v_h-1)(2/c)^j].                   (4.1)

It follows from c(c-2)>1>beta that0<v_h<1. Thus A_j>=2^j,
t_j increases strictly, and t_j<c-2<1/2. With b=h_0=1+a,

    b=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],
    A_1=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)], d_1=1-epsilon,
    A_2=lambda^2-epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))],
    d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2),
    lambda*d_2-A_2
      =epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda^2 epsilon]>0.

Also lambda*d_1-A_1<0. Consequently

    7/5<b<sqrt(2), A_1>12/5, A_2>5,
    t_0,t_1<tstar<t_2,
    0<t_j-tstar<lambda*epsilon for j>=2.              (4.2)

For the rational signs use epsilon<=1/6144, lambda^2<6 and
lambda>241/100; the displayed subtractions are smaller than epsilon.
The exact relations A_(j+1)=(2+t_j)A_j and
t_(j+1)=c*t_j/(2+t_j) also follow directly from the word.

Here is EVERY primitive finite coefficient. Start Lambda^0=0. In a
join of two depth-j children let p=n_j, z=n_(j+1), and let i run over
the nonterminal right queries. Embed the left Lambda^j with weight1
and the right with weight k_j. Add

    i->p: u_j h_i, i->z: v_j h_i, z->p: w_j, p->z: z_j,
    u_j=2lambda,
    v_j=[2lambda d_j+2(A_j+d_j)]/A_j,
    k_j=2+lambda+(lambda+1)t_j,
    w_j=4A_j+2d_j-2lambda(A_j-1), z_j=2A_j.           (4.3)

Equivalently each original coefficient is the sum of its indicated local
contributions over every dyadic node, multiplied by k_l for each ancestor
whose right child contains that node; left ancestor weights are1. This
finite sum/product is an explicit law, not an optimization or executed
recurrence. For w_j write2lambda+2(d_j-A_j/lambda);(4.2) proves its
positivity for j>=2, while w_0=4+2a and w_1>2lambda-2. All other signs
are positive from their formulas. The identities

    u_j+v_j=2(k_j-1),
    u_j(A_j-1)+w_j-z_j=2(A_j+d_j)

give divergence -2h_i at a nonterminal and2S_j at the terminal.
Define the value-cancelled primitive polynomial

    V_j=2sum_(i<n_j)h_i(f_i-f_(n_j))
      +sum_(i<n_j)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
      -A_j(A_j-1)||g_(n_j)||^2,
    Psrc_j=V_j+sum Lambda^j_iv Q_iv.

For r_R=sum_(right nonterminal i)h_i g_i, put
Y=r_R-(A_j-1)g_z and Z=g_p-g_z. Expanding the four row types in(4.3)
before their weights gives the following gradient expressions:

    d_j<r_R,g_p>+||r_R||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2
                                    +(A_j-1)||g_p||^2/2;
    -||r_R||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2
                    +(A_j-1)||g_z||^2/2-<r_R,g_z>;
    d_j<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r_R>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;
    -(d_j+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2-<g_p,r_R>-<g_p,g_z>.

Collecting these terms together with V and the two children yields

    Psrc_(j+1)=Psrc_j^left+k_j Psrc_j^right
      +(tstar-t_j)[(lambda+1)||Y||^2-2lambda A_j<Y,Z>
                        +A_j^2(2+tstar+t_j)||Z||^2]. (4.4)

In particular the pre-collection coefficients of r_R^2,r_R*g_p,r_R*g_z
are (u_j-v_j)/2,u_j*d_j-z_j,w_j-v_j, verifying the mixed-term signs.
The bracket has positive first diagonal and determinant divided by A_j^2
at least lambda>0. Positive epsilon therefore DOES NOT make these
primitive children PSD. That is never assumed below.

The original objective base is Kbase^0=0, mu^0_0=2, xi=0. At a join
set A=S_j, Bjoin=1+d_j, Dp=D_(j+1), Dc=D_j, and
s_j=(A+Bjoin)/(3A+2Bjoin), theta_j=1-s_j. With p=n_j, L_l=l and
R_i=n_j+1+i, define

    Kbase_(L_l,L_v)=Lambda^j_lv/Dp,
    Kbase_(R_i,R_v)=Kbase^j_iv,
    Kbase_(R_i,p)=s_j mu^j_i,
    mu_(L_l)=2h_l/Dp for l<n_j,
    mu_p=2s_j-2A/Dp,
    mu_(R_i)=theta_j mu^j_i.                         (4.5)

All other entries are zero; coincident contributions are summed. For a
fully unrolled description the right-spine depth-l node starts at
2^r-2^l. Its left primitive block is Lambda^(l-1)/D_l; each right query
feeds its bridge with s_(l-1) times that right child's explicitly defined
mu. A query's optimum weight is2h_i/D_l if it is in that node's left
nonterminal block, or2s_(l-1)-2S_(l-1)/D_l if it is its bridge, multiplied
by product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t. The final terminal weight is
2product_(t=0)^(r-1)theta_t. Each query has exactly one such category.
Together with(4.3), these are finite original-index formulas for all rows.

The bridge sign follows because
(A+Bjoin)Dp-A(3A+2Bjoin)=A^2+4A*Bjoin+2Bjoin^2+A+Bjoin>0.
Also0<s_j<1 and theta_j>0. The primitive divergence cancels its left
optimum rows; the bridge divergence is2s_j, canceled by the incoming
right weights of total2s_j. Every right query's old flow is unchanged
since s_j+theta_j=1. Thus full flow(3.1) holds and sum mu=2.
All mu_i are strictly positive. The base has no incoming terminal rows.

Write T_R=sum_i mu^j_i g_(R_i),
x_p=x_0-sum_(l<n_j)h_l g_(L_l),
Rchild=Cbase^j(g_R)-Dc||T_R||^2/4,
and kappa_j=Dc-Dp theta_j^2. All original rows(4.5) give the identity

    Mbase_(j+1)(1/Dp)=Psrc_j(g_L)/Dp+Rchild
     +||x_p+(A-Dp s_j)g_p-(Dp theta_j/2)T_R||^2/Dp
     +(kappa_j/4)||T_R-2g_p||^2.                    (4.6)

For an independent coefficient audit the remaining polynomial after
subtracting Psrc/Dp and Rchild is

    ||x_p||^2/Dp+2(A/Dp-s_j)<x_p,g_p>
    +(A^2/Dp+2s_j)||g_p||^2-theta_j<x_p,T_R>
    +(Bjoin-s_j)<g_p,T_R>+Dc||T_R||^2/4.

Expanding the two squares in(4.6) agrees term by term because
s_j(3A+2Bjoin)=A+Bjoin. No child PSD or sign of kappa is needed for
this identity. At x_0=Dp and all gradients1, x_p=Dp-A and T_R=2;
every square argument is zero. The bracket in(4.4) has Y=Z=0.
Induction from ||x_0-g_0||^2 shows zero value AND zero derivative:

    Cbase^j e=D_j mu^j/2.                           (4.7)

Two further entries used below follow without any numerical computation:

    mu_0=2b/D,    Cbase_00=2/D.                     (4.8)

Here Cbase_00 denotes its first GRADIENT diagonal. In fact only the
first depth1 primitive bracket contributes when g_0=1 and every other
gradient is0; it contributes2-b^2. At the root objective join x_p=-b,
g_p=T_R=0. The x_p square contributes b^2/D, so(4.8) follows.

## 5. Exact paid two-row transport and the two coefficient laws

Write w78_i=2S-P_i, so S<=w78_i<=2S and every w78_i>0. Define the
fixed set E78 of dyadic matched pairs as follows. For each depth
1<=l<=m, each offset o=k*2^l with0<=k<2^(m-l), and
0<=r<2^(l-1), take

    a78=o+r, b78=o+r+2^(l-1).

These are exactly bit-flip edges of the m-dimensional binary cube on
all2^m original gradient indices0,...,n, oriented toward the larger
index. Both child terminal indices participate. Every source belongs
to at most m pairs, and index0 belongs to exactly m. Distinct pairs
are counted once. This is a sparse, explicit dyadic law; it is not a
separable product kernel or an inferred numerical support.

For one pair a78<b78 abbreviate a0=a78, b0=b78 in THIS paragraph only,
ell=P_b0-P_a0>0, wa=w78_a0, wb=w78_b0, so wa-wb=ell.
For a prescribed transfer delta>=0 set

    Tforward=delta*wa/ell, Tbackward=delta*wb/ell.     (5.1)

Add Tforward to K_(a0,b0) and Tbackward to K_(b0,a0), DECREASE the
original Q_(a0,star) multiplier by delta, and INCREASE the original
Q_(b0,star) multiplier by delta. The net value flow is zero at every
node because Tforward-Tbackward=delta. This is a paid change of the
optimum profile, not an additive balanced finite circulation.

The exact complete polynomial increment is

    Delta_ab=Tforward*(Q_ab+Q_(b,star)-Q_(a,star))
             +Tbackward*(Q_ba+Q_(a,star)-Q_(b,star))
       =<g_a-g_b,Tforward*y_b-Tbackward*y_a>.        (5.2)

To check the basic identity, expand the three Q rows. Their function
values cancel. The position terms equal<g_a-g_b,x_b>, and the
difference-square and optimum-square terms sum to
||g_b||^2-<g_a,g_b>. Hence Q_ab+Q_(b,star)-Q_(a,star)
=<g_a-g_b,y_b>. This proves(5.2) without an external theorem.

For BOTH laws put vartheta78=epsilon/m. Use the original base mu from
(4.5), without funding a debit from newly received mass. The ONLY TWO
choices of transfer are

    Law I:  delta_ab=vartheta78*mu_a;
    Law II: delta_ab=vartheta78*mu_a*(P_b-P_a)/w78_a. (5.3)

No additional law or tuned parameter search is part of this invocation.
Law II is the prescribed physical-distance attenuation of Law I.
For every original i,j the final coefficients are explicitly

    K78_ij=Kbase_ij
      +sum_((a,b) in E78)[1_(i=a,j=b)Tforward_ab
                        +1_(i=b,j=a)Tbackward_ab],
    nu78_i=mu_i-sum_((i,b) in E78)delta_ib
                  +sum_((a,i) in E78)delta_ai,
    xi78_i=0, K78_ii=0.                             (5.4)

Every decrement is paid: in either law delta_ab<=vartheta78 mu_a,
so the total outgoing debit at node a is at most epsilon*mu_a.
Thus nu78_i>=(1-epsilon)mu_i>0, and every finite coefficient is
nonnegative. Incoming optimum multipliers are explicitly zero as a
choice of these laws, not a deletion theorem. The total gross optimum
mass moved over the ENTIRE word is at most2epsilon, since sum mu=2.
Outgoing optimum mass remains exactly2. Law II adds at most4epsilon
finite multiplier mass because Tforward=vartheta78 mu_a and
0<Tbackward<=Tforward. Law I's exact finite added mass is
sum_(E78)delta_ab(wa+wb)/ell; it is not silently assumed small.
All added incoming and outgoing terminal rows remain part of(5.4).

## 6. Whole-word contact, initial/common and terminal charges

At the original Huber contact tuple x_0=D, g_i=1,

    y_i=D-P_i-1=w78_i.

Both vector factors on the right side of(5.2) vanish:
g_a-g_b=0 and Tforward*wb-Tbackward*wa=0. Consequently each increment
has zero value AND zero full derivative there, including its initial
coordinate. Combining this with(4.7) proves exactly

    C78 e=D nu78/2.                                 (6.1)

The changes in nu are therefore accompanied by changes in the entire
contact row-sum vector; the old optimum profile is not frozen. There
is ZERO incoming-optimum Huber cost, ZERO contact mismatch, and ZERO
per-node or per-level scalar Huber allowance. The one whole-word
initial/common block and its budget are precisely(3.4). At q=1/D
its kernel is(D,1); at q=21/(20D) it has determinant1/20. Neither
statement certifies the transverse space.

For completeness, let v be any gradient column with e^T v=0, and
write u=nu78^T v. Its coupling to the initial/common coordinates is
(-u/2,Du/2). At q=zeta/D, zeta>1, inversion of the whole block
[[zeta/D,-1],[-1,D]] gives the exact Schur expenditure D*u^2/4:
the numerator is D*u^2*(zeta-1)/4 and its determinant is zeta-1.
Thus the required residual is the COMPLETE matrix

    R78=C78-D*nu78*nu78^T/4,  R78 e=0.               (6.2)

Indeed the entire polynomial has the identity

    M78(q)=||x_0-(D/2)sum_i nu78_i g_i||^2/D
       +sum_(i,j)(R78)_ij<g_i,g_j>
       +(q-1/D)||x_0||^2.                           (6.3)

Conversely any negative R78 direction can have its common component
shifted to make sum nu78_i g_i=0, without changing R78; setting x_0=0
then gives a negative value at EVERY q. This explains why merely
raising q cannot repair a fixed exactly contacting array. It does
not require arbitrary alternative arrays to preserve exact contact.

There is also no omitted terminal cost. The pairs entering n have
sources a=n-2^l,0<=l<m. Let Tin be the sum of their Tforward values.
The base terminal diagonal is1 and has no incoming rows. Full flow(3.1)
therefore makes the actual new terminal gradient diagonal

    gamma78=1+Tin>0.

Writing C78 for(3.3) with the explicit coefficients(5.4), set

    vterm78=(nu78_n/2)x_0-sum_(i<n)(C78)_(i,n)g_i.

The exact terminal completion is

    M78(q)=gamma78*||g_n-vterm78/gamma78||^2
     +q||x_0||^2-sum_(i<n)nu78_i<x_0,g_i>
     +sum_(i,j<n)(C78)_ij<g_i,g_j>
     -||vterm78||^2/gamma78.                         (6.4)

All remaining coefficients in(6.4) are given by(3.3),(4.3),(4.5),(5.4).
An incoming-terminal-free square theorem is NOT applied to this altered
array. Equations(6.2)--(6.4) retain all initial, common, terminal and
cross-block expenditures. The failures below are evaluations of this
whole polynomial, before any coordinate is removed.

## 7. Law I: the first original gradient is already negative

Take x_0=0, g_0=1 and every other original gradient zero, including g_n.
For a pair0->v, write ell=P_v>0. Then y_0=-1, y_v=-b, where b=h_0
is the physical first step from Section4.
Equations(5.1)--(5.2) give

    Delta_(0,target)=-delta*[1+(b-1)*2S/ell]<0.      (7.1)

Every pair not containing0 contributes zero, regardless of its position
vectors, since g_a-g_b=0. There is no pair ending at0. The adjacent
pair(0,1) belongs to E78 and has ell=b, delta=vartheta78*2b/D.
Using the EXACT base diagonal(4.8), the complete value obeys

    M_I(q;g_0=1,x_0=0)
      <=(2/D)*[1-vartheta78*((b-1)D+1)]<0.          (7.2)

The final sign holds for every m>=1536: b-1>2/5,
vartheta78=1/(4m^2), and D>=2^(m+1)-1>10m^2. For the last elementary
inequality, it holds at m=16, and doubling2^(m+1)-1 and adding1
preserves it because2m^2>=(m+1)^2 for m>=3. No recurrence was evaluated
by a program. The inequality is therefore far inside its stated domain.

This is an original gradient diagonal with x_0=0, so changing q does
not alter the negative value. All final coefficients remain nonnegative,
all value flow and Huber contact equations hold, and the optimum profile
has changed. The PSD conclusion nevertheless fails. The mechanism is
specific: paying even a small FRACTION of an optimum row to a nearby
query requires contact-balancing finite weights of order2S/ell.
Those weights consume the first-gradient diagonal.

## 8. Law II fixes that diagonal, but the full deep-wave test fails

For Law II, Tforward=vartheta78*mu_a and
Tbackward=Tforward*w78_b/w78_a. On the first-gradient test, each of
its m outgoing pairs has value

    vartheta78*mu_0*(-b+w78_b/w78_0).

Therefore its entire diagonal is at least

    (2/D)-epsilon*mu_0*b
      =(2/D)*(1-epsilon*b^2)>0,                     (8.1)

since b^2<2. Thus the second law repairs the precise diagonal failure
of Law I. A different full-space test is required.

We derive the inherited negative wave, including ALL depths, here.
Let pterm_j=Psrc_j with only its terminal gradient1, and define

    Fterm_j=A_j^2(1+t_j)-2A_j+(lambda+1).

In(4.4) take Y=-(A_j-1),Z=-1. Expansion of the bracket is Fterm_j.
Thus the finite expression is

    pterm_j=sum_(l<j)(tstar-t_l)Fterm_l
                                product_(v=l+1)^(j-1)k_v. (8.2)

At depth1 pterm_1=2-b^2<3epsilon. The formulas(4.2) give
k_1<7,0<tstar-t_1<epsilon,Fterm_1<13, whence
pterm_2<34epsilon<2epsilon*A_2^2. The depth2-to3 new term is
negative. Its normalized old coefficient is strictly between0 and1,
so pterm_3/A_3^2<2epsilon even if the old coefficient is negative.

For l>=3, monotonicity and the exact t relation give

    t_3>c*tstar/(2+tstar)=(1+epsilon)*tstar,
    t_l-tstar>(2/5)epsilon.

Since A_l>=8 and t_l>2/5, Fterm_l>A_l^2. Together with
A_(l+1)<(5/2)A_l this yields

    (t_l-tstar)Fterm_l/A_(l+1)^2>(8/125)epsilon.

The propagation multiplier is exactly

    k_l/(2+t_l)^2
      =1-(t_l-tstar)(1+t_l)/(2+t_l)^2,
    1-epsilon<k_l/(2+t_l)^2<1, l>=2.                (8.3)

Use (1+t)/(2+t)^2<=1/4 and t_l-tstar<lambda epsilon<5epsilon/2
for the lower inequality. Every product of at most m such factors
is greater than (1-epsilon)^m>=1-m epsilon=3/4; the last inequality
follows by induction on the integer power. Unrolling(8.2) with these
bounds retains every negative contribution and gives

    pterm_j/A_j^2<[2-(6/125)(j-3)]epsilon,
                                      3<=j<=m.     (8.4)

Now take j=m-3, r=2^j-1 and Hstep=h_r=1+d_j. On the FULL original
scalar formal space assign

    x_0=0, g_0=-Hstep/b, g_r=1,
    g_i=0 for every other i, including the actual terminal n. (8.5)

The actual formal displacement is b*(-Hstep/b)+Hstep=0. All later
formal positions vanish. In the first H_j there is no primitive cross
coefficient between its first and terminal gradients: g_0 enters only
the first depth1 bracket of(4.4), while its final terminal enters disjoint
brackets. The first diagonal is2-b^2 and the final is pterm_j. The next
join has Y=0,Z=1 and contributes

    -Edef_j, Edef_j=(t_j-tstar)A_j^2(lambda+t_j)>0.

All larger primitive brackets have Y=Z=0. At the root objective join,
x_p=g_p=T_R=0. Consequently the ENTIRE base polynomial at any real q is

    D*Mbase(q;8.5)
      =(2-b^2)(Hstep/b)^2+pterm_j-Edef_j.            (8.6)

Here Hstep/(b A_j)<1/2, since A_j>5,d_j<A_j/2,b>7/5.
Combining(8.4),(8.6) and2-b^2<3epsilon, and discarding only the
strictly negative -Edef_j, proves

    Mbase(q;8.5)
      <[11/4-(6/125)(j-3)]epsilon*A_j^2/D.          (8.7)

No global-memory statement was used as a premise in this derivation.

It remains to bound EVERY Law II row increment, including terminal rows.
The complete proximal coordinates of(8.5) are

    y_0=Hstep/b,
    y_i=Hstep for1<=i<r,
    y_r=Hstep-1,
    y_i=0 for i>r.

For each dyadic pair u<v the increment is

    vartheta78*mu_u*(g_u-g_v)
                      *(y_v-(w78_v/w78_u)*y_u).    (8.8)

All possible nonzero cases are the following; they exhaust every pair:

* u=0,v<r: the increment is negative, since
  y_v-(w78_v/w78_0)y_0>=Hstep(1-1/h_0)>0.
* u=0,v=r: the increment is negative, since
  Hstep-1-Hstep/h_0>0; Hstep>7 and h_0>7/5 suffice.
* u=0,v>r: it is nonnegative and at most
  vartheta78*mu_0*Hstep^2/h_0^2. There are at most m such pairs.
* 0<u<r,v=r: it equals
  vartheta78*mu_u*[1-Hstep*(P_r-P_u)/w78_u]
  and is at most vartheta78*mu_u.
* u=r,v>r: it is negative.
* Neither endpoint is0 or r: it is exactly zero.

The sum over all positive contributions is therefore bounded by

    M_II(q;8.5)-Mbase(q;8.5)
       <=2epsilon*Hstep^2/(bD)+2epsilon/m.          (8.9)

This uses mu_0=2b/D, sum mu=2, and at most m source0 pairs. It DOES
NOT discard positive remote or terminal contributions. For A_j>=10,
Hstep/A_j<3/5 and b>7/5, so2Hstep^2/(bA_j^2)<18/35<1.
There are exactly three remaining SAME-m joins; hence
A_m<(5/2)^3 A_j and D<(125/4)A_j. In particular
2D/(m A_j^2)<125/(2m A_j)<1. Equation(8.9) is strictly less than
2epsilon*A_j^2/D. Combining with(8.7), j=m-3, yields the full-space bound

    M_II(q;8.5)
      <[19/4-(6/125)(m-6)]epsilon*A_(m-3)^2/D<0,
                       every m>=1536 and EVERY real q. (8.10)

The final numerical constants are exact rational inequalities; no
floating-point calculation or executed recurrence establishes them.
Law II has all signs, all original flow, all exact contact equations,
a changed optimum profile, and the positive first-gradient diagonal(8.1).
It still fails PSD in the full original space by(8.10).

## 9. Scope of the two failures and the remaining interface

The two concrete laws(5.3) are excluded in this joint UNVERIFIED draft.
Their common mechanism was contact-preserving transport of original
optimum mass across dyadic paired queries, with explicit paid debits.
Law I pays too much near the initial query because its finite-row mass
contains the physical factor2S/(P_b-P_a). Law II suppresses that factor;
its total available positive correction on the old deep wave then stays
below the accumulated negative base contribution. These are different
failures, both measured on the WHOLE original polynomial.

The draft does not exclude arbitrary transfer amounts, other pairing
supports, signed decrements of finite rows, alternative base arrays,
nonzero incoming optimum rows, non-contacting arrays at an inflated q,
or any unrestricted PEP certificate. No assertion is made that the raw
finite arrays belong to either of these laws. In particular the raw
arrays were never rounded or projected into the proposed family.
The counterdirections(7.2),(8.5) are formal PSD tests, NOT interpolable
primal trajectories: x_0=0 with a nonzero gradient is incompatible with
starting an actual differentiable function at its normalized minimizer.
They imply no lower bound on W.

What changed relative to the previous round is concrete: original
optimum-profile decrements/increments and nontrivial terminal incoming
mass are now observed in all three saved arrays; the full original
matrices act positively on finite-depth versions of the old displacement
space; two explicit nonseparable dyadic profile transports now have exact
sign, flow, contact and complete terminal/initial formulas. What remains
missing is a global nonnegative multiplier law with a FULL-space PSD proof
at a same-m bound such as21/(20D_m), or a static full primal witness
blocking that bound. Finitely many m<=6 samples cannot supply either.
This is a theorem-level interface, not a routine comparison or a fitted
profile. The original exact-profile target remains OPEN.

Skills used: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving,

# C94: actual changing gates and the price of a curved background

Status: **COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT**. No verifier call is authorized.
This is runtime round 2 of the current allocation, with completed count 24
before this round and ceiling 25. No numerical/model evaluation, solver,
replay, installation or subagent was used. The original target remains open.

The only accepted mathematical predecessors used below are
`1252b8823bb5a290` (the original interpolation convention and its necessity)
and `b98bb807d514e80d` (the scalar lower bounds and original benchmark bound).
Every new assertion is derived in this one joint draft. C93 is motivation,
not a mathematical predecessor. The finite C59 data are not replayed or
used as an asymptotic premise.

## 1. The resolved class and conclusions

All spaces are finite-dimensional real Euclidean spaces. For positive
radii r_minus and r_plus, a centered asymmetric Huber function phi has
phi(0)=0 and derivative

    phi'(s)=max(-r_minus,min(s,r_plus)).

Consider one STATIC objective

    F(x)=x^T A x/2 + sum_(j=1)^r alpha_j phi_j(u_j^T x),       (1)

where A is symmetric positive semidefinite, each u_j is a unit vector,
alpha_j>0, and

    A+sum_j alpha_j u_j u_j^T <= I.                           (2)

The number r may be zero. Dimensions, directions, both radii and weights
may depend arbitrarily on the horizon. Let

    N0=ker A intersect intersection_j u_j^perp, E=N0^perp.

The additional hypothesis tested here is a background-curvature floor:

    z^T A z >= mu ||z||^2 for all z in E, 0<=mu<=1.          (3)

There is NO restriction on which ridge regions the actual queried or
terminal points visit. Hessians in different regions need not commute.
Define the actual GD iterates for a nonnegative physical word h by

    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad F(x_i),
    V=2[F(x_n)-min F]/dist(x_0,argmin F)^2.                   (4)

Initial points in argmin F are omitted. All terminal gradients and values
are those of the SAME function (1).

For the ORIGINAL diagnostic words fix m>=128 and set

    sigma=1+sqrt(2), c=sigma(1+1/m), beta=1-1/m,
    a=beta/c, ell=1+a, d_j=1+a c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, N=2^m, n=N-1,
    S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j, K=1/(2M_m-1).                  (5)

Every child word in this proof keeps these SAME enclosing-m parameters.
The main conclusions are:

1. If mu>=mu_exact:=2/ell-1, the exact supremum of V over the whole
   class (1)--(3), with all changing regions allowed, is

       beta^(2N-2) c^(-2m).                                (6)

   The unit quadratic attains it. The result is exact over this specified
   class, not over unrestricted convex smooth objectives.

2. Put

       mu_floor=[1-1/(beta c)]/ell.                         (7)

   If mu>=mu_floor, every such run satisfies

       V <= beta^(-2)c^(-2m),
       V/K < 2/[beta^2 c^m],
       B_n V -> 0 uniformly over the class as m -> infinity. (8)

   Here B_n is the ORIGINAL Zhang--Jiang denominator of accepted
   `b98bb807d514e80d`. Both mu_floor and mu_exact tend to sqrt(2)-1,
   with mu_floor<mu_exact for every stated m. The thresholds, dimension,
   number of ridges and all other data may vary with m.

3. There is an explicit stronger necessary condition for attaining even
   the accepted same-word scalar value K. Define

       L_m=log[beta^2 c^(2m)/(2M_m-1)],
       mu_comp=[1-exp(L_m/N)/(beta c)]/ell.                 (9)

   Every run in (1)--(3) with V>=K must have mu<=mu_comp. Moreover

       1/(2m-1)<mu_comp<mu_floor,
       mu_floor-mu_comp=[exp(L_m/N)-1]/(beta c ell)>0.      (10)

4. The resolved class really contains nonlinear, noncommuting,
   repeatedly changing queried gates on ORIGINAL H_m. Section 6 gives
   one static two-dimensional function whose ridge visits are positive
   outer, negative outer, positive outer, then core at every remaining
   actual sample. Its actual gradients change sign at EVERY physical
   step, are not collinear, and have nonconstant correlations. Its true
   initial distance is exactly 1. Its payoff is nevertheless bounded by
   (6), hence is strictly weaker than K on this same word.

This supplies a precise obstruction for the newly specified curved-
background subclass. It does not exclude weak or singular backgrounds,
or arbitrary nonlinear changing-region functions. The upper envelopes
below are not claimed attainable outside the regime of (6).

## 2. Static legality, true minimizer distance and all original pairs

Every summand in (1) is nonnegative. A Huber term vanishes precisely
when its scalar argument is zero. Thus argmin F=N0 and min F=0.
Let z0 be the orthogonal projection of x0 onto N0. Translation of every
iterate by -z0 changes neither the values nor the gradients, because
A z0=0 and u_j^T z0=0 for all j. All gradients lie in E. We may work
on E with x0 in E; its squared norm is the TRUE squared distance in (4).

For any two points x,z define the scalar secant coefficient theta_j by

    phi_j'(u_j^T x)-phi_j'(u_j^T z)
                 =theta_j u_j^T(x-z), 0<=theta_j<=1.       (11)

If the arguments coincide take theta_j=0. The monotonicity and the
1-Lipschitz property of clipping prove both bounds, across any number
of region boundaries. Consequently

    grad F(x)-grad F(z)=C(x,z)(x-z),
    C(x,z)=A+sum_j alpha_j theta_j u_j u_j^T,
    mu I_E <= C(x,z)|E <= I_E.                             (12)

This proves global convexity and 1-smoothness. It also gives the actual
secant matrix used below; no matrix is independently chosen along the
trajectory. For h>=0, diagonalizing this ONE symmetric matrix yields

    ||x-z-h[grad F(x)-grad F(z)]||
       <= q_mu(h)||x-z||,
    q_mu(h)=max{|1-h mu|,|1-h|},                           (13)

for x,z in E. Taking z=0 and multiplying the true one-step bounds gives

    ||x_n|| <= ||x0|| product_(i<n) q_mu(h_i).

Smoothness at the minimizer gives 2F(x_n)<=||x_n||^2, so

    V <= product_(i<n) q_mu(h_i)^2.                        (14)

No commutativity of DIFFERENT secant matrices was used in (13)--(14).

For completeness every original ordered interpolation pair is retained.
Put f_i=F(x_i), g_i=grad F(x_i) and include x_star=g_star=f_star=0.
For a fixed i, J(z)=F(z)-<g_i,z> has its global minimum at x_i.
The 1-smooth descent inequality at x_j gives

    J(x_i)<=J(x_j-grad J(x_j))
            <=J(x_j)-||g_j-g_i||^2/2.

Rearrangement is the original residual

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.         (15)

This is the necessity argument of accepted `1252b8823bb5a290`, written
out here to cover both optimum directions, every remote queried pair,
both terminal directions, and all repeated/zero-step samples.

More generally, for ANY fixed nonnegative word all of whose steps obey
h_i>=2/(1+mu), (13) has q_mu(h_i)=h_i-1. Indeed this inequality implies
h_i>=1, 1-h_i mu<=h_i-1, and 1-h_i mu>=-(h_i-1).
The bound (14) is then attained by F(x)=x^2/2, x0=1, r=0.
Thus the exact class supremum on such a word is product_i(h_i-1)^2.
This general finite-word observation includes the empty word. It makes
no matching assertion for schedules that fail the displayed step condition.

## 3. Exact physical word algebra and the same-word scalar comparator

The bounds needed below follow from m>=128 and elementary squaring of
7/5<sqrt(2)<99/70:

    12/5<c<5/2, 2/5<a<29/70<1/2,
    7/5<ell<99/70, 127/128<=beta<1,
    d_1=1+beta=2-1/m>19/10,
    d_j>2 for j>=2.                                      (16)

For the lower bound on a, a=(m-1)/[(m+1)sigma] is at least
8890/21801>2/5. For the upper bound on c,
c<(169/70)(129/128)<5/2. Also a<1/sigma=sigma-2<c-2.

Counting a level j in H_m gives exactly 2^(m-j-1) occurrences.
Summing the physical masses or solving M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j gives

    M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j,
    A_mass=a/(c-2), 0<A_mass<1.                           (17)

In particular 1<=M_j<=c^j and a c^j<=(c-2)M_j.

We now rederive the best Huber/single-cut comparison directly from
accepted `b98bb807d514e80d`, without using C93 as a lemma. For a cut of
level j, the numerator is (a c^j)^2. If P is its actual preceding mass,
its denominator is

    (1+P)^2[1+2(S_m-d_j-P)].                             (18)

The logarithm of (18) has second derivative
-2/(1+P)^2-4/[1+2(S_m-d_j-P)]^2<0. Hence its minimum over the mass
interval from the first to the last occurrence is at one of those
endpoints. Write M=M_(m-1). At the first occurrence P=S_j and the
following mass is at least S_(m-1). Its cut value is at most
(c-2)^2/(2M-1). At the last occurrence the preceding mass is at least
S_(m-1) and the following mass is S_j. Its value is at most

    (c-2)^2 M_j^2/[M^2(2M_j-1)] <= (c-2)^2/(2M-1),       (19)

because x^2/(2x-1) is nondecreasing for x>=1 and M_j<=M.
Finally M_m<=cM, c<5/2 and c-2<1/2 imply

    (c-2)^2(2M_m-1)<=(5M-1)/4<=2M-1.

The last inequality is equivalent to M>=1. Thus every cut is no larger
than K=1/(2M_m-1). The global Huber term attains K, proving

    K_n(H_m)=K.                                          (20)

An explicit attaining scalar function is the symmetric Huber function
with radius K, started at 1. Its iterates are
x_i=1-K sum_(t<i)h_t; they all remain on
the positive outer ray. Its terminal normalized value is K.

## 4. All gates allowed: exact supremum and the wider curvature screen

The product of the upper-endpoint factors over H_j is

    U_j:=product_(h_i in H_j)(h_i-1)
        =a^(2^j-1)c^(2^j-j-1)
        =beta^(2^j-1)c^(-j).                            (21)

For j=0 the expression gives 1. One can also check (21) by the exact
recursion U_(j+1)=U_j^2 a c^j.

For h>1 and 0<=mu<=1, (13) can be written

    q_mu(h)=max{1-mu h,h-1}.                            (22)

For all bridge levels j>=2 the second term wins, by d_j>2. At level 1
it wins whenever

    mu>=mu_1:=2/d_1-1=1/(2m-1).                         (23)

Define q0=max{1-mu ell,a} and q1=max{1-mu d_1,beta}. The ENTIRE
product bound, with no lost low-level factor, is

    V <= U_m^2 (q0/a)^N (q1/beta)^(N/2).                 (24)

This holds for all 0<=mu<=1. If mu>=mu_1 it reduces exactly to

    V <= beta^(-2)c^(-2m) [beta c q0]^N.                 (25)

If mu>=mu_exact=(1-a)/(1+a)=2/ell-1, q0=a. All steps satisfy the
condition following (15), and (25) equals beta^(2N-2)c^(-2m).
The unit quadratic attains it with true distance 1, proving (6).
It can be embedded with arbitrary flat directions without changing the
true-distance quotient, because Section 2 projects onto E.

For the weaker condition mu>=mu_floor of (7), note first that
beta c>2 and ell<3/2. Therefore mu_floor>1/3>mu_1. Also
1/(beta c)>a, since a beta c=beta^2<1. It follows that
mu_floor<mu_exact and q0<=1/(beta c). Equation (25) proves the first
bound in (8). By (17) and (20),

    V/K <=(2M_m-1) beta^(-2)c^(-2m)
           <2/[beta^2 c^m].                            (26)

This ratio is less than 1 for the stated m. The accepted original-
benchmark estimate in `b98bb807d514e80d` is
B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3). Since n=2^m-1<2^m,

    B_n V <= beta^(-2)[c^(-2m)+4(3/c^2)^m] -> 0,         (27)

uniformly, because beta>=127/128 and c>12/5 gives 3/c^2<25/48<1.
No fitted profile, internal child capacity or substituted recurrence is
used here. Both thresholds tend to (1-1/sigma)/(1+1/sigma)
=sqrt(2)-1 by their displayed definitions.

## 5. A necessary weak direction for even the scalar payoff

The exponent L_m in (9) is strictly positive. Indeed (17) implies

    exp(L_m)=beta^2 c^(2m)/(2M_m-1)
               >beta^2 c^m/2>1.                       (28)

The last inequality follows already from beta>1/2, c>2 and m>=128.
Thus mu_comp<mu_floor. To check the lower bound in (10), first
2m/2^m<=1/8 for m>=7, as follows from 2^7>=16*7 and induction.
Since exp(L_m)<=c^(2m),

    exp(L_m/N)<=c^(1/8)<(5/2)^(1/8)<9/8.                (29)

The last strict comparison follows by the binomial expansion of
(1+1/8)^8, retaining enough positive terms to exceed 5/2. Hence
exp(L_m/N)/(beta c)<9/16 and mu_comp>7/24>1/(2m-1).

Suppose a legal run has V>=K. If mu<mu_1, then already mu<mu_comp.
Otherwise (25) gives

    q0 >= exp(L_m/N)/(beta c) > 1/(beta c)>a.             (30)

Consequently q0=1-mu ell, and (30) is exactly mu<=mu_comp.
Subtracting (9) from (7) proves the exact gap formula (10).

Thus the background matrix of any competitive member of this static
family must have a sufficiently weak direction in the active space E.
This is only a NECESSARY condition. No construction below that floor
is supplied by the product bound, and mu_comp is not asserted to be a
sharp threshold for the actual fixed-function family.

## 6. Actual multiple outer sign changes, noncommuting gates and all visits

Here is a concrete run in the resolved class, for EVERY original H_m
with m>=128. Let phi_r be the symmetric Huber function with radius
r=1/100 and define, once and for all,

    F_gate(x,y)=3x^2/8+7y^2/16
                    +(1/16)phi_r((x+y)/sqrt(2)),
    X_0=(1,0).                                         (31)

Write u=(1,1)/sqrt(2), A=diag(3/4,7/8) and

    H=A+(1/16)u u^T=(1/32)[25,1;1,29].                 (32)

The Hessian in either outer region is A; in the core it is H. Globally
(3/4)I<=A<=H<=(15/16)I<I. Thus (31) is a fixed convex 1-smooth
function, its unique minimizer is 0, and the true initial squared
distance is exactly 1. Its visited Hessians do not commute:
the (1,2) entry of A H-H A is -1/256, hence nonzero.

We prove ALL its region visits, without a finite replay. Abbreviate

    b=d_1=1+beta,
    k=r/(16sqrt(2))=sqrt(2)/3200,
    s(lambda)=1-ell lambda, t(lambda)=1-b lambda.

At X_0 the ridge is strictly positive outer. Its gradient is

    g_0=(3/4+k,k),
    X_1=(s(3/4)-ell k,-ell k).                         (33)

By ell>7/5, the first coordinate of X_1 is less than -1/20 and the
second is negative. Hence u^T X_1<-1/(20sqrt(2))<-r: the first actual
step lands in the strictly negative outer region. Its gradient is
g_1=A X_1-k(1,1), and the second actual step gives

    X_2=(t(3/4)[s(3/4)-ell k]+b k,
                         t(7/8)(-ell k)+b k).          (34)

Both entries are positive. Since b>19/10, t(3/4)<-17/40, the first
entry is greater than 17/800. Consequently
u^T X_2>17/(800sqrt(2))>r, so this query is strictly positive outer.
The third physical step is again ell. For lambda=3/4 or 7/8 set

    C(lambda)=-ell s(lambda)t(lambda)+b s(lambda)-ell.

Directly applying this third step in its now established positive
outer region yields

    X_3=(s(3/4)^2 t(3/4)+k C(3/4), k C(7/8)).           (35)

All s,t in (35) are negative. Therefore both entries of X_3 are
strictly negative. We next establish a uniform small-norm bound with
explicit rational margins. From (16),

    |s(3/4)|<61/1000, |t(3/4)|<1/2,
    |s(7/8)|<119/500, |t(7/8)|<3/4,
    ell<283/200, b<2, k<443/1000000.

These imply

    |C(3/4)| < (283/200)(61/2000)+122/1000+283/200
                  <1581/1000,
    |C(7/8)| < (283/200)(357/2000)+238/500+283/200
                  <2144/1000.

Using the sum of absolute coordinates to bound the Euclidean norm,

    ||X_3|| < (61/1000)^2/2
                   +(443/1000000)(3725/1000)
             =3510675/1000000000 < 9/2500.              (36)

The slightly larger round margin 9/2500 is retained below; no numerical
evaluation of the trajectory is being used in (33)--(36).

It remains to prove that EVERY subsequent actual query and the actual
terminal sample are in the core. For H_j let R_j be the largest product
of (h_i-1) over ANY prefix, including the empty prefix. Formula (21) gives

    U_j a c^j=a beta^(2^j-1)<=a<1.

The physical recursion of H_(j+1) shows that every prefix is either a
prefix of its first H_j or has product U_j a c^j times a prefix of its
second H_j. Thus R_j=1 for all j. More precisely every NONEMPTY prefix
has product at most a: this holds for H_1, is inherited in the first
child, and holds in the second child by the displayed inequality.

The background floor 3/4 exceeds mu_exact, so each ACTUAL step of
(31) obeys ||X_(i+1)||<=(h_i-1)||X_i||, independently of its current
gate. The product over the first three steps is U_2=a^2 beta. For any
i>=3, the suffix product between sample 3 and sample i is therefore
at most a/U_2=1/(a beta). Since a>2/5 and beta>=127/128,

    ||X_i|| <=||X_3||/(a beta)
               <(9/2500)(320/127)<1/100=r.              (37)

This includes i=3. Hence |u^T X_i|<r at EVERY remaining actual sample.
There is no circular assumption of future core behavior in (37): its
contraction bound was valid for every gate before any visits were known.

Thus the complete gate chronology is

    positive outer, negative outer, positive outer,
                       core, core, ..., core.           (38)

The terminal position is explicitly specified, without a replay, by

    X_n=[product_(i=3)^(n-1)(I-h_i H)]X_3,               (39)

with the usual empty-product convention. Equations (33)--(35) and (39)
give every actual point and gradient of this one static function.

For i>=3 all entries of I-h_i H are strictly negative: h_i>=ell>7/5,
and (7/5)(25/32)>1, (7/5)(29/32)>1. Since both coordinates of X_3
are negative, multiplication shows that both coordinates of EVERY
X_i, i>=3, have sign (-1)^i. H has strictly positive entries, so the
same is true of both gradient coordinates. Equations (33)--(35) also
give g_0 positive, g_1 negative and g_2 positive. Thus there is an actual
gradient-coordinate sign change at EVERY step, not a relabeling of
character signs or an alternation of separately chosen functions.

The gradients are genuinely noncollinear. Let b_vec=k(1,1). In the
first two outer regions,

    g_1=g_0-2b_vec-ell A g_0,
    det(g_0,g_1)=-3k/2-(ell/8)(3/4+k)k<0.               (40)

Moreover <g_0,g_1><0 while <g_0,g_2>>0, so their normalized pair
correlations are not constant. The switch from A to the noncommuting
H occurs between actual queried samples 2 and 3. This run is outside
the fixed-queried-region hypothesis of C93 and outside the single-
initial-sign chronology of C92.

All original pairs hold by Section 2, and the true radius is 1. Its
terminal payoff is exactly X_n^T H X_n, with X_n given by (39), and

    0<V_gate<=beta^(2N-2)c^(-2m)<K.                     (41)

To see strict positivity, H has eigenvalues (27 plus or minus sqrt(5))/32.
Its smaller eigenvalue exceeds 3/4, and h_i>7/5, so no I-h_i H is
singular. Formula (35) is nonzero and H is positive definite. The
upper comparison follows from (6) and (26). Thus (31) is a real
changing-region mechanism, but it supplies NO lower-bound improvement;
the same-word accepted global Huber witness is already stronger.

## 7. Attack on the tempting extension below the curvature floor

The product in (14) is an upper envelope over endpoint contractions.
It is not an instruction to switch curvatures independently. A concrete
failure occurs on the first two actual steps of H_m. Set mu=1/4 and
pretend that a scalar run starts at x0=1, uses g0=mu x0, and after the
first step uses the upper endpoint curvature with g1=x1. Then

    x1=1-ell/4, 0<x1<x0, and g1=x1>1/4=g0,              (42)

because ell<3. These two queries violate monotonicity of the derivative
of any convex scalar function. Equivalently
(g0-g1)(x0-x1)<0. They cannot be part of a legal static adversary, even
though each chosen scalar curvature separately belongs to [mu,1].

This attacks the failed positive interpretation of the envelope below
its proved screen. It does NOT prove that the whole weak-background
class is harmless. It also does not assert a multidimensional static
counterexample from arbitrary Hessian switching.

The genuine scalar Huber witness attaining K supplies the opposite
scope check: it has A=0, lies within (1)--(2), and has value K, which
exceeds beta^(-2)c^(-2m) for the stated m. Thus deleting the curvature
floor from (8) is false even before introducing multiple interacting
ridge directions. This known witness is a scope counterexample, not a
new lower bound.

## 8. What this leaves open, and literature grounding

C94 resolves a changing-queried-region subclass that C93 did not cover:
an arbitrary number of coupled ridge gates cannot offset a background
floor at or above (7). The exact strong-floor extremum is (6), and even
scalar competitiveness requires (9). The explicit run (31) proves that
this class is not empty or secretly a fixed-region class. It includes
many actual sign changes and different noncommuting visited Hessians.

The remaining static-ridge question requires weak background directions
in E, together with actual queried gate changes. No sufficient gluing
rule, improved H_m lower bound, arbitrary-h matching theorem or original
target fact is established here. In particular neither max's actual31
child nor the finite C59 adversary is inserted as a reusable child.

Search was conditioned on the changing-gate mechanism. Matlas returned
the standard strong-convexity sector inequality (for example,
arXiv:1909.13400, Lemma III.2), but no external result is used as a proof
premise. The downloaded Stanford course notes
`https://web.stanford.edu/class/msande318/notes/notes-first-order-smooth.pdf`
were read through the browser after local text extraction was unavailable
and one browser request failed. Lemma 2.2 concerns the usual contraction
estimate under twice differentiability. Huber functions are not twice
continuously differentiable at their joins. The explicit clipped secants
(11)--(13) supply the needed proof here without a pointwise vector mean-
value assertion or any smooth-Hessian assumption.

All claims remain UNVERIFIED. The current assignment prohibits fact_submit.

# C56: a legal two-dimensional repair and its unavoidable normalization cost

Status: **complete scoped joint proof candidate, not verifier-accepted**.
No optimization or fact_submit is allocated. The only mathematical
predecessor is accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, for smooth-convex
interpolation, its explicit conjugate construction, and exact unqueried
terminal completion. The scalar template is defined and analyzed here;
no unverified C53/C55 claim is used as a premise.

This is a primal construction for the actual critical schedule. It produces
one globally defined convex smooth function, with all queried data jointly
legal, in dimension two. Its limitation is quantitative: the specific
repair mechanism cannot retain the very large formal objective ratio.
Neither this construction nor its obstruction decides the actual worst-case
performance of the schedule or the unrestricted minimax problem.

## 1. Schedule and a completely specified scalar formal trajectory

Fix m>=128 and put

    epsilon=1/m, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,
    d_j=a c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.

Write h=H_m, n=n_m, S=S_m, M_m=1+S, and

    b0=1+a, b1=1+d_1=2-epsilon,
    h_max=1+d_(m-1).

Every step is greater than one; the largest step is the central bridge.
All choices depend only on the fixed horizon, before an oracle is queried.

Define signed template moments

    k_2=M_2+6epsilon,
    k_(j+1)=-k_j^2/d_j for j>=2.                       (1)

For an H2 block whose terminal gradient is the scalar v, prescribe

    g_0=(2-c+6epsilon/b0)v,
    g_1=v, g_2=c v, g_3=v.                           (2)

Start this block at x_0=k_2 v and use its actual GD steps. Directly,
b0 g_0+b1 g_1+b0 g_2+g_3=k_2 v, so x_3=v.
At a higher H_(j+1) block with terminal gradient v, use the level-j
template with terminal v on the right and with terminal

    v_left=-k_j v/d_j

on the left. Start the parent at x_start=k_(j+1)v. Since its left queried
displacement is (k_j-1)v_left, its left endpoint is v_left. Its bridge
then ends at -d_j v_left=k_j v, exactly the prescribed right start.
The right endpoint is v. This proves the actual chronological GD recursion
of the formal positions and gradients at every level.

Use terminal gradient 1 at the root. Thus

    x_0=k_m, x_n=g_n=1,
    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i.

For each scalar sample put y_i=x_i-g_i. Every H2 seed endpoint has y=0.
Inside a seed with terminal v, define G0=2-c+6epsilon/b0. Its gradients
and proximal coordinates, in order, are

    gradients:   (G0, 1, c, 1) v,
    proximal:    (k_2-G0, 1+d_1+b0 c, 1+a c, 0) v.     (3)

The template is not legal. In the rightmost seed the two queried indices
i=n-2 and j=n-1 have gradients 1,c and displacement x_i-x_j=b1.
Their sum of smooth-convex residuals, independently of function values, is

    Q_ij+Q_ji=(c-1)(d_1+c)>0,                        (4)

where Q is defined below. Thus no scalar function can realize these jets.

For the size estimates needed in the repair, the critical ratio obeys

    R0=k_2/(c d_2)<1-epsilon/4,
    |k_j|=(1-epsilon)c^j R0^(2^(j-2)).                (5)

Here is a direct verification of the strict gap. Since M_2=5-epsilon+2a,

    (c d_2-M_2)/epsilon
      =[6sqrt(2)+epsilon-2lambda^2 epsilon^2
                                  -lambda^2 epsilon^3]/(1+epsilon)>8.

For the last inequality subtract 8 after multiplying by 1+epsilon and use

    6sqrt(2)-8-7epsilon-2lambda^2 epsilon^2-lambda^2 epsilon^3
      >2/5-7/128-12/128^2-6/128^3>0.

It follows that c d_2-k_2>2epsilon, while c d_2<25/4. This proves the
first inequality in (5); the second follows by squaring the ratio at each
step of (1). Every absolute left-child scale |k_j|/d_j is less than c.
Consequently every seed's absolute terminal amplitude is at most c^(m-2).

The elementary bounds c<5/2, k_2<6, |G0|<1, and b0<sqrt(2)<3/2 give
from (3)

    |g_i|<3c^m, |y_i|<7c^m                           (6)

for every sample. For clarity, k_2<6 follows from
M_2<5+6/7 and 6epsilon<=3/64; and
G0 lies between -1/2 and 2-12/5+3/64<0. These bounds include the terminal
sample, with the optimum sample g_star=y_star=0 added separately.

Equation (5) also gives

    |k_m|<c^m exp[-2^(m-4)/m] ->0.

Thus the formal choice f_n=1/2 would have an enormous ratio 1/k_m^2. It
is precisely this initially tiny normalization that the repair must pay for.

## 2. One explicit extra coordinate repairs ALL interpolation inequalities

Choose any curvature

    0<kappa<1/h_max,

and define the monotone quadratic trajectory

    xi_0=1, xi_(i+1)=(1-kappa h_i)xi_i,
    xi_star=0.

All xi_i are positive and strictly decreasing. This is a single extra
coordinate; no incompatible instances are averaged. For a scalar T>0
set the proposed two-dimensional jets to

    X_i=(x_i,T xi_i),
    G_i=(g_i,T kappa xi_i),
    f_i=[g_i^2+T^2 kappa xi_i^2]/2,
    X_star=G_star=0, f_star=0.                        (7)

The entire vector GD recursion is exact:
X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i G_i. The scalar first coordinate by itself is still not a
legal function; the final interpolating function will be jointly convex
in both coordinates and is not asserted to be a separable sum.

Use the accepted interpolation convention

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2.

Direct expansion gives the FULL all-pair law, including every star pair:

    Q_ij=g_i(y_j-y_i)
                   -T^2 kappa(1-kappa)(xi_i-xi_j)^2/2. (8)

The first term follows by substituting f_i^formal=g_i^2/2 and x_i=g_i+y_i;
the second is the residual for the quadratic t -> kappa t^2/2. Thus the
same function values and the same two coordinates are tested against all
ordered pairs, not merely against one aggregated dual form.

From (6), g_i(y_j-y_i)<=42c^(2m). For any two distinct finite indices,
their xi displacement is at least one adjacent displacement, and

    xi_i-xi_(i+1)=kappa h_i xi_i>kappa xi_n.

For an optimum pair xi_i>=xi_n>kappa xi_n, since kappa<1. Therefore
the completely explicit choice

    T^2=84c^(2m)/[kappa^3(1-kappa)xi_n^2]+k_m^2        (9)

makes every Q_ij<=0. There is no optimization or nearest-feasible-point
problem hidden in (9): every quantity is an explicit scalar or product
of the predetermined steps.

To produce one actual function, let J={star,0,...,n}, and define on R^2

    H(v)=max_(i in J) {
       <G_i,X_i>-f_i+<X_i,v-G_i>+||v-G_i||^2/2 },
    F(X)=sup_v {<X,v>-H(v)}.                         (10)

By accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290`, F is finite everywhere, convex,
differentiable with gradient Lipschitz constant one, and interpolates every
jet in (7). Formally this use of the predecessor is obtained by appending
one zero step to h, so all indices 0,...,n are queried prefix samples.
The predecessor explicitly permits that zero step. All the required
prefix interpolation inequalities were proved in (8)-(9).

The minimizer of F is UNIQUE and equals zero. Indeed write

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+
           max_(i in J){<X_i-G_i,v>+||G_i||^2/2-f_i}.

At v=0, the star term is zero, whereas every other intercept is

    ||G_i||^2/2-f_i=-T^2 kappa(1-kappa)xi_i^2/2<0.

There are finitely many terms. In a neighborhood of zero only the star
term is active, so H(v)=||v||^2/2 there. The set of minimizers of its
conjugate is the subgradient set of H at zero, which here is {0}.
This last assertion can also be checked from the defining supremum:
F(X)=0 requires <X,v><=H(v) for all v, hence X=0 by taking arbitrarily
small positive and negative v; and H(v)>=||v||^2/2 gives F(0)=0.

Consequently the TRUE squared distance to the closest minimizer is

    dist(X_0,argmin F)^2=k_m^2+T^2.                  (11)

There is no artificial enlargement from choosing a remote minimizer.
The exact normalized terminal payoff of the constructed function is

    P_assigned=[1+T^2 kappa xi_n^2]/[k_m^2+T^2].       (12)

Multiplying F by any L>0 gives a convex function with L-Lipschitz gradient,
the same GD trajectory for steps h_i/L, and the same project-normalized
payoff 2(F(X_n)-F_star)/dist(X_0,argmin F)^2.

## 3. This is a nonzero legal witness, with a fully quantified scale

For the explicit choice kappa=1/(2S), we have kappa h_i<=1/2, so it is in
the permitted range. For 0<=z<=1/2,

    log(1-z)>=-2z,

because the derivative of log(1-z)+2z is (1-2z)/(1-z)>=0 and its value
at zero is zero. It follows that xi_n>=exp(-2kappa S)=exp(-1).
Since (9) gives T^2>=k_m^2, (12) yields the explicit lower bound

    P_assigned >= kappa xi_n^2/2
                       >=exp(-2)/(4S).               (13)

This is an actual legal two-dimensional witness with order 1/S payoff.
It does not claim a new worst-case lower bound beyond what simpler
quadratic or Huber examples already provide. Its purpose is to make the
proposed deformation of the formal template fully concrete and legal.

## 4. One retained QUERY pair forces a cubic normalization cost

The cost is not an artifact of the conservative sufficient amplitude (9).
Take ANY T>0 and ANY function values for which the queried positions and
gradients (7) are jointly legal with minimizer zero. Keep only the queried
pair i=n-2, j=n-1; no terminal sample is used in this argument.

In the first coordinate the rightmost H2 seed has

    g_i=1, g_j=c, x_i-x_j=b1,
    Q_ij^formal+Q_ji^formal=(c-1)(d_1+c)=:D_bad.

Function values cancel in this sum, so these equalities remain valid for
arbitrary proposed values. In the second coordinate

    xi_i-xi_j=kappa b1 xi_i.

The necessary sum of the TWO original interpolation inequalities is
therefore exactly

    D_bad-T^2 kappa^3(1-kappa)b1^2 xi_(n-2)^2<=0.

Thus every feasible repair in this stated geometry must obey

    T^2 >= D_bad/[kappa^3(1-kappa)b1^2 xi_(n-2)^2].    (14)

We have D_bad>4: c-1>4/3 and d_1+c>3. Also b1<=2 and xi_(n-2)<=1.
Consequently

    1/T^2<kappa^3, and T^2>kappa^(-3)>h_max^3.        (15)

This is a necessary inequality for the original queried geometry. Changing
function values or applying maximal completion at the last unqueried point
cannot remove it. It explains the cubic price: the quadratic coordinate's
adjacent displacement is proportional to kappa, and its interpolation
slack is proportional to another kappa times the squared displacement.

For the assigned terminal values, combining (12),(15) gives

    P_assigned < kappa xi_n^2+kappa^3
               <=1/[2 exp(1) S]+h_max^(-3).           (16)

Here xi_n<=exp(-kappa S), from 1-z<=exp(-z), and
max_(t>=0) t exp(-2St)=1/[2 exp(1)S], as follows by differentiating.

## 5. Maximal terminal completion also pays the cost

Keep only the queried jets i<n with the assigned values (7), together
with the optimum. Use the accepted maximal prefix interpolant from
`1252b8823bb5a290` to complete the last unqueried point. This is a legal
single global function, and its value at X_n is maximal among all
interpolants of those prefix jets. The strict negative intercepts in
Section 2 still apply to every retained non-star jet, so its minimizer
is again uniquely zero. Thus (11) is still the actual closest-minimizer
distance. No terminal gradient or terminal value is imposed in this part.

There is a uniform upper bound on this maximal terminal payoff. More
generally it applies to any legal prefix with the displayed X_i,G_i and
minimizer zero, for its ratio normalized by ||X_0||^2. Let i=n-1 be the
last queried point. Interpolation with the optimum gives

    f_i<=<G_i,X_i>-||G_i||^2/2.

The smoothness inequality on the actual final step X_n=X_i-b0 G_i gives

    F(X_n)<=f_i-b0(1-b0/2)||G_i||^2.

These two inequalities imply

    2F(X_n)<=2<G_i,X_i>-(1+2b0-b0^2)||G_i||^2.        (17)

Here g_i=c, x_i=1+b0 c, and the second coordinate is (T xi_i,T kappa xi_i).
Since 1+2b0-b0^2>0, the right side is at most

    2c+(b0^2-1)c^2+2T^2 kappa xi_i^2
                     <12+2T^2 kappa xi_i^2.

Divide by k_m^2+T^2, apply (15), and use
xi_i<=exp[-kappa(S-b0)]. The normalized completed payoff satisfies

    P_completed < 12kappa^3+2kappa exp[-2kappa(S-b0)]
                 <=12h_max^(-3)+1/[exp(1)(S-b0)].     (18)

The denominator S-b0 is positive. This bound holds uniformly in the
curvature 0<kappa<1/h_max and in every feasible amplitude T. In particular
it covers the explicit repair (9) and exact maximal unqueried-terminal
completion. Every queried-prefix constraint, especially (14), is retained.

As m tends to infinity, S tends to infinity and b0 stays bounded. Also

    M_m<=1+n h_max<2^m h_max,
    h_max>(127/128)lambda^(m-2),
    M_m/h_max^3 < 2^m/h_max^2 ->0.

The last limit follows from 2/lambda^2<1. Equations (16),(18) therefore
give the explicit leading-scale bounds

    limsup M_m P_assigned <=1/[2 exp(1)],
    limsup M_m P_completed <=1/exp(1)<1.              (19)

Hence this whole monotone single-quadratic-coordinate repair mechanism
cannot supply a witness separating the schedule's actual worst case from
the proposed objective level 1/M_m. Its canonical repaired functions have
a genuine unique minimizer, so this failure is not caused by a loose choice
of normalization point. It is caused by the retained adjacent queried
pair (4), which forces the normalization cost (14).

## 6. Exact scope and the remaining repair requirement

The constructive statement covers the original C55 formal trajectory
with one monotone quadratic auxiliary coordinate and the explicit
conjugate function (10). The necessary cost (14) does not depend on the
values, while the unique-minimizer assertion is proved for the two
canonical functions with assigned prefix values: the full-jet interpolant
and its maximal unqueried-terminal completion. For arbitrary different
prefix values or different interpolants, (18) concerns the specified
minimizer zero; it does not silently assert that zero is their closest
minimizer.

No assertion is made about oscillating auxiliary coordinates, multiple
independent repair directions, a changed first-coordinate trajectory,
different knot/value rules, or a completely different adversary. The
result also does not claim that every fixed-dimensional witness has this
cost. The curvature restriction 0<kappa<1/h_max is essential to this
particular monotone repair and is displayed throughout.

The original target quantifies over arbitrary nonnegative schedules,
including zeros and unbounded positive steps. The present construction
uses a strictly positive schedule with growing bridges; it does not
replace those quantifiers. The appended zero step is only a precise way
to apply the already accepted full-prefix interpolation theorem.

For the next primal repair, a concrete requirement is now visible: the
auxiliary coordinates must supply order-one adjacent co-coercivity at
the rightmost H2 defect without paying order kappa^(-3) initial energy,
or the scalar gradients at that pair must be changed. This is a test of
a proposed new construction, not a theorem-equivalent claim that such a
repair exists or has negligible cost.

All new claims here remain draft evidence. There is no new accepted fact,
no unrestricted upper bound for W_n(H_m), and no resolution of the exact
Zhang--Jiang profile problem.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-003152 -->
# General removal of terminal credit with an explicit physical contraction

Status: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. This result changes
the physical steps and does not prove same-schedule credit re-selection.
It uses all actual interpolation coefficients and a full PSD identity.
No solver or verifier call, and no external theorem used as a premise.

## 1. Statement, including zero steps

Fix n>=0 and a nonnegative physical schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)). Write
s=sum h_i, C=1+s. On independent formal gradients g_0,...,g_n and values
f_0,...,f_n, substitute x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t and put

 Q_ij^h=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                            -s(s+1)||g_n||^2,
 J_h=sum_(i<n)h_i Q_(i,n)^h,
 E_theta=V_h-theta J_h.

Suppose 0<=theta<2 and actual nonnegative multipliers Lambda_ij for all
distinct finite pairs give an exact certificate

              E_theta+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij^h=M,       (1)

where all value coefficients vanish and M is PSD on the FULL independent
gradient space. This includes the project range 0<=theta<1.

Define positive parameters and the contracted schedule by

 a=1-theta/2,
 kappa=theta C/(2-theta),
 t=1/(1+kappa)=(2-theta)/(2+theta s),
 c=2/(2+theta s),
 h'_i=t h_i,     Lambda'_ij=c Lambda_ij.                  (2)

Then Lambda' is an ordinary nonnegative certificate for V_(h') with the
complete PSD remainder

 V_(h')+sum Lambda'_ij Q_ij^(h')
       =(t^2/a)[M+kappa(S_Lambda+a Var_h)],               (3)

where

 S_Lambda=(1/2)sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij||g_i-g_j||^2,
 Var_h=sum_(i<n)h_i||g_i||^2+||g_n||^2
                     -(1/C)||sum_(i<n)h_i g_i+g_n||^2.   (4)

Both added forms are PSD, with no missing gradient directions. Every
actual output multiplier is nonnegative because c>0. Zero steps remain
zero, and the proof works on the original formal space without identifying
duplicate gradients or invoking compression. Empty and all-zero schedules
are included.

The actual physical capacity after this same-length conversion is exactly

             C'=1+sum h'_i=2C/[2+theta(C-1)].             (5)

No prefix or bridge is appended. The loss in (5) must be charged before
claiming an improving ordinary seed.

## 2. Two identities before changing the schedule

Put H_g=sum_(i<n)h_i g_i and define the rank-at-most-two quadratic

                    D_h=||H_g+g_n||^2-C^2||g_n||^2.

Direct expansion gives

                    V_h-2J_h=D_h,                       (6)
                    E_theta=a V_h+(1-a)D_h.

Here is an explicit verification of (6). The value terms cancel. Write
x_n-x_i=-sum_(j=i)^(n-1)h_j g_j in J_h. Expanding 2J_h and canceling the
diagonal norm terms in V_h leaves

 2sum_(i<=j<n)h_i h_j<g_i,g_j>-sum_(i<n)h_i^2||g_i||^2
     +2<H_g,g_n>-[s(s+1)+s]||g_n||^2.

The first two terms equal ||H_g||^2, and s(s+1)+s=C^2-1. The displayed
expression is therefore D_h, proving (6).

Also define the formal scalar value combination

                    F_h=sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n).

The value part of E_theta is 2a F_h. Exact cancellation in (1) forces

              sum Lambda_ij(f_i-f_j)=-2a F_h.            (7)

This checks the complete finite-node flow, not only its total sum.

## 3. Complete coefficient identity under contraction

All query differences scale by t, so, as an identity in independent
values and gradients,

 Q_ij^(h')=t Q_ij^h
                +(1-t)[f_i-f_j+||g_i-g_j||^2/2].         (8)

Let K_h=sum_(i<n)h_i||g_i||^2-s||g_n||^2. The value and gradient terms of
V_(h') give exactly

                 V_(h')=t^2 V_h+t(1-t)(2F_h+K_h).       (9)

Using Lambda'=c Lambda, equations (1), (6), (7), and (8) now yield

 V_(h')+sum Lambda'_ij Q_ij^(h')
  =V_(h')+ct[M-aV_h-(1-a)D_h]
                         +c(1-t)[-2aF_h+S_Lambda].      (10)

The parameter identities t=ac, ct=t^2/a, 1-t=kappa t and
1-a=a kappa/C are exact consequences of (2). Substitute (9) into (10):
the V_h coefficients cancel since cta=t^2, and the F_h coefficients cancel
since ca=t. The remaining expression is

 ct M+t(1-t)K_h-ct(1-a)D_h+c(1-t)S_Lambda
   =ct[M+kappa S_Lambda+a kappa(K_h-D_h/C)].              (11)

Finally K_h-D_h/C is precisely Var_h, because C-s=1. Equations (11) and
ct=t^2/a prove the entire formal identity (3).

## 4. Positivity on the full formal space

S_Lambda is a nonnegative sum of squared gradient differences. For Var_h,
assign weights w_i=h_i for i<n and w_n=1, whose sum is C>0. Expanding
the squared differences gives

 Var_h=(1/C)sum_(0<=i<j<=n)w_i w_j||g_i-g_j||^2>=0.     (12)

This remains valid when any h_i vanishes. Since a,t>0 and kappa>=0, (3)
and the assumed full PSD of M prove full PSD of the repaired matrix.
All output interpolation coefficients are positive scalar multiples of
actual nonnegative coefficients, so none of their signs is omitted.
The cancellation of the independent F_h terms in Section 3 explicitly
checks the new value flow. This establishes the statement in Section 1.

For an all-zero schedule, t=a, c=1, Var_h=0, and the input value flow is
zero. Its remainder is M=S_Lambda, so (3) reduces to the same matrix M.
For n=0 every sum of distinct finite pairs is empty and M=0. Thus these
boundary cases satisfy the formula directly.

## 5. Explicit target-relevance cost

Summing the contracted physical steps gives (5). Let K>1 be any desired
capacity threshold for an ordinary seed of this same length. Algebra shows

 C'>K
   iff (2-theta K)C>K(2-theta).                         (13)

Consequently a necessary condition is theta<2/K. When theta<2/K,
condition (13) is equivalent to the explicit input requirement

                    C>K(2-theta)/(2-theta K).            (14)

If theta>=2/K and theta<2, no finite C>=1 can satisfy C'>K: the left side
of (13) is nonpositive and its right side is strictly positive. In
particular, for the previously proposed useful primitive-seed threshold
K=(n+1)^rho, rho=log_2(1+sqrt2), this same-length conversion requires
theta<2/(n+1)^rho and then (14). This is only an exact cost comparison;
the conditional asymptotic amplification argument is not invoked here as
a verified premise, and no seed meeting the threshold has been found.

When theta>0 is fixed and C tends to infinity, C' tends to2/theta. Thus
fixed positive credit cannot yield unbounded ordinary capacity by THIS
conversion. An improving sequence by this mechanism must control theta
at the corresponding inverse-capacity scale. Other conversions with
prefixes or bridges may behave differently and are not excluded.

## 6. A useful corollary about uniform contraction

Given an ordinary nonnegative certificate for h, adding theta h_i to
every incoming-terminal multiplier makes (1) hold with the SAME M for
any 0<=theta<2: those additions cancel -theta J_h. For any prescribed
0<t<=1, choose

                    theta=2(1-t)/(1+t s).

It lies in [0,2), and (2) gives exactly that t. Therefore every uniform
contraction t h of a certified ordinary schedule has the explicit ordinary
certificate (3). This statement concerns certificates on the full formal
space, including zero steps, and does not assert that enlarging steps is
safe or that arbitrary independent contractions preserve the property.

## Remaining issue

This general transformation removes credit by paying an exact physical
sum loss. It neither fixes h nor proves that E_theta and V_h have the same
certificate cone. The same-schedule problem remains open outside the
saturated-cut and weighted-cut repair classes. Any target-level use must
produce a concrete E_theta family surviving (5), an exact ordinary
certificate as above, and the full infinite-horizon benchmark comparison.


Exact audit performed this round: all5 values and15 independent Gram monomials match for the complete h=(1,2,1,1),theta=1/2 certificate, giving h'=h/3 and C'=8/3. Also checked all original independent coefficients with an interior zero-step h=(1,0),theta=3/5 and an all-zero h=(0,0),theta=3/2. All three fixed rational audits passed; no SDP/PEP or verifier call. The tiny exact audit is local_memory/check_credit_contraction.py, results credit_contraction_exact_audit.json.
