# Signed factors, zero steps, and the boundary of the two-sequence certificate

Status: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max2 round38,
the second and final analytical round allocated after completed36 by
TASK.md and master_guidance3acdda5439874057. The completed ceiling is38;
attempted ceiling39 includes the historical interruption. There is no
renewal, formal-verifier allowance, or original-target completion claim.

The original Goal remains OPEN. No numerical evaluation, solver,
machine algebra, replay, installation, consult, subordinate agent, or
fact_submit was used. All mathematics below is a textual joint proof.

The new input class permits arbitrary FINITE REAL factors, including
zeros, and EVERY nonnegative physical step. It imposes no alternating,
palindromic, constant, or monotone word pattern. This removes both
positivity restrictions of the earlier two-sequence program. The exact
original entry signs, flow, and zero matrix remainder are retained.

The resulting obstruction is complete for that declared class. Signed
factors do not supply a new actual seed: after a common sign change and
algebraic isolation of zero-step coordinates, every nontrivial member is
a positive-factor free-word certificate. Its capacity is at most the
basic capacity for its number of positive steps, and is strictly less
than C_r at each assigned physical length r=5 or6. Every fixed integer
ray and every actual primitive-leaf substitution have the wrong direction
for the required improvement. Limits of this class still have A<=C_r.

Two further scope results are proved. First, EVERY original nonnegative
zero-remainder certificate, without any factor restriction, excludes
physical steps in (0,1] and has identically zero multiplier rows and
columns at zero steps. Second, a full explicit three-step certificate,
and the accepted basic maximizing words at lengths5 and6, show that
finite signed two-sequence factorization is NOT lossless even inside
the zero-remainder cone. Two factor pairs suffice for the explicit
three-step array. No obstruction to arbitrary sums of factor pairs,
to the entire zero-remainder cone, or to nonzero PSD remainders follows.

Accepted inputs are fact04219554e41d9750, proof Sections2,10--13, for
the original scalar recurrences, the full certificate identity, physical
joins and arbitrary-length substitution; and fact1252b8823bb5a290 for
the interpolation orientation. The unverified drafts0421d0c1066b8f2d
andc1d41aaebcae37de are awareness only. Every new positive-factor
ingredient used here is rederived within this document, including the
hereditary scalar extremal theorem. No unverified result is a premise.

All local auxiliary symbols have implicit suffix sf38. In particular
U_i,D_i below are factors, not the benchmark U_n^ZJ or a dominant mass.

## 1. Original coordinates and the enlarged declared class

Let r>=1, h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)) with h_i>=0, s=sum h_i, A=1+s,
and t_i=h_i-1. Retain ALL r+1 independent formal gradients g_0,...,g_r
and formal values f_0,...,f_r. At normalized smoothness1 write

 x_i=x_0-sum_(j<i)h_j g_j,
 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 Qbar_ij=-2Q_ji
        =2(f_i-f_j)-2<g_j,x_i-x_j>-||g_i-g_j||^2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)
       +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_r||^2.

For the ORIGINAL array Lambda, require Lambda_ij>=0 for every i!=j
and Lambda_ii=0. Let e be the all-ones column of length r+1 and set

 T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
 L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
 D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r, -s(s+2)).

The required exact flow is

 L_flow^T e=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T.                  (1.1)

The original matrix is

 M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.                (1.2)

The full primitive cone allows M_prim>=0 on ALL R^(r+1). The class
in this document imposes the additional equality M_prim=0. Accepted
fact04219554e41d9750 supplies the polynomial identity

 V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
                  =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.

Consequently, on writing mu_ij=Lambda_ji/2, zero remainder and flow
are equivalent to

                V_h=sum_(i!=j)mu_ij Qbar_ij,  mu>=0.    (1.3)

For the DECLARED TWO-SEQUENCE CLASS choose arbitrary finite real
U_0,...,U_(r-1),D_1,...,D_r. Set U_-1=D_(r+1)=0 and set t_r=0
only as an auxiliary placeholder. For EVERY 0<=i<j<=r define

 mu_ij=(U_i-U_(i-1))(D_j+t_j D_(j+1)),
 mu_ji=(U_(i-1)+t_i U_i)(D_j-D_(j+1)),
 Lambda_ij=2mu_ji, Lambda_ji=2mu_ij.                    (1.4)

There is no deleted off-diagonal pair. Membership requires ALL entries
in(1.4) to be nonnegative and requires(1.3), equivalently(1.1) and
M_prim=0. Factors are not assumed positive, nonzero, or monotone.
In particular, a change of internal factor signs is allowed only when
these actual ORIGINAL entry conditions remain true.

## 2. A necessary lemma for the ENTIRE zero-remainder cone

This section assumes only(1.3), not(1.4). Define auxiliary net flow
and the two incoming sums by

 d_i=sum_(j!=i)(mu_ij-mu_ji),
 I_i=sum_(j<i)mu_ji, J_i=sum_(j>i)mu_ji.

The function coefficients in(1.3) give d_i=h_i for i<r and d_r=-s.
At a nonterminal i the squared-difference terms have coefficient
-d_i-2I_i-2J_i on ||g_i||^2. A trajectory term can contribute to
this diagonal only from Qbar_(j,i) with j>i; their total contribution
is 2h_i J_i. Matching the diagonal of V_h therefore gives

           t_i J_i-I_i=(1-t_i^2)/2,  0<=i<r.            (2.1)

If 0<h_i<=1, then -1<t_i<=0. The left side of(2.1) is nonpositive,
because I_i,J_i>=0, while its right side is strictly positive. This
is impossible. Thus every positive step is strictly greater than1.

If h_i=0, then t_i=-1 and(2.1) gives I_i+J_i=0. Nonnegativity makes
EVERY incoming mu entry at i zero. Since d_i=h_i=0, every outgoing
entry at i is also zero. The corresponding entire original Lambda row
AND column vanish. This is an algebraic assertion on the full array;
it does not impose equality between any two formal gradients or values.

There is also an exact balance restriction. Substitute the actual scalar
unit quadratic f(x)=x^2/2, x_0=1 into(1.3). Its gradients equal its
positions, and every Qbar_ij is zero. Put

 v_0=1, v_(i+1)=(1-h_i)v_i=-t_i v_i.

For these samples the primitive energy is

 V_h=sum_(i<r)h_i(2-h_i)v_i^2-s(s+2)v_r^2
     =sum_(i<r)(v_i^2-v_(i+1)^2)-s(s+2)v_r^2
     =1-A^2 v_r^2.

It must vanish, so

                 A product_(i<r)|t_i|=1.                (2.2)

Here no factorization, irreducibility, or rank assumption has entered.
For positive steps t_i>0; zero steps have |t_i|=1. This necessary
lemma applies to the full ZERO-remainder cone only. It does not extend
to an arbitrary positive semidefinite remainder.

## 3. Finite signed factors collapse to positive factors with zero padding

Assume now(1.4) as well. If s=0, every step is zero; Section2 makes
every multiplier zero, and A=1. Hence suppose s>0.

At the terminal, the total incoming auxiliary weight telescopes to

 I_r=sum_(i<r)mu_ir=U_(r-1)D_r.

The terminal squared-gradient coefficient in sum mu Qbar is s-2I_r.
Matching -sA gives

             U_(r-1)D_r=s(s+2)/2>0.                    (3.1)

Changing both factor sequences to their negatives preserves every
entry in(1.4). Make this change if necessary so that D_r>0. Every
terminal entry then gives

 mu_ir=(U_i-U_(i-1))D_r>=0.

Thus U_-1=0<=U_0<=...<=U_(r-1), with U_(r-1)>0.

Let a be the first index with U_a>0. If i<a, both U_i and U_(i-1)
vanish, so every entry incident to i in(1.4) is zero. Its flow gives
h_i=0. At i=a the entry mu_ar=U_aD_r is strictly positive, so this
row cannot be a zero-step row by Section2. Hence h_a>1 and t_a>0.
For every j>a the actual entry

          mu_ja=t_a U_a(D_j-D_(j+1))>=0

forces D_j>=D_(j+1). Consequently

 D_(a+1)>=D_(a+2)>=...>=D_r>0,
 U_i>=U_a>0 for every i>=a.                             (3.2)

All ACTIVE factors are already positive. Arbitrary D entries preceding
this active suffix cannot affect the array, since all earlier U entries
vanish.

Consider a zero step at i>=a. It has every incident multiplier zero
by Section2. Its terminal edge gives U_i=U_(i-1). Since i>a, the
edge mu_ia=t_aU_a(D_i-D_(i+1)) gives D_i=D_(i+1). A zero step
therefore creates exact plateaus in BOTH active factor sequences.

Let m be the number of positive steps. List their original indices as

 iota(0)<...<iota(m-1),  and set iota(m)=r.

Define the compressed word and factors by

 hhat_j=h_(iota(j)), Uhat_j=U_(iota(j)) for 0<=j<m,
 Dhat_j=D_(iota(j)) for 1<=j<=m,
 Uhat_-1=Dhat_(m+1)=0.

All Uhat and Dhat in their nonboundary ranges are strictly positive.
The U and D plateaus imply that, on every retained ordered pair, the
original formula(1.4) is EXACTLY the same formula for hhat,Uhat,Dhat.
For example U_(iota(j)-1)=Uhat_(j-1), including the first-index zero,
and D_(iota(j)+1)=Dhat_(j+1). The latter identity includes trailing
zero steps and the retained original terminal r.

Every removed row and column was already identically zero. Also its
step is zero, so x_(iota(j)) is the GD position for the compressed word
using its retained formal gradients. Restricting the polynomial identity
to the retained variables proves(1.3) for the compressed word. Conversely
its identity extends to the full original space with zero rows at the
unused independent coordinates. NO quotient relation between gradients
at duplicated positions is used anywhere.

This proves the forward reduction. Its converse is explicit: starting
from any positive-factor word, insert zero steps, copy the neighboring
U and D values across each inserted zero index, put U=0 before the
first active index, and choose the unused leading D entries arbitrarily.
Then every inserted row and column is zero and all retained entries are
unchanged. Thus the enlarged class is exactly the positive-factor
free-word class padded with arbitrary zero steps, apart from irrelevant
factor gauges and unused leading factor values.

## 4. Joint positive-factor capacity proof: coefficient and prefix identities

For this section let the positive compressed length be q>=1, and drop
hats. Thus h_i=1+t_i with t_i>0, U_i,D_j>0 in their nonboundary ranges,
and(1.3)--(1.4) hold with r replaced by q and with U_-1=D_(q+1)=t_q=0.
Nonnegative entries imply

                 U_i>=U_(i-1), D_j>=D_(j+1).             (4.1)

The first implication follows from the terminal entries. The second
follows from mu_(j,0)=t_0U_0(D_j-D_(j+1)). Conversely(4.1) makes
all entries of(1.4) nonnegative when t,U,D are positive.

For i<j, the two telescoping sums are

 sum_(a<=i)mu_(a,j)=U_i(D_j+t_jD_(j+1)),
 sum_(a>j)mu_(a,i)=(U_(i-1)+t_iU_i)D_(j+1).

The coefficient of <g_i,g_j> in sum mu Qbar is

 2(mu_ij+mu_ji)-2h_iU_i(D_j+t_jD_(j+1))
                    +2h_j(U_(i-1)+t_iU_i)D_(j+1),

where h_q=1 is an unused auxiliary value. Expanding(1.4) makes this
coefficient zero for every pair; at j=q its last term is zero.
This is a variable-step identity on the entire formal space.

Put p_0=0 and p_k=U_(k-1)D_k for 1<=k<=q. Here

 I_i=U_(i-1)(D_i+t_iD_(i+1)),
 J_i=(U_(i-1)+t_iU_i)D_(i+1),

with I_0=0. Hence t_iJ_i-I_i=t_i^2p_(i+1)-p_i. Section2 and the
terminal diagonal yield

 t_i^2(2p_(i+1)+1)=2p_i+1,
 2p_q+1=A^2.                                          (4.2)

Define prefix capacity and reciprocal product by

 P_k=1+sum_(i<k)(1+t_i),
 R_k=product_(i<k)t_i^(-1), P_0=R_0=1.

Then

 p_k=(R_k^2-1)/2>0 for 1<=k<=q,
 R_q=P_q=A.                                           (4.3)

In particular every nonempty prefix has R_k>1, even if some individual
excess t_i is greater than1.

Define the normalized weighted prefix and suffix sums

 u_k=(sum_(i<k)h_iU_i)/U_k,
 v_k=(sum_(j=k+2)^q h_(j-1)D_j)/D_(k+1), 0<=k<q.

Outgoing cut weight across {0,...,k} is p_(k+1)(1+v_k):
sum_(j>k)(D_j+t_jD_(j+1))=D_(k+1)+sum_(j>=k+2)h_(j-1)D_j.
Incoming cut weight is p_(k+1)(t_k+u_k). The cut flow is therefore

 p_(k+1)(1-t_k+v_k-u_k)=P_(k+1)-1.                    (4.4)

We prove, for EVERY positive representation,

 u_k=(P_k-R_k)/(1+R_(k+1)),
 v_k=(P_(k+1)-R_(k+1))/(R_(k+1)-1).                    (4.5)

At k=0 the first formula is u_0=0. Inserting t_0=1/R_1 and
p_1=(R_1^2-1)/2 into(4.4) gives the second. For k<q-1 the defining
tail sum is positive, so the formula implies P_(k+1)>R_(k+1).

For induction let 1<=k<q and abbreviate
P=P_k,R=R_k,Y=R_(k+1),z=U_k/U_(k-1)>0. The preceding tail gives
P>R>1. Recurrence and the p products give

 u_k=(u_(k-1)+h_(k-1))/z,
 v_(k-1)=[p_(k+1)/(p_k z)](h_k+v_k).

The induction hypothesis makes

 u_(k-1)+h_(k-1)=(P+R)/(1+R),
 v_(k-1)=(P-R)/(R-1).

By(4.4), v_k=u_k+delta_k, where

 delta_k=2(P+t_k)/(Y^2-1)-1+t_k,
 p_(k+1)(h_k+delta_k)=P+RY.

Elimination gives the quadratic

 (1+R)(P-R)z^2-2(P+RY)z
                  -(Y^2-1)(P+R)/(1+R)=0.               (4.6)

Its roots are

 (P+R)(1+Y)/[(1+R)(P-R)],  (1-Y)/(1+R).

To check them, the discriminant calculation reduces to
(P+RY)^2+(P^2-R^2)(Y^2-1)=(PY+R)^2. The second root is negative,
so the first is forced by z>0. It gives the asserted u formula.
For the v formula use t_kY=R and the identity

 (P-t_kY)(Y-1)+2(P+t_k)+(t_k-1)(Y^2-1)
                      =(Y+1)(P+1+t_k-Y).

This completes the induction, including v_(q-1)=0. Thus

                  1<R_k<P_k,  1<=k<q.                  (4.7)

The forced ratios, useful for auditing every original sign, are

 U_k/U_(k-1)=(P_k+R_k)(1+R_(k+1))
                  /[(1+R_k)(P_k-R_k)],
 D_(k+1)/D_k=(P_k-R_k)(R_(k+1)-1)
                  /[(R_k-1)(P_k+R_k)].                 (4.8)

Together with the positive denominators in(4.7), their exact sign
conditions U_k>=U_(k-1),D_(k+1)<=D_k are equivalently

 t_k>=(P_k-R_k)/(P_k+R_k-2),
 t_k(P_k-R_k-2)<=P_k+R_k.                              (4.9)

The second inequality is deliberately not divided by its possibly
negative coefficient. No equality pattern between different steps has
been assumed. One can independently choose t_0,...,t_(q-2)>0 and set
the last excess to the unique positive root of
t^2+(P_(q-1)+1)t-R_(q-1)=0; this enforces only terminal balance.
Equations(4.7),(4.9) remain necessary sign tests on that free word.

## 5. Every contiguous positive-step interval is product-admissible

For a nonempty interval I=[l,k) in the compressed positive word put

 H_I=sum_(i=l)^(k-1)h_i, K_I=1+H_I,
 q_I=product_(i=l)^(k-1)t_i.

We prove

                  K_I q_I>=1,                           (5.1)

with strict inequality whenever k<q, and equality for the whole word.
For l=0 this is P_k>=R_k, including(4.7).

For 1<=l<k<=q write P=P_l,R=R_l,H=H_I,z=q_I. Let
b_j=(P_j-R_j)/(R_j-1)=v_(j-1), so b_j>=0 and b_q=0. Splitting
the actual weighted tail and using D monotonicity yields

 b_l=sum_(i=l)^(k-1)h_iD_(i+1)/D_l+(D_k/D_l)b_k
                                             <=H+b_k.

Here b_l+1=(P-1)/(R-1), b_k+1=z(P+H-1)/(R-z), and both denominators
are positive because R_l,R_k>1. Multiplication and cancellation give

                    z>=1-H(R-1)/(P-1).                  (5.2)

The endpoint inequality R_k<=P_k is R<=z(P+H). Inserting it into
the decreasing right side of(5.2) gives

 z[P-1+H(P+H)]>=P-1+H.

Since P-1+H(P+H)=(H+1)(P-1+H), this proves z>=1/(1+H).
If k<q the endpoint inequality is strict, giving strictness in(5.1).
This is a theorem about the declared factors, not about every original
zero-remainder array.

## 6. Exact scalar extremum of the hereditary relaxation

For any length q>=1 consider positive t_i, h_i=1+t_i satisfying

 (1+sum_(i in I)h_i) product_(i in I)t_i>=1
                         for EVERY nonempty contiguous I,
 A product_(i<q)t_i=1, A=1+sum_(i<q)h_i.                (6.1)

The empty word has capacity1. Define the ORIGINAL benchmark by

 Omega(a,c)=(a+c+sqrt((a+c)^2+4ac))/2,
 C_0=1, C_q=max_(0<=j<q)Omega(C_j,C_(q-j-1)).             (6.2)

The maximum capacity in(6.1) is exactly C_q. Moreover every maximizer
has a recursive maximizing split at one bridge index. The following
argument proves the statement without importing an unverified premise.

The set is compact when nonempty. Its singleton conditions give
t_i>=c_*=sqrt(2)-1>0. Global equality gives
1=A product_i t_i>=t_j c_*^(q-1), so t_j<=c_*^(1-q). The constraints
are closed on this bounded positive box, and A is continuous.

Fix a maximizer. Write F_I=log(1+sum_I h_i)+sum_I log t_i, and set
F_empty=0. Call an interval tight when F_I=0. Two tight intervals
cannot overlap without containment. Indeed, if their left-only, common,
right-only pieces have masses a>0,b>0,c>0, then

 F_I+F_J-F_(I union J)-F_(I intersection J)
  =log[(1+a+b)(1+b+c)/((1+b)(1+a+b+c))]>0,

because the numerator minus denominator in the ratio is ac>0. Tightness
of I,J and nonnegativity of the other two F values contradict this.
Nor can two nonempty tight intervals be adjacent: their union is an
interval, and
F_I+F_J-F_(I union J)=log[(1+a)(1+c)/(1+a+c)]>0.

The inclusion-maximal proper tight intervals are therefore disjoint,
and every two are separated by at least one uncovered index. At least
one index is uncovered: a single proper interval cannot cover the whole
word, and multiple intervals covering it would include an adjacent pair.

There cannot be two uncovered indices u,v. Perturb t_u to t_u+epsilon
and t_v to t_v-epsilon, with a sufficiently small nonzero epsilon of
sign such that epsilon(t_v-t_u)<=0. Positivity is preserved. The total
capacity stays fixed, every proper tight interval is unchanged, and all
other proper inequalities stay strict by continuity and finiteness.
But the changed two-factor product is

 t_u t_v+epsilon(t_v-t_u)-epsilon^2<t_u t_v.

The global expression A product t_i is now less than1. Increase t_u
alone until this expression returns to1. Such an increase exists because
the expression is continuous, strictly increasing in t_u and unbounded.
All proper interval expressions are nondecreasing under this increase,
so none becomes invalid. The resulting feasible word has strictly larger
capacity, a contradiction.

There is exactly one uncovered index j. All indices to its left form
one proper tight interval, if any; otherwise two maximal intervals on
that side would require another uncovered index between them. The same
holds on the right. Thus the left and right children are saturated
admissible words (possibly empty). If their capacities are a,c and the
bridge excess is d=t_j, global equality says

                         A=a+c+d, Ad=ac.

Hence A=Omega(a,c). By induction and strict monotonicity of Omega,
A<=Omega(C_j,C_(q-j-1))<=C_q. Equality forces both children to be
maximizers as well. This proves the asserted upper bound and structure.

For attainment, join saturated children of capacities a,c with the
positive d solving d(a+c+d)=ac. Intervals inside a child are admissible.
An interval crossing the bridge has a left suffix of capacity x<=a and
a right prefix of capacity y<=c, where an empty portion has capacity1.
Its product is at least d/(xy) and its capacity is x+y+d. Since

 xy/(x+y+d)<=ac/(a+c+d)=d,

it is admissible. Monotonicity in x,y follows from the positive derivatives
y(y+d)/(x+y+d)^2 and x(x+d)/(x+y+d)^2. The whole joined word has
product d/(ac)=1/(a+c+d). Starting with empty children and a maximizing
split in(6.2) constructs capacity C_q. These are the accepted actual
primitive joins as well as feasible points of the scalar relaxation.

## 7. Strictness at the assigned lengths

Sections5--6 give A<=C_q for every positive-factor compressed length q.
If A=C_q, the maximizing structure in Section6 supplies its unique
uncovered index j. A nonempty left child would be a tight proper prefix,
contrary to the strictness in Section5. Thus j=0 and

                         A<=Omega(1,C_(q-1)).            (7.1)

We show this is strictly smaller than C_q at q=5,6 by exact comparisons.
Let lambda=1+sqrt(2), x=C_2. The balanced scalar identities in accepted
fact04219554e41d9750, Section2, give

 C_3=lambda^2, C_4=Omega(lambda,x),
 C_5=lambda x, C_6=Omega(x,lambda^2),
 x^2-(1+lambda)x-lambda=0.

We have 12/5<lambda<17/7 and 4<x<81/20. The lambda comparisons use
(7/5)^2<2<(10/7)^2. The polynomial for x at4 is12-5lambda<0;
at81/20 with lambda replaced by17/7 it is247/2800>0. Since this
polynomial decreases with lambda, these isolate its positive root.

At X=31/4 the polynomial defining Omega(17/7,81/20) has value1/56>0,
so C_4<31/4. Also C_5>48/5. Put g(X)=X(X-1)/(X+1); its derivative
is positive for X>sqrt(2)-1. Direct comparisons give

 g(48/5)=2064/265>31/4,
 4*2064-31*265=41>0.

As Omega(1,c)<X is equivalent to c<g(X) for the positive quantities
here, Omega(1,C_4)<C_5.

For q=6, x>4 and lambda^2=3+2sqrt(2)>29/5. At X=47/4 the polynomial for
Omega(4,29/5) is -23/80<0, so C_6>47/4. Meanwhile C_5<1377/140,
and

 g(47/4)=2021/204>1377/140,
 2021*140-1377*204=2032>0.

Thus Omega(1,C_5)<C_6. Equation(7.1) is impossible at either assigned
length, and every positive-factor member has A<C_5 or A<C_6.

Return to the original signed-factor word of physical length r=5 or6.
If all steps are zero, A=1<C_r. Otherwise let m be its number of
positive steps. Section3 gives A<=C_m. If m=r, the preceding argument
gives A<C_r. If m<r, the original scalar recurrence is strictly increasing:
C_(k+1)>=Omega(1,C_k)>C_k. Again A<=C_m<C_r. Consequently

       EVERY finite real two-sequence member at r=5,6 has A<C_r. (7.2)

At an arbitrary physical length r the same reasoning gives A<=C_m<=C_r.
In particular any coordinatewise limit of words in the declared class,
even with unbounded or differently gauged factors, has A<=C_r by continuity
of the finite sum. Strictness need not persist in that closure. If arrays
also converge, their entry signs, flow and full zero matrix identity
persist by continuity; no bounded-factor representation of the limiting
array has been asserted.

## 8. True lower ray comparisons and the actual physical deficit

For completeness define the original dominant recurrence

 G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2,
 B_0=1, B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Use W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over the
entire original smooth convex class, all finite dimensions and x_0!=x_star,
with updates x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R is unchanged.
Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 proves the positive finite ray limits

 Z_b=lim_(j->infinity)lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1), b>=1 integer,

and the same limits for every fixed nonnegative terminal offset.

The comparison needed for exclusion is the TRUE LOWER comparison

                   Z_((r+1)b)>=C_r Z_b, every b>=1.     (8.1)

To prove it, take a fully expanded maximizing B_n tree. Replace each
primitive empty leaf by a maximizing primitive tree of length r and
capacity C_r, and its one dominant empty leaf by a maximizing dominant
tree of length r and mass B_r. The accepted elementary comparison
B_r>=C_r, together with homogeneity and monotonicity of Omega and G,
gives an original tree with (r+1)(n+1)-1 steps and mass at least C_r B_n.
The original all-split recurrence therefore gives

                  B_((r+1)(n+1)-1)>=C_r B_n.

Putting n=b*2^j-1 and taking the accepted limits proves(8.1). It is
not a comparison to a sufficient upper enclosure.

For a fixed actual signed-factor seed of physical length r=5 or6 put
C=C_r and delta=C/A-1>0. Section7 and(8.1) show, for EVERY integer b>=1,

                   Z_((r+1)b)/(A Z_b)>=1+delta>1.        (8.2)

The initially requested rays remain b=1, with ratios Z_6/Z_1 and Z_7/Z_1.
No switch of ray can repair this seed class.

The actual substitution keeps the original physical zero steps, if any.
Let H_A be obtained by replacing only the n primitive empty leaves of a
fixed fully expanded maximizing B_n tree by this seed and recomputing
the accepted bridges. Its ONE dominant empty leaf stays physically empty.
Write D_A=1+2sum H_A. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750, Sections12--13,
gives

 length(H_A)=(r+1)n, W_((r+1)n)(H_A)=1/D_A,
 [sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1)]^2<=D_A<=A B_n.                (8.3)

This equality is for the unrestricted all-function W: the accepted
full certificate supplies the upper bound and its actual one-dimensional
Huber trajectory attains it. Zero steps do not alter that argument and
do not remove their physical time indices.

Perform the same substitution using a basic primitive word of the SAME
physical length r and capacity C. This yields an original basic dominant
tree of length (r+1)n, with mass D_C, so B_((r+1)n)>=D_C. Strict
monotonicity of the joins and C>A imply D_C>D_A whenever n>=1. Hence

           W_((r+1)n)(H_A)>U_((r+1)n)^ZJ, every n>=1.   (8.4)

For a quantitative fixed-seed deficit the untouched dominant leaf is paid
using the actual lower bound in(8.3) applied at capacity C:

 B_((r+1)n)W_((r+1)n)(H_A)
 >=[sqrt(C B_n)-(sqrt(C)-1)]^2/(A B_n)
 >=(C/A)(1-B_n^(-1/2))^2.                              (8.5)

Set epsilon=1-sqrt((1+delta/2)/(1+delta))>0. Since the accepted benchmark
has B_n>=2n+1, every n>=1 with 2n+1>=epsilon^(-2) satisfies

             W_((r+1)n)(H_A)>=(1+delta/2)U_((r+1)n)^ZJ. (8.6)

The deficit is seed-dependent. No positive uniform gap across all finite
factorized seeds or their closure is claimed. For a limiting actual
certificate from the closure, A<=C_r gives non-strict physical domination
and a limiting ratio at least1, which already excludes strict improvement.

On an input ray n=b*2^j-1, the ACTUAL output horizon is

                 (r+1)(b*2^j-1).

For b=1 these are6*2^j-6 and7*2^j-7. The accepted substitution gives
the exact limit Z_((r+1)b)/(A Z_b). Neither the four-step193/25 threshold
nor its j25 specialization has been transferred to the present lengths.

## 9. Exact counterexample to losslessness of finite signed factorization

This section gives a scope check, not an improving seed. Set

 w=sqrt(2), lambda=1+w, kappa=lambda^2, h=(w,2,w).

Its physical length is3 and its capacity is C_3=kappa. In the original
ordered indexing 0,1,2,3 define the COMPLETE array

 Lambda =
 [ 0       2                0                 0                 ]
 [ 2lambda 0                0                 kappa-1           ]
 [ 0       (kappa-1)w        0                 2kappa+(kappa-1)w  ]
 [ 0       kappa-1           2kappa*lambda      0                 ]. (9.1)

All entries are nonnegative and every diagonal entry is zero. The
original row sums minus column sums are

                    (-2w,-4,-2w,4lambda),              (9.2)

as required because s=2lambda. To check the entries without decimal
evaluation use kappa=2lambda+1 and lambda*w=lambda+1.
At index0 the difference is2-2lambda=-2w. At index1 it is
4lambda-(4lambda+4)=-4. At index2 it is
(8lambda+6)-(10lambda+4)=-2w. The last difference is4lambda.

The complete zero-remainder identity follows from the accepted primitive
join, and can be specified entirely in the original residuals. A single
step w has

                  V_(w)=-2Q_01-2lambda Q_10.

This identity follows by expanding the two displayed residuals, using
(w-1)lambda=1. Join two such children with bridge2. Their capacities
are lambda, and d=1 solves d(2lambda+d)=lambda^2. The accepted exact
join identity is

 V_h=V_left+kappa V_right-(kappa-1)
                    [wQ_21+Q_31+Q_13+wQ_23].           (9.3)

Insert V_left=-2Q_01-2lambda Q_10 and
V_right=-2Q_23-2lambda Q_32. The coefficient of every original ordered
Q is exactly the negative of(9.1); all omitted coefficients are zero.
Thus V_h+sum Lambda Q=0 as an identity in ALL four formal gradients
and values. In particular M_prim=0 on all R^4, not merely on actual
trajectories or a selected subspace.

The array(9.1) cannot have ANY finite real two-sequence representation.
If it did, Section3, since every physical step is positive, would give
strictly positive U_0 and D_3 after one common sign change. But
mu_03=U_0D_3>0 would imply Lambda_30>0, whereas(9.1) has Lambda_30=0.

In fact the same WORD has no such representation with ANY other choice
of the original multipliers. Its first proper prefix has

        (1+h_0)(h_0-1)=(1+w)(w-1)=1.

Section5 forces every proper positive-step prefix to have product strictly
greater than1. This is a contradiction independent of the chosen array.

Here is an explicit two-pair representation of the full array, showing
where a larger factor class begins. Use the SAME t=(w-1,1,w-1) for two
copies of formula(1.4), with

 U^(1)=(1,0,0),          D^(1)=(lambda,0,0),
 U^(2)=(0,1,kappa+1),    D^(2)=(0,lambda,lambda).

Set mu=mu^(1)+mu^(2). The only nonzero entries of the first component
are mu^(1)_01=lambda and mu^(1)_10=1. The only nonzero entries of the
second are

 mu^(2)_12=lambda*w, mu^(2)_13=lambda, mu^(2)_31=lambda,
 mu^(2)_23=kappa*lambda, mu^(2)_32=lambda+kappa+1.

All are positive. Substitution into(1.4) gives precisely these entries:
for example (U^(2)_2-U^(2)_1)D^(2)_3=kappa*lambda and
(U^(2)_1+(w-1)U^(2)_2)D^(2)_3=lambda+kappa+1.
Since lambda*w=lambda+1, Lambda=2mu^T is exactly(9.1).
Neither component separately satisfies the full h flow and diagonal
matching. Therefore the obstruction for individual factorized certificates
does not apply to their sum. This is not a mixture of two feasible
full-word certificates.

There are also counterexamples to losslessness at BOTH assigned lengths.
Take an original maximizing primitive tree of length5 or6. Its leaves
have zero remainder; the accepted primitive join preserves zero remainder
and gives a full nonnegative original array with capacity C_5 or C_6.
Every step is positive. Equation(7.2) prohibits any finite signed
two-sequence representation of a certificate for such a word. These
basic words are not strict-threshold seeds; they witness the genuine
restriction of the factorization inside the original zero-remainder cone.

No statement here excludes a limiting factorization with divergent factors
or claims that two pairs span the whole zero-remainder cone. Section7
already controls the capacity of limits of feasible one-pair words.
The hereditary inequality of Section5 remains UNPROVED for arbitrary
full zero-remainder arrays; the example here satisfies it and therefore
does not refute that separate possible theorem.

## 10. Deliverable, remaining interface, and literature scope

The complete obstruction covers all finite real factors and all nonnegative
words in the specific two-sequence construction, as well as the non-strict
capacity bound on its closure. It includes all30/42 original multipliers
and all6/7 independent gradient coordinates at the assigned lengths.
No altered ray, zero padding, or internal sign choice repairs this class.

The full primitive cone is OPEN. Even the full zero-remainder cone has
not been excluded. A new seed needs a genuinely different array, for
example a sum of factor pairs whose individual flows are not prescribed,
or the nonzero-remainder route owned by max4. The concrete missing
implication for a possible larger obstruction is whether EVERY original
nonnegative zero-remainder array forces

 (1+sum_(i in I)h_i)product_(i in I)(h_i-1)>=1

for every contiguous interval in its positive-step compression. Section2
proves only the step/zero-row constraints and global product balance;
Section5 uses one-pair factorization in its weighted-tail comparison.
No claim that the displayed implication holds, and no uncomputed generic
optimization posed as a solution, is part of this handoff.

Source inspiration remains Salah R. Chikhi, *Last-Iterate Performance of
Gradient Descent and Relaxed Proximal Point via s-Composability*,
arXiv:2609.13765v2, Section4, formulas(10)--(14),
https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13765v2. Its precise local text
downloads/2609.13765v2.txt, Definition1 and Section4 including the proof,
was reread. The source proves the constant-step construction. No theorem
there about variable-step signs, signed factors, hereditary capacity,
or the enlarged class is imported. Both Matlas searches returned unrelated
theorems; web search likewise supplied no additional usable theorem.
The new direction reduces by explicit original-coordinate identities to
the positive-factor case, whose complete proof was rechecked and included.

All results in this document remain UNVERIFIED. The assignment explicitly
forbids fact_submit, so no new accepted fact or verifier verdict is claimed.
This completes the final allocated analytical round only. It is not an
inspected worker-process exit, an allocation renewal, a finalized answer,
or a solution of the original unrestricted exact-profile problem.

Local glossary: r is original physical seed length; h is its nonnegative
word; t_i=h_i-1; s=sum h and A=1+s; U,D are finite real factor sequences;
mu is the auxiliary array with Lambda=2mu^T; d_i is its outgoing-minus-
incoming flow; I_i,J_i are incoming weights from smaller/larger indices;
v_i in Section2 is the unit-quadratic gradient; a in Section3 is the first
positive U index; m is the number of positive steps; iota maps compressed
indices to their original active indices and terminal; hats denote that
algebraic compression. In Sections4--7 q is a positive compressed length,
p_k=U_(k-1)D_k, P_k is prefix capacity, R_k is reciprocal prefix product,
u_k,v_k are the explicitly normalized factor sums, z is a local factor
ratio or interval product as defined, and delta_k is the cut-flow offset.
H_I,K_I,q_I denote interval mass, capacity and product; F_I is their
logarithmic defect; c_*=sqrt(2)-1. Omega,C_n,G,B_n,Z_b,lambda,W_n and
U_n^ZJ have the accepted original benchmark meanings. H_A,D_A,D_C are
the actual substituted word and its two mass comparisons; C=C_r,
delta=C/A-1,epsilon is the explicit fixed-seed deficit threshold.
In Section9 w=sqrt(2),kappa=lambda^2 and the superscripts(1),(2) index
the two explicitly specified factor pairs.

# Max49: three actual faces and a remote-forced return

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact submission
is authorized. This is the final of the two sessions allocated after
completed47, with completed ceiling49 and attempted ceiling50. The
original all-n/all-nonnegative-word exact-profile goal remains OPEN.

## 1. Result and scope

The construction below changes gradients at the actual queries inside
the original H2 blocks. In one fixed planar triangle its exact sequence
of projection locations is

    original indices 0,1,2,3: the vertical edge,
    index4: upper sloping edge,
    index5: lower sloping edge,
    indices6,7: quadratic core,
    index8: vertical edge again,
    every index from9 onward: quadratic core.

The lower-edge transition at5 happens INSIDE the second physical H2.
The return at8 is forced by a remote original interpolation row. In
particular, observing core entry at6 does not justify a quadratic tail.
Every gradient and value is given by finite formulas; the proof checks
the actual projections against the whole fixed triangle and the optimum.

The result holds uniformly for every integer m>=1000000 and every real
aspect t>=1000. These cutoffs are conservative symbolic enclosures, not
the outcome of a fit or a computation. The aspect may depend arbitrarily
on the entire word and on m. Every child uses the SAME enclosing-m
parameters. There is one static function for each (m,t).

This explicit family is legal but too weak. Its true-distance payoff
V49 obeys

    0<V49<2 Qunit,
    V49<Huber/10<=K_n/10<=F_ray_n/10,
    V49<(sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z))/10,
    0<B_n V49<10(25/48)^m ->0.

All comparisons use the same original physical word. D_h is the FULL
schedule-dependent complex domain, not a lens or a short-cycle screen.
The benchmark B_n is the ORIGINAL all-split recurrence. The bounds
concern this family, not the unrestricted W_n(h).

Two precise forbidden continuations are also proved below. Continuing
the lower affine face at6 fails original row Q_(0,6), even if its new
value is free but all retained values remain fixed. Continuing the
quadratic core through8 fails Q_(8,0); the optimum rows force its value,
so a value repair cannot save that prescribed gradient.

## 2. Correctness inputs and literature use

The accepted inputs, in their stated scopes, are:

* 1252b8823bb5a290: every original directed smooth-convex interpolation
  row and both optimum directions are necessary. Its Q convention is
  used throughout.
* b98bb807d514e80d: full K_n and the original all-split estimate
  B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3).
* 1fca3e5dd555cb4a: full alternating-ray maximum F_ray_n, including
  every feasible checkpoint set, with K_n<=F_ray_n.
* dfde2fa326c908bf: full D_h, maximal interpolating Gap_h, and the
  legal real parameters used in the complete complex comparison.

Accepted e9a55f57461f253c is only an actual H6 result with epsilon1/6,
dimension66 and a unique minimizer. No new-m transfer or radius claim
is inferred from its dimension. The previously saved H5/H6 observations
motivate changing within-block gradients only. None is recomputed here.

Unverified max48, finding ec0b4a7f59979259, identifies a useful free-aspect
interface, but is NOT a correctness input. Every needed formula and
argument is reproduced here. The older constant-H2 T1/T2 laws are not
retuned or used as premises.

Matlas returned Lemma S17 of arXiv:2107.07257 and Theorem4/Lemma3 of Jai
Moondra, Hassan Mortagy and Swati Gupta, "Reusing Combinatorial Structure:
Faster Iterative Projections over Submodular Base Polytopes,"
arXiv:2106.11943v3 (2023). The exact PDF already exists as
downloads/2106.11943.pdf. The general-polytope definitions, Section3,
Lemma1, Theorem4 and its proof were read in the
[arXiv HTML](https://arxiv.org/html/2106.11943v3). The useful
distinction is between membership in an affine face and the sign of its
normal residual. Here these conditions are proved directly. No external
theorem is a proof premise, and no submodular-polytope assumption is
imposed on the triangle.

## 3. Original word and explicit scalar definitions

Fix m>=1000000, t>=1000 and define

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/m, beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, ell=1+a, b=beta c,
    A=4+2a+beta, eta=a^2 beta, k=A/(b eta)=A/(a beta^3),
    s=(b+1)/(k-1), D=b+s,
    rho=(a k+1)/(k-1), e=b rho-s,
    N=(A-1)s, C=(A-ell)s, p=ab=beta^2,
    delta=t^(-2), omega=epsilon+delta,
    u=N/(1+delta).
                                                               (1)

All symbols in this draft are local aliases; none renames a project
benchmark or reference. In shared records the new objects carry the
suffix49. In particular C in(1) is a scalar, while the convex triangle
below is denoted T49. The letter e in(1) is a positive edge intercept,
not the exponential constant, and t is an aspect, not a time index.

Define the exact further coefficients, in the following order:

    x5=p-ell u,       v5=C-ell u,
    d5=-(rho x5+v5+e),
    E5=d5/(rho^2+delta),
    j5=x5+rho E5,    w5=v5+delta E5,
    x6=x5-(1+beta)j5, v6=v5-(1+beta)w5,
    gamma=beta^2 c,
    x8=gamma x6,     v8=gamma v6,
    x9=x8-ell,       v9=-a v8.
                                                               (2)

These are finite explicit rational expressions in lambda, m and t.
Their denominators are positive by the bounds in Section4. There is
no unknown Gram matrix, optimization oracle, implicit trajectory solve,
or root-selection problem in these definitions.

Use the three finite vectors

    U=(1,t(D+1)), V=(1,-a t(D+1)), Lvert=(-b,s t),
    T49=conv{U,V,Lvert},
    F49(X)=max_(g in T49) {<X,g>-||g||^2/2},
    X0=(A,t(D+1)).
                                                               (3)

Here Lvert is a vertex label, not the smoothness constant. All vector
inner products and distances are Euclidean. The formula specifies one
static function; its actual gradients are proved below rather than
assumed from the envelope definition.

## 4. Uniform finite-parameter enclosures

The auxiliary coefficients with final subscript0 are algebraic reference
values at epsilon=delta=0. They are NOT substituted for the actual word.
Put

    a0=lambda-2=1/lambda, ell0=lambda-1,
    A0=lambda^2, k0=lambda^3,
    s0=1/(2lambda-1), D0=lambda+s0, rho0=2s0, e0=1,
    z0=lambda s0, T0=lambda^2 s0=1+2s0,
    N0=1+s0, C0=(A0-ell0)s0,
    x50=z0-ell0, v50=-z0,
    d50=(3-lambda)s0^2, E50=(3-lambda)/4,
    j50=-ell0/2, w50=-z0,
    x60=v60=z0, x80=v80=T0,
    x90=a0 s0=1/(3lambda+2), v90=-z0.
                                                               (4)

These identities follow from lambda^2=2lambda+1. For example
(lambda^2-2)(lambda+1)=lambda^3-1, which gives s0; then
(2lambda-1)s0=1 gives rho0=2s0, e0=1, N0=1+s0 and T0=1+2s0.
Also (lambda-1)(2lambda-1)=lambda+3. This last equality gives
x50=z0-ell0, d50=(3-lambda)s0^2, j50=-ell0/2 and x90=a0 s0
by substituting successively into(1)--(2).

Squaring rational endpoints gives

    12/5<lambda<29/12<5/2,
    6/23<s0<5/19,
    29/46<z0<12/19,
    35/23<T0<29/19,
    4/37<x90<5/46,
    1/26<d50<1/24.
                                                               (5)

For z0, the function q/(2q-1) decreases for q>1/2, giving the
displayed endpoints. For d50, the respective product bounds are
21/529 and15/361; they lie strictly between1/26 and1/24.

Here epsilon,delta<=10^(-6) and omega<=2*10^(-6). The basic differences
are bounded as follows:

    |a-a0|<epsilon, |b-lambda|<epsilon, |A-A0|<3epsilon,
    |k-k0|<120epsilon, |s-s0|<4epsilon,
    |D-D0|<5epsilon, |rho-rho0|<3epsilon, |e-1|<13epsilon,
    |N-N0|<22epsilon, |C-C0|<20epsilon.
                                                               (6)

Here are details ensuring that(6) is a finite, uniform estimate.
The exact identities a=a0(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon) and
b=lambda(1-epsilon^2) prove its first bounds. Since
|beta^3-1|<=3epsilon, one has

    |a beta^3-a0|<(5/2)epsilon,  a beta^3>3/8.

The quotient difference for k is at most
(3+15*(5/2))epsilon/(3/8)=108epsilon<120epsilon, because k0<15.
It also gives13<k<15. Thus k-1>12 and k0-1>12. The quotient for s
is bounded by [1+(1/3)120]epsilon/12<4epsilon.
Using rho=a+(a+1)/(k-1), the difference for rho is bounded by

    epsilon+epsilon/12+(3/2)*120epsilon/144<3epsilon.

In particular s<1/3 and rho<3/5. The difference for e=b rho-s
is below [1+(5/2)*3+4]epsilon<13epsilon. For N, use A0-1<5:
its difference is below 3epsilon/3+5*4epsilon=21epsilon.
For C, use A0-ell0=lambda+2<9/2:
its difference is below4epsilon/3+(9/2)*4epsilon<20epsilon.
These arguments also give the useful strict bounds

    2/5<a<1/2, 12/5<c<5/2, 9/10<beta<1,
    1/4<s<1/3, 1/2<rho<3/5, 9/10<e<11/10,
    D>5/2, 18/5<D+1<15/4, N<4/3,
    0<A<6, b>2, A-1>4.
                                                               (7)

For example D0>12/5+6/23>5/2, and the error5epsilon preserves
this bound. The sharper lower bound D0+1>12/5+6/23+1>18/5
proves the stated lower bound on D+1. All margins in(7) exceed
the corresponding error in(6).

Since u=N/(1+delta),

    |u-N0|<22epsilon+(4/3)delta,
    |ell u-ell0 N0|<35epsilon+2delta.

It follows from |p-1|<2epsilon and(6) that

    |x5-x50|<40omega, |v5-v50|<60omega.
                                                               (8)

In particular |x5|<1. In the d5 expression in(2), use rho0<3/5
to get

    |d5-d50|<3epsilon+(3/5)40omega+60omega+13epsilon
             <=100omega.
                                                               (9)

Consequently

    1/27<d5<1/23.
                                                               (10)

Indeed the larger error allowed in(9) is1/5000, and
1/26-1/5000>1/27 while1/24+1/5000<1/23.
Also rho^2+delta>1/4 and
|rho^2+delta-rho0^2|<4epsilon+delta. The quotient difference gives

    |E5-E50|<4[100omega+(3/20)(4epsilon+delta)]<410omega.

Since E50<3/20, this implies0<E5<1/6. Substitution into(2) now yields

    |j5-j50|<300omega, |w5-w50|<61omega,
    |x6-z0|<650omega, |v6-z0|<190omega.

For the first pair, the respective bounds before rounding are

For x6 and v6 use1+beta=2-epsilon, |j5|<3/4 and |w5|<2/3;
the bounds are40omega+2*300omega+(3/4)epsilon and

Finally |gamma-lambda|<5epsilon, since
beta^2(1+epsilon)=1-epsilon-epsilon^2+epsilon^3. With(11),

    |x8-T0|<1700omega, |v8-T0|<500omega,
    |x9-x90|<1701omega, |v9+z0|<252omega.

For x8 the bound is(2/3)5epsilon+(5/2)650omega;
for v8 replace650 by190. For v9 use v8<8/5 and a0<1/2.
Equations(5),(8)--(12) prove the following margins, which will be
used to select the actual faces:

    -3/4<j5<-2/3, -2/3<w5<-3/5,
    3/5<x6<2/3, 3/5<v6<2/3,
    3/2<x8<8/5, 3/2<v8<8/5,
    1/10<x9<3/25, -2/3<v9<-3/5.
                                                               (13)

For the narrowest x9 margin, its error is at most1701/500000,
and4/37-1701/500000>1/10, while5/46+1701/500000<3/25.
For x8, 35/23-1700/500000>3/2 and
29/19+1700/500000<8/5. The other intervals follow using the
smaller errors in(11)--(12) and29/46<z0<12/19.
Thus(13) does not rely on a qualitative continuity assertion.

## 5. The fixed function and the actual first block

The identities D+1=ks and e+rho=a(D+1) show that T49 is exactly
the intersection of the three halfspaces

    X<=1,
    -t X+Y<=t D,
    -rho t X-Y<=t e.
                                                               (14)

The upper line contains U,Lvert, the lower contains V,Lvert,
and the vertical line contains U,V. The other vertex in each case
is strictly on the interior side. All three inequalities are strict
at0 because D,e>0. Thus T49 has nonempty interior containing0.

For any X the maximum in(3) exists and is unique, by compactness of
T49 and strict concavity of the objective in g. Completing the square
shows that its maximizer is the Euclidean projection of X. A vector
g in T49 is that projection if and only if

    <X-g,v-g><=0 for EVERY v in T49.
                                                               (15)

Necessity follows by differentiating the squared distance from X along
each segment from g to v; sufficiency follows by expanding the squared
distance. Adding(15) at two inputs proves
||g(X)-g(Z)||^2<=<g(X)-g(Z),X-Z>, hence g is1-Lipschitz.
The two maximizing-objective inequalities give

    0<=F49(X+d)-F49(X)-<g(X),d><=||d||^2.

This proves differentiability with gradient g, as well as convexity
and global1-smoothness. Because0 is an interior point of T49, the
same formula in(3) is strictly positive at every nonzero X: take a
sufficiently small positive multiple of X as its feasible g. Therefore

    min F49=0, argmin F49={0},
    R49^2:=dist(X0,argmin F49)^2=A^2+t^2(D+1)^2.
                                                               (16)

For the first actual H2, put

    (P0,P1,P2,P3)=(0,ell,ell+1+beta,A-1),
    (T0',T1',T2',T3')=(1,-a,a beta,-eta).

The prime distinguishes these four tangent coefficients from T0 in(4).
For0<=i<=3 define

    Xi=(A-Pi, t(D+1)Ti'),
    Gi=(1,t(D+1)Ti'),
    Yi=Xi-Gi=(A-1-Pi,0),
    fi=||Gi||^2/2+(A-1-Pi).
                                                               (17)

Each Ti' lies in[-a,1], so Gi lies on UV. Each Yi is a nonnegative
multiple of the outward normal of the first inequality in(14).
Thus(15) holds against EVERY vertex and Gi is the actual gradient.
The tangent relation Ti+1'=(1-h_i)Ti' and the partial sums Pi prove
each of the three original updates separately. Because a,beta>0 and
eta<a, all these gradients are distinct at consecutive queries.

At index3 the residual is zero. Using D+1=ks and b eta k=A gives

    X3=G3=(1,-A s t/b),
    X4=-b X3=(-b,A s t).
                                                               (18)

## 6. Upper edge4, lower edge5, and the return at8

The upper normal is n_plus=(-t,1). The difference between its
inner product at X4 and its supporting value tD is Nt. Subtracting
the corresponding normal component gives exactly

    G4=(-b+u,t(s+u)),
    Y4=X4-G4=(Nt/(1+t^2)) n_plus,
    f4=<X4,G4>-||G4||^2/2.
                                                               (19)

This is a point in the RELATIVE INTERIOR of Lvert U. Its horizontal
increment u is positive and smaller than N<4/3<b+1, and it lies
on the upper supporting line. The residual in(19) has positive
coefficient. Hence(14)--(15) check the remote vertex V and the
optimum as well as the two edge vertices. G4 is the actual gradient.
The physical step h4=ell gives

    X5=(x5,t v5).

Its violation of the LOWER supporting inequality is exactly t d5>0.
The lower normal is n_minus=(-rho t,-1). Its true projected candidate is

    G5=(j5,t w5),
    Y5=X5-G5=(-rho E5,-delta t E5)
                 =(t d5/(1+rho^2 t^2)) n_minus,
    f5=<X5,G5>-||G5||^2/2.
                                                               (20)

The definitions in(2) give rho j5+w5+e=0. Also -b<j5<1 by(7),(13).
Thus G5 lies in the relative interior of Lvert V, rather than being
an endpoint or an affine projection outside the segment. The positive
normal coefficient and(14) verify(15) against the REMOTE U as well.
This is the second genuine face transition, at an internal query of
the second original H2 block.

The next original step h5=1+beta gives

    X6=(x6,t v6).

By(13), x6<1, v6-x6<1/15<D, and rho x6+v6+e>0. Therefore
X6 is in the interior of T49, and G6=X6, f6=||X6||^2/2.
The original step h6=ell gives

    X7=-a X6.

For this point its horizontal coordinate is negative, and its upper
line expression divided by t is a(x6-v6)<1/3<D. Its lower slack,
also divided by t, is

    e-a(rho x6+v6)>9/10-(1/2)[(3/5)(2/3)+2/3]>0.

Thus G7=X7 and f7=||X7||^2/2. The physical bridge at index7 is
1+beta c^2. Consequently

    X8=-(beta c^2)X7=gamma X6=(x8,t v8).
                                                               (21)

Its horizontal coordinate is greater than3/2. Its projection is
NOT itself. Since3/2<v8<8/5<D+1 and the bottom endpoint of UV
has negative vertical coordinate, the exact projection is

    G8=(1,t v8), Y8=(x8-1,0),
    f8=<X8,G8>-||G8||^2/2.
                                                               (22)

It lies in the relative interior of UV, and the positive horizontal
residual proves(15) for the entire triangle, including Lvert and0.
The next original step h8=ell now gives

    X9=(x9,t v9).
                                                               (23)

In the second H2, G4 has positive vertical coordinate, G5 negative,
G6 positive, and G7 negative. The gradients vary at EVERY one of its
three actual updates. No constant-block compression has been used.

## 7. Permanent core from9 with every physical query retained

Both endpoints X9 and -c X9 lie in the strict interior of T49.
For X9, the right and upper inequalities in(14) follow from(13),
and its lower slack divided by t is greater than e-2/3>0.
For -c X9, the right inequality holds because its horizontal coordinate
is negative. Its upper expression divided by t satisfies

    c(x9-v9)<(5/2)(3/25+2/3)=59/30<D.

Its lower slack is

    e+c(-rho x9-v9)>e+c[3/5-(3/5)(3/25)]>0.

By convexity,

    [-c X9,X9] subset interior(T49).
                                                               (24)

Here [P,Q] denotes the closed Euclidean segment, including its endpoints.
For a positive integer j let nu2(j) be the exponent of2 dividing j,
and for a nonnegative integer i let s2(i) be the number of binary1
digits in i, with s2(0)=0. The original word recursion gives

    h_i=1+beta c^(nu2(i+1)-1), 0<=i<n.

Indeed the central position in each word has the stated valuation,
and adding its power of2 to a right-child index preserves the smaller
valuation of that child index. Induction on the recursion proves the
formula. Counting multiples of2^j gives

    sum_(j=1)^i nu2(j)=sum_(r>=1)floor(i/2^r)=i-s2(i).

The last equality follows by writing i in binary and summing the
contribution 2^r-1 of each binary1 digit. Thus the EXACT prefix product
on the original physical word is

    Pprod_i:=product_(j<i)(1-h_j)
             =(-1)^i beta^i c^(-s2(i)).
                                                               (25)

For EVERY9<=i<=n define

    sigma_i=(-1)^(i-9) beta^(i-9)c^(2-s2(i)),
    Xi=Gi=sigma_i X9, fi=||Xi||^2/2, Yi=0.
                                                               (26)

For odd i>=9, s2(i)>=2, so0<sigma_i<=1. For even i>=10,
s2(i)>=1, so-c<=sigma_i<0. Hence(24) proves every Xi in(26)
lies in T49 and is its own actual projection. Equation(25) proves
Xi+1=(1-h_i)Xi for every remaining physical step. This is induction
from the already established X9, not an assumed quadratic suffix.
All factors are nonzero, so the gradients continue to change sign at
every physical query, including all later original H2 blocks.

## 8. Every remote and optimum interpolation row

At every finite sample use the single value

    fi=<Xi,Gi>-||Gi||^2/2.

This agrees with(17),(19),(20),(22),(26) and the core values at6,7.
Put X_star=G_star=0, f_star=0, Y_star=0. Direct expansion of the
accepted convention gives, for every distinct pair of indices,

    Q_ij:=fi-fj+<Gi,Xj-Xi>+||Gi-Gj||^2/2
         =<Gi-Gj,Yj>.
                                                               (27)

Every Gi is in the SAME triangle. For j=0,1,2,3 and8, Yj is a
nonnegative right normal; for j=4 it is a positive upper normal;
for j=5 it is a positive lower normal. Equations(14) and(19)--(22)
therefore make(27) nonpositive for EVERY possible source i, including
all remote samples and the optimum. For j=6,7 and j>=9 it is zero.
For j=star it is also zero. Since G_star is in the triangle, the
reverse optimum inequalities are included too. In particular no
incoming or outgoing terminal row, earlier remote row, or original
query has been omitted.

Together with Sections5--7 this supplies a full static interpolant,
its true minimum set, all original points, gradients, values and
normal conditions. Dimension2 and the paid radius in(16) are separate
quantities. Orthogonal embedding preserves the same closest distance:
the minimizer set in the larger space is the orthogonal complement
of the original plane.

## 9. Two exact original-row obstructions

### 9.1 Continuing the lower affine face through query6

Retain all actual points, gradients and values through5. Define the
closest point to0 on the lower supporting LINE by

    q=(e t/(1+rho^2 t^2)) n_minus,
    w=G5-q.

Then q is perpendicular to w, and(20) gives

    X5=(1+d5/e)q+w,
    X6=(d5/e-beta)q-beta w.

The affine-line continuation would prescribe

    G6_aff=q-beta w,
    Y6_aff=[d5/e-(1+beta)]q.
                                                               (28)

By(7),(10), the scalar in square brackets is negative. Using the
natural projection-form value at6, direct expansion(27) gives

    Q_(0,6_aff)
       =(1+beta-d5/e) e ell(D+1)t^2/(1+rho^2 t^2)>20.
                                                               (29)

To see this exact numerator, U=G0 and

    <q,U-q>=-e ell(D+1)t^2/(1+rho^2 t^2).

This follows from <n_minus,U>-e t=-ell(D+1)t, using
e+rho=a(D+1). The candidate G6_aff lies on the same supporting
line as q, so <q,G6_aff>=||q||^2. Substitution into(28) proves
the equality in(29). For its quantitative bound, d5/e<10/207<1/20,
1+beta>19/10, ell>7/5, D+1>18/5, e>9/10, and
1/(rho^2+delta)>5/2. Their product is greater than
(37/20)(9/10)(7/5)(18/5)(5/2)=209790/10000>20.

Allowing a free value f6 cannot repair this prescribed gradient while
the prior value f5 is retained. In fact

    G5-G6_aff=(1+beta)w,
    X5-X6=(1+beta)G5,
    <G5-G6_aff,X5-X6>=||G5-G6_aff||^2.

Therefore Q_(5,6_aff)+Q_(6_aff,5)=0 independently of f6. If both
rows were nonpositive, both would be equalities and they would uniquely
force f6. The projection-form value used in(29) makes both equalities
hold, since both normals are perpendicular to w. Thus it is the forced
value and the original remote row(0,6) remains positive. This rejects
only this same-line continuation with the specified retained jets;
it does not reject arbitrary new gradients or a new whole transcript.

### 9.2 Declaring a permanent core at query6

Retain the ACTUAL construction through7, including its optimum and
initial jet. The physical query X8 is fixed by(21). The false quadratic
continuation would set G8_core=X8. The two optimum rows in accepted
1252b8823bb5a290 force f8_core=||X8||^2/2. Since Y0=(A-1,0),

    Q_(8_core,0)=(A-1)(x8-1)>(A-1)/2>2.
                                                               (30)

Thus even with free new values the stated core continuation is
impossible for ANY smooth convex interpolant retaining these earlier
jets and optimum. The actual projection(22) repairs this row by
returning to the old vertical face. Both source and target in(30)
are original physical queries, not added support points or compressed
block endpoints.

## 10. Exact true-distance payoff

Since n=2^m-1 is odd and s2(n)=m, formulas(16),(26) yield the exact
normalized payoff of this actual function:

    V49(m,t):=2(F49(Xn)-min F49)/dist(X0,argmin F49)^2
      =beta^(2(n-9)) c^(4-2m)
             (x9^2+t^2 v9^2)/(A^2+t^2(D+1)^2)>0.
                                                               (31)

Let Qunit=Pprod_n^2=beta^(2n)c^(-2m). This is the scalar unit
quadratic's exact payoff on the SAME word. Using t>=1 and(7),(13),

    (x9^2+t^2 v9^2)/(A^2+t^2(D+1)^2)
       <[(3/25)^2+(2/3)^2]/(18/5)^2
        =2581/72900<1/25.

Bernoulli's inequality beta^18>=(1-18/m)>=9/10 follows by induction
from (1-v)^r>=1-rv for0<=v<=1. Thus

    V49/Qunit<beta^(-18)c^4/25
              <(10/9)(5/2)^4/25=125/72<2.
                                                               (32)

The bound by2 Qunit has a precise large-aspect limit. Fix any allowed
m and let t tend to infinity, so delta tends to0 while the enclosing
word stays fixed. All coefficients in(1)--(2) remain finite and have
limits. At4 the projected vertical coefficient tends to A s; at5
it tends to -a A s because delta E5 tends to0. Hence successive
vertical coefficients give

    lim_(t->infinity) v6=a beta A s,
    lim_(t->infinity) v8=beta^4 A s,
    lim_(t->infinity) v9=-a beta^4 A s
                       =(D+1)Pprod_9.

The last identity uses D+1=k s=A s/(a beta^3) and
Pprod_9=-beta^9/c^2. Divide numerator and denominator of the radius
ratio in(31) by t^2. Since x9 and A remain bounded, it follows that

    lim_(t->infinity) V49(m,t)/Qunit(m)=1.                (32a)

Thus arbitrarily increasing the aspect does not improve its asymptotic
scale, even though the same three-face pattern remains present at
every finite allowed t. No claim that V49<=Qunit at every finite t
is needed or asserted.

The payoff is paid at the closest actual minimizer by(16). For any
positive smoothness constant L_s, replace F49 by L_s F49 and use
steps h_i/L_s. All positions, closest distances and normalized payoffs
stay the same. The convention is W=2R throughout.

## 11. Complete same-word references and original B_n

Put S=sum H_m and M=1+S. The exact multiplicities of the recursively
defined steps give

    M=2^m+(beta/c)(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)
       =(1-zeta)2^m+zeta c^m,
    zeta=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).
                                                               (33)

Indeed summing the unit portions gives2^m-1, and summing the excesses
gives (beta/c)sum_(j=0)^(m-1)2^(m-1-j)c^j. The geometric sum is
the displayed quotient. Since c>lambda>2 and lambda(lambda-2)=1,
one has c(c-2)>1>beta. Consequently M<c^m.

The COMPLETE reference from accepted b98bb807d514e80d is

    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S),
      max_(0<=j<n) (h_j-1)_+^2/
         [(1+sum_(i<j)h_i)^2(1+2sum_(i>j)h_i)]}.

Define Huber=1/(1+2S)=1/(2M-1). The complete F_ray_n of accepted
1fca3e5dd555cb4a maximizes over ALL feasible chronological checkpoint
sets, retaining the offsets and accumulated value areas specified
there; in particular its zero-cut option is Huber and its one-cut
options are all the displayed K terms. Thus Huber<=K_n<=F_ray_n,
with no checkpoint choice suppressed.

Using(32)--(33), beta<1 and c>2,

    V49/Huber=V49(2M-1)<4 c^(-m)<1/10.
                                                               (34)

The final strict inequality already follows for m>=6 because2^6>40;
our stated domain has m>=1000000. This proves the full K/ray comparison.
It uses a lower bound on each complete reference, rather than replacing
the reference by a chosen checkpoint.

For completeness, the FULL complex reference in accepted
dfde2fa326c908bf uses Poly_i(z)=product_(j<i)(1-h_j z), the real-plane
points xi_i=Poly_i(z), gradients gamma_i=z Poly_i(z), and the optimum0.
For every directed simple cycle C on{star,0,...,n}, it imposes

    (Re(z)-|z|^2) Energy_C>=2|Im(z) Area_C|,
    Energy_C=sum_(j->i in C)|xi_i-xi_j|^2,
    Area_C=sum_(j->i in C)Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i).

D_h is the set satisfying ALL these cycles. Gap_h(z) is the maximal
normalized terminal objective among interpolants of these specified
points and gradients with f(0)=0, using initial point1 and denominator1.
We compare with the entire supremum over D_h.

Take the real parameter theta=1/(2M). It is in[0,1], so all cycle
inequalities hold: their right sides vanish and their left coefficients
are nonnegative. The explicit convex1-smooth scalar quadratic
f_theta(x)=theta x^2/2 produces the exact specified real polynomial
points and gradients. It can be embedded in the real plane by making
the function independent of the second coordinate. Its minimum is0
and its initial squared distance is1. Therefore the maximality in the
accepted fact implies

    Gap_h(theta)>=theta Poly_n(theta)^2.

All factors are positive and
sum_j theta h_j=S/(2M)<1/2. Induction on the factors, using
(1-u)(1-v)>=1-u-v for u,v>=0, proves

    Poly_n(theta)>=1-theta S>1/2,
    sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z)>1/(8M)>1/(8c^m).
                                                               (35)

Together with(32),

    V49/[sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z)]<16 c^(-m)<1/10.
                                                               (36)

The final inequality follows for m>=8 because2^8>160. This is a
comparison to the FULL unaugmented complex channel, not only its
universal lens. It also dominates V49 after any Huber augmentation.

Finally retain the ORIGINAL all-split recurrences. In the notation of
accepted b98bb807d514e80d they are Cbench_0=B_0=1 and

    Cbench_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(Cbench_j,Cbench_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) Gjoin(Cbench_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    Gjoin(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2.

Cbench is only a local alias for the accepted primitive mass. Using
its proved bound B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3)<5*3^m at n=2^m-1, and(32),

    0<B_n V49<10(3/c^2)^m<10(25/48)^m ->0.
                                                               (37)

This closes the entire stated large-aspect family uniformly, even if
t grows arbitrarily fast with m. It is not an upper bound on W_n(h),
and does not transfer the finite accepted H6 result to another word.

## 12. Exact remaining boundaries

The first new conclusion is a complete legal three-face construction
for every stated(m,t), with changing gradients inside physical H2
blocks, every ordered original row, and true minimum/radius accounting.
It enters the core permanently only AFTER the remote-forced return8.
Its payoff is quantitatively below every complete reference above.

The two row obstructions(29)--(30) retain specific earlier jets and
the optimum. They close exactly the corresponding proposed next
gradients, including arbitrary reassigned new values. They do not
exclude whole-transcript redesign, different extra vertices, conjugate
heights, a moved initial state, or a changing closest-minimum state.

Together with max48 as awareness only, the two drafts identify a
balanced-aspect failure and the present large-aspect failure. This
joint draft alone does NOT close the intermediate/small aspect region,
all triangles, all polytopes, or all changing-gradient laws. It does
not assert optimality of its phase partition outside its stated domain.

A stronger family on these H_m words would still leave the original
all-n/all-nonnegative-word lower comparison BLOCKED. That is a
theorem-level requirement, not a routine induction. No actual seed
for the separate verified upper-lift interface is supplied either.

Resource record: zero new host mathematical evaluations, numerical
solves, DP, trajectory runs, proof replays, machine algebra, formal
verifier calls, fact_submit calls, installations, consults or native
subagents. Literature retrieval, file reads and textual persistence
only. All new mathematics remains UNVERIFIED. No further round or
formal gate is renewed by this handoff.

# max65: completed full-objective discovery for the exact small-tau hierarchy

**Status: numerical discovery complete; no new fact; original minimax goal OPEN.**
All six authorized jobs completed, with five solver invocations and one
independent solver-free readback. The canonical ledger is `closed_exhausted`:
180 accounted seconds, **49.26249355098116 measured external child seconds**,
zero remaining jobs. No correction, timeout, failed mathematical job,
installation, external service, subagent, new benchmark DP, or formal
submission was used. The reservation guard1131->1311 is not measured runtime.

The finite full-PEP results are numerically Huber-like on all four specified
words. None of these solves supplies a stronger numerical objective than
the same-word Huber witness. This batch did not find the extra hard geometry
sought by the assignment. This is a scoped discovery outcome, not proof of
Huber optimality, a worst-case upper bound, or a conclusion about large m.

## 1. Exact object and prior-work distinction

For each enclosing m in {3,4,5,6}, the word is exactly

    tau=1/4, lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=1-tau/m,
    c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=beta_word/c,
    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1.

All children retain their enclosing m and tau. The numerical records save
the binary64 evaluation of these formulas and every physical step.

The historical full-PEP finding df56220f87e79c22 uses tau=1 at each of
m=3,4,5,6. This was checked from every public raw schedule formula, not
from an H label or a search match. Accepted e9a55f57461f253c uses exactly
the old m=6,tau=1 word, with c=(7/6)lambda and a=5(sqrt(2)-1)/7.
The new word differs already at its first leaf: the factor
(1-tau/m)/(1+tau/m) decreases strictly with tau, by cross multiplication.
Thus the previous experiments were not a replay of the new cases.
`historical_identity_inputs.json` stores the source identities and hashes.

Current steering: assignment/master_guidance6a33fa593da4ce7d; the later
7a4e289802c5f694 expressly leaves this max65 allocation unchanged.
Preregistration finding:79fdc29308070926. The three code files, exact saved
benchmark rows, historical identity record and preregistration were hashed
before the first mathematical job in `freeze_manifest.json`.

## 2. Full original mathematical model

Normalize L=1 and the SPECIFIED minimizer x_star=g_star=f_star=0. The
full symmetric Gram matrix G is on all formal vectors(x0,g0,...,gn).
The physical positions are x_i=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t. The program maximizes
2f_n under

    G PSD; G00=1;
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0
    for all distinct ordered i,j in {star,0,...,n}.

Diagonal rows are identities0=0. Every original nontrivial row is retained,
including both terminal directions and both minimizer directions. The
terminal gradient g_n is an interpolation variable, not an extra update.
There is no rank, support, scalar, quadratic, or primitive-certificate
restriction. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 proof Sections1--3 supplies the full
interpolation equivalence and attained normalization at G00=1.

The only conditioning is the invertible transformation
M=1+sum h, D=diag(1,1/M,...,1/M), G=D Ghat D, f=fhat/M. The solver maximizes
2fhat_n and imposes M Q<=0. If A,F are the original Gram/value row maps,
the raw pair multipliers lambda are already in original units, while
q=raw_norm_multiplier/M. The full original dual quadratic matrix is
C=q E00+A^T lambda. Its original value flow must be F^T lambda=2e_n,
and scaled stationarity compares M D C D with the raw PSD multiplier.
The norm multiplier is unrestricted because normalization is equality.

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| h=(3/2) | 1 | 3 | 6 | 8 | 17 |
| H_(3,1/4) | 7 | 9 | 72 | 53 | 13 |
| H_(4,1/4) | 15 | 17 | 272 | 169 | 14 |
| H_(5,1/4) | 31 | 33 | 1056 | 593 | 20 |
| H_(6,1/4) | 63 | 65 | 4160 | 2209 | 29 |

All five solver statuses were `optimal`, using the fixed settings
CLARABEL(max_iter120,max_threads1,time_limit20,tol_gap_abs1e-9,
tol_gap_rel1e-9,tol_feas1e-9). An independent external30-second timeout
covered each entire child, including numerical imports, build, solve,
all diagnostics and raw export. No retry or fallback occurred.

Runtime: Python3.12.14, CVXPY1.9.3, CLARABEL0.11.1, NumPy2.5.3,
SciPy1.18.1; arithmetic IEEE754 binary64. Solver tolerance is not arithmetic
precision. BLAS and solver thread counts were restricted to1.

## 3. Calibration with exact symbolic reference

For h=(3/2), expand the original Q rows to obtain

 (1/4)||x0||^2-2f1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)
        =||x0/2-g0-g1||^2.

Thus2f1<=1/4 for G00=1. The scalar function f(x)=x^2/2 with x0=1 gives
x1=-1/2 and2f1=1/4. The Huber function with threshold1/4 also attains
this value: g0=g1=1/4, x1=5/8, f1=1/8. This derivation was in the
preregistration before execution, not a post-fit interpretation.

The solver returned primal0.24999999998853667 and dual0.24999999993770056,
gap-5.0836113096863755e-11. Original G and C minimum eigenvalues were
-2.2403978076548187e-10 and-1.0956870692392237e-09; the maximum original Q
was-5.248615431163728e-11. All frozen calibration checks passed.
The numerical matrices are still not exact primal/dual certificates.
