# Every finite delay: exact value pricing and complete redundancy

Status: UNVERIFIED complete joint proof draft, max2 round26.
TASK.md and master_guidance6e0a18d700dcd62f prohibit fact_submit.
Nothing in this document is an accepted fact. No numerical/model scan,
solver, machine algebra, replay, installation or subordinate agent was used.

Accepted inputs: fact04219554e41d9750 Section10 for the ORIGINAL
primitive identity and its full formal-gradient certificate interface;
fact1252b8823bb5a290 proof paragraphs1--2 for the interpolation convention
and constructive sufficiency. Every other calculation is proved jointly
below. The earlier immediate-annihilation draft cc0fc35f2fb5d6bb is
awareness, not a predecessor or an assumed lemma.

Sections1--7 first prove a family with TWO negative fast samples before
annihilation, including its exact three-path price and a polynomial
budget. Section9 then proves the stronger final result: ANY finite
negative excursion after a reversal at ANY index, followed by
annihilation, is redundant under the stated global and local prefix
bounds. For killing step b<=2 the local bounds are automatic. Thus this
final scope is not limited to an initial reversal, two negative samples,
or the previous immediately annihilated family. Its entire
parameter-and-value minimum is decided, not merely its price formula.

The proof supplies one static attaining smooth convex function, every
directed finite and optimum inequality, the complete minimizer set and
true distance, all coincident-point boundaries, and all zero-step indices.
It does not decide the general full cone or the original exact-profile
target. The verifier gate stays closed.

## 1. Family, exact minimum and complete comparison

Fix an integer n>=3 and a nonnegative finite word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)).
Write P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, s=P_n and C=1+s. Assume

    h_0=r, h_1=c, h_2=b,
    1<=b<=r<=2, 0<=c<=b,
    h_i<=P_i+2 for every i<n.                       (1)

No bound on n or on the total tail mass is imposed. In particular the
case n=4 retains the original five formal gradients at arbitrary
capacity allowed by(1); capacity C is not frozen to a sample value.

Choose d>=1, amplitudes u_1,...,u_d,v>=0 and curvatures satisfying

    beta=1/b, 0<alpha_q<beta,
    alpha_q*h_i<=1 for every i<n and 1<=q<=d.

Set

    p_(0,q)=1, p_(i,q)=product_(t<i)(1-alpha_q*h_t),
    m=r/b-1, theta=1-c/b,
    z_0=1, z_1=-m, z_2=-m*theta, z_i=0 for i>=3,
    x_i=(u_q*p_(i,q) for1<=q<=d, v*z_i),
    g_i=(alpha_q*u_q*p_(i,q) for1<=q<=d, beta*v*z_i). (2)

These samples obey every GD update x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i. Before values
are varied they are samples of the SINGLE fixed diagonal quadratic

    F_base(x)=sum_q alpha_q*x_q^2/2+beta*x_(d+1)^2/2.

Thus no Hessian switching or gluing of independently chosen jets is
assumed. Every slow coordinate is nonnegative and nonincreasing. If
r>b, v>0 and 0<c<b, the fast mode is positive at0, negative and nonzero
at1 and2, and first zero at3. This is genuine delayed annihilation.

Define the nonnegative slack weights and the first three slow gaps by

    U_q=alpha_q*(1-alpha_q)*u_q^2/2,
    B=beta*(1-beta)*v^2/2,
    Delta_(i,q)=p_(i,q)-p_(i+1,q) for0<=i<n.

The three energy price increments are

    X26=4r*sum_q U_q*(Delta_(0,q)*Delta_(1,q)
                     +Delta_(0,q)*Delta_(2,q)
                     +Delta_(1,q)*Delta_(2,q)),
    Z26=4r*[sum_q U_q*Delta_(0,q)*Delta_(1,q)
                         +B*m*theta*(1+m*theta)],
    Y26=4r*B*[m+m^2*(1-theta+theta^2)].             (3)

Here X26 bypasses BOTH negative samples; Z26 visits the second negative
sample and bypasses the first; Y26 visits both chronologically in reverse.
They are increments above the sum of separate-mode optimal prices.

For a_(i,q)=alpha_q*u_q*p_(i,q) and
t_(i,q)=a_(i,q)-a_(i+1,q), put

    L_slow=sum_(q,i<n) P_(i+1)*t_(i,q)
               *[2(1+P_i)*a_(i,q)-P_(i+1)*t_(i,q)],
    K_neg=c^2*beta*(2-c^2*beta)
                   +(c+b)*(c+2-b)*theta^2,
    H_fast=beta^2*v^2*[r*(2-r)+m^2*K_neg].          (4)

For any scalar values f_i on(2), define the primitive energy

    V_h(f)=2sum_(i<n)h_i*(f_i-f_n)
       +sum_(i<n)h_i*(1-h_i)*||g_i||^2-s*(s+1)*||g_n||^2.

Among ALL values satisfying ALL ordered finite interpolation constraints,
the exact minimum is

    V_min=L_slow+H_fast-min(X26,Y26,Z26).           (5)

The same minimum holds when the fixed actual optimum jet(0,0,0) is
required. It is attained by the static function in Section6.

The full parameter comparison is

    L_slow>=X26+r^2*(2-r)*sum_q alpha_q^3*u_q^2,
    H_fast>=beta^2*v^2*r*(2-r),
    V_min>=r*(2-r)*[beta^2*v^2
                           +r*sum_q alpha_q^3*u_q^2]>=0. (6)

The first THREE terms of each slow-mode energy already cover its share
of X26. All subsequent terms are nonnegative. The comparison is uniform
over every permitted curvature, amplitude, tail and choice of legal
joint values, including values from nonseparable functions. Consequently
the ENTIRE displayed delayed-annihilation family imposes no additional
primitive condition beyond(1).

For r<2 and a nonzero initial vector, the final bound in(6) is strict.
For 1<b<r, 0<c<b, v>0 and at least one active slow mode with
alpha_q*r<1, all three quantities in(3) are positive. Thus incompatible
orderings really do create a strict positive joint price. Inequality(6)
decides its information content after full optimization.

## 2. Original orientation and the all-value problem

Keep every original index I={0,...,n}, including zero-step samples.
For distinct i,j set

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 gives both the necessity of Q_ij<=0 and its
constructive sufficiency with an optimum sample. Let Lambda_ij>=0,
Lambda_ii=0, e=(1,...,1)^T, and define

    L_flow=diag(Lambda*e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,
    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    D_prim=diag(h_i*(2-h_i) for i<n, -s*(s+2)).

The ORIGINAL sufficient primitive certificate is

    L_flow^T*e=omega,
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow*T+T^T*L_flow^T)/2 PSD on R^(n+1). (7)

Its full identity from accepted04219554e41d9750 is

    V_h(f)+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij*Q_ij
                        =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>. (8)

The flow here is OUTGOING minus incoming. The terminal n is the source
of mass2s, and each i<n is a sink of mass2h_i. An auxiliary minimum-cost
flow below is not presumed to satisfy the PSD condition in(7).

For the base values f_i^0=F_base(x_i), direct expansion gives the
symmetric nonnegative slack costs

    w_ij=-Q_ij(f^0)
        =sum_q U_q*(p_(i,q)-p_(j,q))^2+B*(z_i-z_j)^2. (9)

Let d_i be the shortest path cost n->i in the complete finite graph
with these costs. The minimum exists over simple paths; nonnegative
costs allow the removal of every cycle even when some edges cost zero.
New values f_i^0+delta_i satisfy every directed inequality exactly when

    delta_i-delta_j<=w_ij for every distinct i,j.

Every path n->i implies delta_i-delta_n>=-d_i. Since h_i>=0,

    min_f V_h(f)=V_h(f^0)-2sum_(i<n)h_i*d_i.       (10)

All lower bounds are attained simultaneously by delta_i=-d_i: the
distance inequalities d_j<=d_i+w_ij verify EVERY directed constraint.

For any original prescribed flow, w_ij>=d_j-d_i and the flow equation
give

    sum Lambda_ij*w_ij>=-sum omega_i*d_i
                                =2sum_(i<n)h_i*d_i.

Send2h_i units along a shortest n->i path for each i with h_i>0.
This is a nonnegative original prescribed flow attaining equality.
Zero-weight vertices remain in the complete graph. This proves that
(10) is the exact ORIGINAL terminal-source price, not the reverse-flow
quantity. It also proves why this optimizing flow need not be PSD.

## 3. All shortest paths and the three competing increments

For ordered scalar positions p_0>=...>=p_n and squared-distance edge
costs U*(p_i-p_j)^2, the distance from n to i is

    U*sum_(k=i)^(n-1)(p_k-p_(k+1))^2.              (11)

Indeed an edge spanning several ordered gaps has cost at least the sum
of the squared gaps it spans. Every path between two ordered positions
crosses every intervening gap at least once. Summing these lower bounds
proves(11); the path through the consecutive ordered points attains it.
Zero gaps and tied positions satisfy the same proof.

In the separate fast scalar coordinate, the finite sampled positions
are1,-m,-m*theta,0 with possible repetitions. The shortest costs from
the zero terminal are

    d_0^fast=B,
    d_1^fast=B*m^2*[theta^2+(1-theta)^2],
    d_2^fast=B*m^2*theta^2,
    d_i^fast=0 for i>=3.                           (12)

Apply the same ordered-gap proof on each side of0. For the positive
target1, detouring through any negative position only increases cost.
For each negative target, detouring through the positive position only
increases cost. This also proves every equality in degenerate cases.

Let d_i^sep be the sum of(12) and all slow distances(11). Every joint
path costs at least the sum of the separate minima. For every i>=1,
the chronological path n,n-1,...,i simultaneously attains all those
separate minima, so

    d_i=d_i^sep for every i>=1.                    (13)

There is no assumption that the best paths to0 coincide across modes.
For a path whose last edge is k->0, the preceding cost is at least
d_k^sep, and this lower bound is attained by a path avoiding0.
Thus d_0=min_(k>=1)(d_k^sep+w_(k,0)).

Among k>=3 the best choice is k=3. The fast position is zero at every
such k. In each slow coordinate insert the intermediate points
k-1,...,3 into the last edge k->0. Splitting a nonnegative gap reduces
the sum of squared gaps, so

    d_k^sep+w_(k,0)>=d_3^sep+w_(3,0).

Therefore exactly three paths suffice: n,...,3,0; n,...,2,0; and
n,...,1,0. Their costs minus d_0^sep are respectively

    2sum_q U_q*(Delta_(0,q)*Delta_(1,q)
                +Delta_(0,q)*Delta_(2,q)
                +Delta_(1,q)*Delta_(2,q)),
    2sum_q U_q*Delta_(0,q)*Delta_(1,q)
                         +2B*m*theta*(1+m*theta),
    2B*[m+m^2*(1-theta+theta^2)].                  (14)

For the first line expand the square of the sum of three slow gaps.
For the second line the slow excess is the cross product of the first
two gaps; the fast excess is
B*[(m*theta)^2+(1+m*theta)^2-1]. For the third line the fast excess is
B*[m^2*theta^2+m^2*(1-theta)^2+(1+m)^2-1]. These expansions give(14).

Equations(13)--(14) check all possible paths in the COMPLETE graph.
In particular, no detour via a later vertex or via the opposite sign
can evade them. By the distance argument of Section2, all directed
inequalities for the optimal potential follow. Multiplication of(14)
by2h_0=2r proves the exact joint price increment

    2sum_(i<n)h_i*(d_i-d_i^sep)=min(X26,Y26,Z26).  (15)

An attaining original flow sends the sink at0 along the least-cost
one of these three paths, and every other sink chronologically. Ties
can be resolved either way. All flows have the original source and
exact node divergences, and no PSD claim is made for them.

## 4. Exact separate energies

For a slow scalar coordinate write a_i=alpha*u*p_i and
t_i=a_i-a_(i+1)>=0. At its optimal scalar values the reverse adjacent
inequality is tight and

    f_i-f_(i+1)=h_i*a_i*a_(i+1)+t_i^2/2.

This follows either by substituting the base values minus distances(11),
or by expanding that tight inequality. Substituting these differences
into V_h makes its value term

    sum_i[2P_(i+1)*h_i*a_i*a_(i+1)+P_(i+1)*t_i^2].

After writing a_(i+1)=a_i-t_i, the coefficient of a_i^2 is
h_i*(1+2P_i+h_i)=P_(i+1)*(P_(i+1)+1)-P_i*(P_i+1).
Telescope those coefficients against the terminal term. The result is

    L_alpha=sum_(i<n)P_(i+1)*t_i
                  *[2(1+P_i)*a_i-P_(i+1)*t_i].    (16)

This is an EXACT scalar minimum since the distance potential satisfies
every nonadjacent inequality as well. Also 0<=t_i<=a_i, so

    2(1+P_i)*a_i-P_(i+1)*t_i
                   >=(P_i+2-h_i)*a_i>=0.          (17)

Thus every slow energy summand is nonnegative under(1).

The separate fast optimum has terminal value0 and f_0=beta^2*v^2/2.
On its negative side, the two-step word(c,b) starts at gradient
-beta*v*m, contracts by theta at its first step and reaches zero at
its second. Sign reflection does not change its energy. Formula(16),
proved directly above, on this two-step monotone history gives

    beta^2*v^2*m^2*
       [c^2*beta*(2-c^2*beta)+(c+b)*(c+2-b)*theta^2].

The initial positive sample contributes beta^2*v^2*r*(2-r), with
no nonzero terminal term because the final fast gradient is zero.
This proves the exact H_fast in(4). Since c<=b<=2,

    2-c^2*beta>=2-c>=0, c+2-b>=0,

so K_neg>=0. The base energy is a sum of coordinate energies. Combining
their exact separate minima with(15) proves the exact joint minimum(5).
It has not combined incompatible scalar values into a supposed function;
its actual simultaneous values are constructed in Section6.

## 5. Three slow terms pay for the full delayed bypass

It suffices to consider one slow mode alpha,u. Write

    ell=1-r*alpha, tau=c/r, zeta=b/r,
    a=1-tau, k=a+tau*ell, R=1+tau+zeta.

All ell,tau,zeta belong to[0,1], and p_1=ell, p_2=ell*k.
Let X26_alpha be that coordinate's contribution to X26. Since
Delta_0=r*alpha, Delta_1=c*alpha*ell and
Delta_2=b*alpha*ell*k, one has

    X26_alpha=2r*alpha^3*(1-alpha)*u^2
                       *[r*c*ell+r*b*ell*k+c*b*ell^2*k].

The first three terms of(16), divided by alpha^3*u^2 when u>0, are

    r^2*(2-r^2*alpha)
    +c*(r+c)*ell^2*[2(1+r)-c*(r+c)*alpha]
    +b*(r+c+b)*ell^2*k^2*[2(1+r+c)-b*(r+c+b)*alpha].

Define S=tau*ell+zeta*ell*k+tau*zeta*ell^2*k. Subtracting
X26_alpha and substituting alpha=(1-ell)/r gives exactly

    first_three_terms-X26_alpha=r^2*alpha^3*u^2*D(r),

    D(r)=2-r+r*ell
      +tau*(1+tau)*ell^2*[2+2r-r*tau*(1+tau)*(1-ell)]
      +zeta*R*ell^2*k^2*[2+2r*(1+tau)-r*zeta*R*(1-ell)]
      -2*(r-1+ell)*S.                             (18)

Here ell,tau,zeta are held fixed when viewing D(r) as an affine
polynomial in the symbol r. Its formal coefficient evaluation at r=0
is ONLY an algebraic device, not a proposed zero-step physical instance:

    D(0)=2+2tau*(1+tau)*ell^2+2zeta*R*ell^2*k^2
                                              +2(1-ell)*S>=2. (19)

At r=2, put A=6-2tau-2tau^2 and B_aux=6+4tau-2zeta*R. Since
zeta<=1, zeta*R<=2+tau and B_aux>=2+2tau. Expanding(18) gives

    D(2)=C_1*ell+C_2*ell^2+C_3*ell^3+C_4*ell^4+C_5*ell^5,
    C_1=2a*(1-zeta),
    C_2=tau*(1+tau)*A+zeta*R*a^2*B_aux
                    -2[tau+zeta*a+tau*zeta*(2-tau)],
    C_3=2tau^2*(1+tau)^2+2zeta*R*a*tau*B_aux
                         +2zeta^2*R^2*a^2-4tau*zeta,
    C_4=tau^2*zeta*(R*B_aux-2)+4zeta^2*R^2*a*tau,
    C_5=2zeta^2*R^2*tau^2.                        (20)

C_1,C_4,C_5 are nonnegative: a>=0, zeta<=1, R>=1 and B_aux>=2.
The two remaining coefficients have explicit nonnegative bounds.
First define F_tau=tau*(2+2tau-2tau^2-tau^3). Then

    C_2/2
      =F_tau-zeta*(1+tau-tau^2)
                +zeta*R*a^2*(3+2tau-zeta*R)
      >=F_tau-zeta*tau*(1+tau-tau^3)
                                      +zeta^2*(1+tau)*a^2
      >=tau*(1-tau)*(1+2tau)+zeta^2*(1+tau)*a^2>=0. (21)

For the first inequality use 3+2tau-zeta*R>=1+tau and
(1+tau)^2*a^2=(1-tau^2)^2. For the second use zeta<=1 and
tau*(1+tau-tau^3)>=0, then expand F_tau-tau*(1+tau-tau^3).
For C_3 use B_aux>=2(1+tau) to obtain

    C_3/2>=[tau*(1+tau)+zeta*R*a]^2-2tau*zeta.

The expression inside the square exceeds tau+zeta because

    tau*(1+tau)+zeta*R*a-(tau+zeta)
                         =tau^2*(1-zeta)+zeta^2*a>=0.

All quantities being squared are nonnegative. Hence

    C_3/2>=(tau+zeta)^2-2tau*zeta=tau^2+zeta^2>=0. (22)

Every coefficient in(20) is nonnegative, proving D(2)>=0. Affineness
and 0<=r<=2 now give

    D(r)=(1-r/2)*D(0)+(r/2)*D(2)>=2-r.            (23)

Multiply back in(18). If u=0, the same multiplied identity holds
without division. Every later term in(16) is nonnegative by(17).
Therefore

    L_alpha>=X26_alpha+r^2*(2-r)*alpha^3*u^2.

Sum over all slow modes, use min(X26,Y26,Z26)<=X26, and combine with
the fast lower bound from Section4. This proves ALL of(6), including
the entire parameter minimization. No numerical coefficient checks or
symbolic-algebra computation have been substituted for this proof.

## 6. Static attainment, fixed optimum and true distance

Append a distinguished index star with x_star=g_star=f_star=0.
Its shortest distance from n is

    D_star=sum_q U_q*p_(n,q)^2.                    (24)

Indeed the fast terminal is zero, and in each slow coordinate every
finite position is at least its terminal position. No sampled position
lies strictly between zero and that terminal position. The ordered-gap
proof of(11) therefore gives a lower bound U_q*p_(n,q)^2 for each
slow coordinate. The direct edge to star attains their sum.

Adding star cannot shorten any finite distance. For every finite j,

    w_(n,j)=D_star+w_(star,j)
                        -2sum_q U_q*p_(n,q)*p_(j,q)
             <=D_star+w_(star,j).

Any path through star first pays at least D_star to reach it. Replace
that prefix and its following edge star->j by n->j. The cost does not
increase. Thus the finite distances in Section3 remain exact after
adding the optimum. No optimum multiplier has been inserted into(7).

The simultaneous attaining values are

    f_i=F_base(x_i)+D_star-d_i, f_star=0.          (25)

They are the negative distance potential shifted by D_star relative
to the base quadratic. All ordered finite and optimum inequalities
follow from distance inequalities, in BOTH directions, including all
coincident-point pairs.

For i in I together with star put y_i=x_i-g_i,
eta_i=||g_i||^2/2-f_i, including y_star=eta_star=0. Define on R^(d+1)

    H(w)=||w||^2/2+max_i(<y_i,w>+eta_i),
    F(x)=sup_w(<x,w>-H(w)).                        (26)

This is ONE static global function. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 proves
the needed constructive interpolation statement, and its elementary
argument applies to this complete finite set: H is finite, continuous,
1-strongly convex and has quadratic growth, so every x has a unique
maximizing w(x). Strong subgradient inequalities give
<x-x',w(x)-w(x')>>=||w(x)-w(x')||^2. The maximizing inequalities
then prove differentiability of F with gradient w(x), which is
1-Lipschitz. As a supremum of affine functions, F is convex.

At each g_i, expansion of a competing branch minus the i-th branch
is exactly Q_ij. All are nonpositive, and the i-th branch is active
with gradient x_i. Thus grad F(x_i)=g_i and F(x_i)=f_i. In particular
F realizes EVERY GD step in(2), F(0)=0, and grad F(0)=0.

Its COMPLETE minimizer set and exact initial radius are

    K_min=conv{y_i:eta_i=0},
    R_true=dist(x_0,K_min).                        (27)

To verify this, the optimum inequalities give eta_i<=0 and eta_star=0.
The zero gradient condition is x in partial H(0). The subdifferential
there is the convex hull of the active affine slopes: a convex
combination gives the subgradient inequality directly; for a point
outside the hull, a separating direction and a sufficiently small
positive displacement exclude every inactive branch and contradict
that inequality. This proves both inclusions and(27).

If some active slow mode has p_(n,q)>0, all finite eta_i are strictly
negative. In fact d_i<=w_(n,i) and(25) give

    eta_i<=-2sum_q U_q*p_(i,q)*p_(n,q)<0.

Then K_min={0} and R_true^2=sum_q u_q^2+v^2. The complete open family
with alpha_q*h_i<1 and an active slow mode has this simpler radius.
If all slow amplitudes vanish and v>0, the largest active scalar slope
is y_0=(1-beta)*v, while all other slopes are nonpositive. Thus the
closest minimizer to x_0=v is y_0 and R_true=beta*v. Formula(27)
covers every other terminal-zero or vanishing-amplitude boundary
without asserting a false unique minimizer. For every nonzero initial
vector, g_0!=0, so x_0 is outside K_min and R_true>0.

The function can be rescaled to true initial radius1 by
F_scaled(z)=[F(x_closest+R_true*z)-min F]/R_true^2, where x_closest
is the Euclidean projection of x_0 onto K_min. Its gradient Lipschitz
constant remains1 and its GD uses the same word. Multiplication by
any L>0 gives the corresponding L-smooth instance.

## 7. Boundaries, prior tests and exact four-step scope

The formulas retain all non-strict boundaries:

* c=0 gives theta=1 and coincident samples1,2. Then Delta_1=0 and
  Y26=Z26; the theorem reduces algebraically to the earlier two-sample
  chronology after retaining its zero step.
* c=b gives theta=0, so the fast mode already reaches zero at2.
  Z26 is the shorter bypass through that first zero; the stated
  three-candidate minimum still includes it. Nothing assumes two
  strictly negative samples on this boundary.
* r=b or v=0 makes the genuine fast excursion absent and Y26=0.
* b=1 gives beta=1 and B=0, so Y26=0 as required by zero fast slacks.
* Vanishing slow amplitudes remove their costs and X26=0. If a slow
  product reaches zero, every subsequent gap of that mode is zero.
* r=2 is a nonnegative, not universally strictly positive, conclusion.
  Formula(23) still applies. No strict lower bound is falsely retained.
* Repeated points force equal gradients and equal attained values;
  their zero-cost paths verify both ordered interpolation inequalities.

Any additional zero steps inserted into the physical chronology can be
retained as repeated vertices with zero cost. The preceding path and
value proof applies with them present. No equality of FORMAL gradient
coordinates is imposed on the certificate(7), and no formal pair is
deleted. In n=4 the statement preserves all twenty original finite
multiplier signs and the entire ambient R^5 PSD requirement.

For clarity, the prefix-step bounds used in(1) are already necessary
for any primitive certificate. A direct legal witness proves this:
fix a cut j, put P=P_j, and suppose h_j>P+2. Take the one-sided capped
quadratic phi(t)=0 for t<=0, phi(t)=t^2/2 for0<=t<=1, and
phi(t)=t-1/2 for t>=1. It is convex differentiable and1-smooth, with
minimizers(-infinity,0]. Start at x_0=P+1. All gradients through j
are1; at j the point is1; after its step the point is1-h_j<0 and
all subsequent gradients and values are0. Substitution in V_h gives

    V_h=(P+h_j)*(P+2-h_j)<0.

Indeed for S=P+h_j, the value and gradient sums are
2(P+1)*S-2sum_(i<=j)h_i*P_i-sum_(i<=j)h_i^2
=2(P+1)*S-S^2. Its true initial distance is P+1. Identity(8) forbids
a full PSD certificate for this word. Thus h_i<=P_i+2 are old actual
primitive tests, not newly invented restrictions masking the gain.

In the four-step case the NEW interior covered here is

    h=(r,c,b,t), 1<b<r<=2, 0<c<b,
    0<=t<=r+c+b+2,
    0<alpha_q<min{1/b,1/max_i h_i}, beta=1/b.

The fast trajectory has two negative samples before the zero at3.
Tail t can be arbitrary in its displayed old-bound interval. The
complete redundancy is uniform over capacity C=1+r+c+b+t, including
C>=193/25 where that interval permits it. The theorem does not turn
any auxiliary minimum-price flow into a feasible full PSD matrix.

The finite-delay and initial-reversal restrictions of Sections1--7 are
removed in Section9 under its stated suffix hypotheses. Fast modes that
never reach zero, multiple sign reversals, multiple sign-changing
curvatures, and planar fractional repairs remain outside the result.
The complementary C82 full-system comparison remains open. No global
no-seed statement or original exact-profile answer follows.

## 8. Literature and verification status

The exact Matlas query about matched-curvature value minimization and
shortest squared-distance paths returned unrelated saddle-point and
low-rank-matrix statements; none was used. A primary-source web search
recovered Taylor, Hendrickx and Glineur, "Smooth Strongly Convex
Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance of First-order Methods",
arXiv:1502.05666. The local extracted Theorem4 proof, constructive
Remark2, Corollary1 and cycle Remark3 were read. Their interpolation
mechanism is already proved in accepted1252b8823bb5a290, which is the
input actually used here; no additional external theorem is imported.

This is a complete joint DRAFT proof with exact new class scope and
full comparison. It remains UNVERIFIED. No intermediate draft is used
as an accepted brick, and no fact_submit has been made.

## 9. Arbitrary finite delay after a reversal at any index

This is the final, stronger scope. The derivation below is part of the
same joint proof. It does not adopt a previous unverified draft as a
premise. The interpolation and flow arguments are already proved in
Sections2 and6; their hypotheses are checked on the new geometry here.

Choose integers0<=j<K-1<n and a nonnegative word h of length n.
Put P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and assume the global old prefix bounds
h_i<=P_i+2. Let

    P=P_j, r=h_j, b=h_(K-1), H_cut=P+r,
    1<=b<=r, beta=1/b,
    h_i<=b for i<j and for j<i<K-1.                (28)

The contracting negative block must itself satisfy the old prefix
bounds, when measured from its own beginning:

    R_i=sum_(j+1<=t<i)h_t,
    h_i<=R_i+2 for j+1<=i<K.                       (29)

If b<=2, ALL of(29) follows automatically from(28), because every
step in that block is at most b. In this important whole subfamily the
only energy sign assumptions beyond the chronology are the original
global prefix-step bounds. If b>2, condition(29) is an explicit extra
restriction on the family; it is not claimed necessary for the full
original cone.

Use any d>=1, u_q,v>=0 and slow curvatures0<alpha_q<beta with
alpha_q*h_i<=1 for every i. Define p_(i,q) as in(2), and replace z_i by

    z_i=product_(t<i)(1-beta*h_t) for0<=i<=K,
    z_i=0 for i>K.                                (30)

Then z_0>=...>=z_j>=0, z_(j+1)=(1-r/b)*z_j<=0,
z_(j+1)<=z_(j+2)<=...<=z_K=0. The same diagonal quadratic defines
the base geometry x_i=(u_q*p_(i,q),v*z_i) and
g_i=(alpha_q*u_q*p_(i,q),beta*v*z_i). Genuine single reversal and
delayed annihilation mean v>0, z_j>0, r>b, and h_i<b for j<i<K-1.
All boundaries are allowed without asserting that the reversal remains
strict. The number of negative samples K-j-1 is arbitrary.

Keep U_q and B from Section1. For j+1<=k<=K put

    A_k=sum_q U_q*[(p_(j,q)-p_(k,q))^2
                         -sum_(l=j)^(k-1)Delta_(l,q)^2],
    B_k=B*[sum_(l=k)^(K-1)(z_l-z_(l+1))^2
                                   +(z_j-z_k)^2-z_j^2],
    delta_cut=min_(j+1<=k<=K)(A_k+B_k),
    X_all=2H_cut*A_K.                             (31)

All A_k and B_k are nonnegative. For A_k expand a square of a sum of
nonnegative slow gaps. For B_k expand (z_j-z_k)^2-z_j^2
=z_k^2-2z_j*z_k>=0, because z_j>=0 and z_k<=0. Also B_K=0.

Define L_slow by the same all-index formula(4). For the positive fast
prefix let a_i^+=beta*v*z_i for i<=j and t_i^+=a_i^+-a_(i+1)^+ for i<j.
For the negative fast block let a_i^-=-beta*v*z_i for j+1<=i<=K and
t_i^-=a_i^--a_(i+1)^- for j+1<=i<K. Put

    E_pos=sum_(i<j)P_(i+1)*t_i^+
                     *[2(1+P_i)*a_i^+-P_(i+1)*t_i^+],
    E_neg=sum_(j+1<=i<K)R_(i+1)*t_i^-
                     *[2(1+R_i)*a_i^--R_(i+1)*t_i^-],
    H_general=E_pos+H_cut*(P+2-r)*(a_j^+)^2+E_neg. (32)

The EXACT minimum over ALL directed finite-compatible values is

    V_min=L_slow+H_general-2H_cut*delta_cut.        (33)

Requiring the actual optimum jet0 does not change this minimum. Its
attaining static function and full minimizer set are(25)--(27), using
the distances proved next. The COMPLETE comparison is

    L_slow>=X_all+H_cut*r*(P+2-r)
                              *sum_q alpha_q^3*u_q^2*p_(j,q)^2,
    0<=2H_cut*delta_cut<=X_all,
    V_min>=H_general>=0.                          (34)

Thus every finite delay is redundant throughout(28)--(29). In
particular, for b<=2, adding arbitrary monotone slow modes and
optimizing ALL legal joint values gives no new primitive obstruction
after a single reversal at any index and any later annihilation.

### 9.1 Exact original-flow price and every directed constraint

Separate slow distances are still(11). The separate fast distances are

    d_i^fast=B*[z_j^2+sum_(l=i)^(j-1)(z_l-z_(l+1))^2] for i<=j,
    d_i^fast=B*sum_(l=i)^(K-1)(z_l-z_(l+1))^2 for j<i<K,
    d_i^fast=0 for i>=K.                           (35)

The ordered-gap argument proves these formulas on the positive and
negative sides, respectively. Excursions to the opposite side cannot
decrease scalar squared-distance path cost. Let d_i^sep be the sum of
(35) and all separate slow distances. For every i>=j+1 the chronological
path n,...,i simultaneously attains these minima, hence d_i=d_i^sep.

For i<=j propose

    d_i=d_i^sep+delta_cut.                         (36)

The paths n,...,k,j,j-1,...,i have costs d_i^sep+A_k+B_k, so at least
one such path attains(36). Every directed distance inequality is now
checked explicitly to prove that no other path is cheaper.

The sum d^sep of scalar distance potentials satisfies every such
inequality for the summed costs. Within the early group i<=j or within
the later group i>=j+1, the added constant delta_cut cancels. For an
edge whose source is early and target is later, it only weakens the
inequality. Consider a later source k and an early target i. In every
slow coordinate and in the fast coordinate, the position of k is at
most that of j; the points j,j-1,...,i are coordinatewise nondecreasing.
Expanding nonnegative coordinate gaps therefore gives

    w_(k,i)>=w_(k,j)+sum_(l=i)^(j-1)w_(l+1,l),
    d_i^sep=d_j^sep+sum_(l=i)^(j-1)w_(l+1,l).

It suffices to check the target j. If j<k<=K, its slack relative to
d^sep is exactly A_k+B_k>=delta_cut. If k>=K, all fast coordinates
there are zero; insert slow ordered points k-1,...,K into k->j.
The squared-gap inequality gives

    d_k^sep+w_(k,j)>=d_K^sep+w_(K,j)
                                      =d_j^sep+A_K>=d_j^sep+delta_cut.

This proves the remaining directed inequalities. The proposed d_n=0
and these inequalities imply the corresponding lower bound on every
path from n, while the stated paths attain it. Thus(36) is exact.

The common extra distance on all early sinks costs
2sum_(i<=j)h_i*delta_cut=2H_cut*delta_cut. Sending each early sink
along its minimizing crossing path and every later sink chronologically
attains that price with the ORIGINAL terminal-source flow. This proves
the price part of(33), with all pairs retained. It proves no PSD claim
about the resulting auxiliary flow.

The fast terminal is zero and every slow point is at least its terminal
point. Thus the entire fixed-optimum argument(24)--(27) applies:
the optimum does not shorten finite distances; the simultaneous values
are legal for all ordered pairs; one static F attains them; K_min and
R_true in(27) are the actual full minimizer set and actual radius.

### 9.2 Separate fast energy and its sign

For the scalar fast values, the smallest positive sampled position is
z_j, so its optimal value is (a_j^+)^2/2. The positive-prefix value
differences attain the ordered equalities used to derive(16). The
negative side realizes the reflected monotone block with exact energy
E_neg and terminal value and gradient zero. No positive-prefix position
is inserted into its negative ordered path.

Split the original scalar primitive energy into the prefix up to j,
the step r at j, and the negative block. The prefix's own energy is
E_pos and subtracts P(P+1)*(a_j^+)^2. Restoring that term and shifting
its value reference from f_j to the zero final value adds
[P(P+1)+P]*(a_j^+)^2. The step r contributes r*(2-r)*(a_j^+)^2.
Their total added coefficient is

    P(P+1)+P+r*(2-r)
                       =(P+r)*(P+2-r)=H_cut*(P+2-r).

This proves the EXACT expression H_general in(32). Its terms are
nonnegative by the global prefix bounds, the bound r<=P+2, and the
local negative-block bounds(29), respectively, using inequality(17)
with the appropriate local partial sums. Together with the exact
price, this proves(33).

### 9.3 A direct complete budget for an arbitrarily long bypass

Fix one slow coordinate alpha,u. Its optimized energy is(16).
All terms at indices i>=K are nonnegative by the global prefix bounds,
so it suffices to use the first K terms. Their sum is the exact scalar
primitive energy for the prefix word of length K, by the same
telescoping derivation of(16).

If u*p_j=0, this coordinate's A_K is zero and all its energy terms
are nonnegative; the desired comparison follows. Otherwise put

    a_j=alpha*u*p_j, q=p_K/p_j,
    T_neg=sum_(j+1<=i<K)h_i,
    ell=1-r*alpha,
    q_bar=ell/(1+alpha*T_neg).                     (37)

All quantities here are defined for this coordinate. Monotonicity and
the GD products imply0<=q<=q_bar<=1. For completeness the elementary
product bound is

    product_(j+1<=i<K)(1-alpha*h_i)
                                 <=1/(1+alpha*T_neg).

To prove it inductively, if A is the sum of previous nonnegative
factors alpha*h_i and t is the next factor in[0,1], then
(1-t)*(1+A+t)=1+A-t*A-t^2<=1+A. Multiplying the previous product
by this inequality proves the bound, including factors equal to1.

Let E_pre,alpha be the slow energy of the prefix ending at j, and let
E_tail,alpha be its slow energy on the word(h_(j+1),...,h_(K-1)) with
local partial sums beginning at zero. By(17),

    E_pre,alpha>=0, E_tail,alpha>=0,               (38)

using respectively the global prefix bounds and(29).

Subtracting this coordinate's full bypass charge2H_cut*A_K,alpha
from the first K slow terms has an exact algebraic description.
Decrease every value at indices i<=j by A_K,alpha and leave all later
values unchanged. The change in V is exactly-2H_cut*A_K,alpha.
The new value difference between j and K is the direct-edge value

    f_j'-f_K=alpha*u^2*(p_j^2-p_K^2)/2
                          -U*(p_j-p_K)^2,

or equivalently

    2(f_j'-f_K)/a_j^2
           =1+2(1/alpha-1)*q-(2/alpha-1)*q^2.

These temporary comparison values need not be interpolable; this step
is ONLY an algebraic bound on the cost of the explicit bypass path.
Actual attaining values have already been proved legal in9.1.

Split the energy of these comparison values at j and j+1. Restoring
the prefix terminal term contributes P(P+1)*a_j^2, the step r
contributes r*(1-r)*a_j^2, and the shift in value references contributes
2H_cut*(f_j'-f_K). The final gradient coefficient differs from that
of E_tail,alpha by-H_cut*(H_cut+2T_neg+1)*a_j^2*q^2. Combining them gives

    first_K_slow_terms-2H_cut*A_K,alpha
      =E_pre,alpha+E_tail,alpha+H_cut*a_j^2*J(q),
    J(q)=P+2-r+2q*[(1/alpha-1)
                              -(1/alpha+H_cut/2+T_neg)*q]. (39)

This also checks the prefix weights explicitly, instead of treating
the reversal as a new initial point without paying its earlier mass.

The function J is concave on the interval[0,q_bar], so it is at least
the smaller of its two endpoint values. The first is

    J(0)=P+2-r>=0.

For the second, use q_bar=(1-r*alpha)/(1+alpha*T_neg) to obtain

    J(q_bar)=P+2-r+2(r-1)*q_bar-H_cut*q_bar^2
            =(1-q_bar)*[P+2-r+H_cut*q_bar]>=0.     (40)

The equality uses H_cut-(P+2-r)=2(r-1). All its factors have the
required signs by the GLOBAL old prefix bound at the reversal.
There is a useful quantitative margin. Since q_bar<=1-r*alpha and
0<=r*alpha<=1, BOTH endpoints in(40) and J(0) are at least
r*alpha*(P+2-r). Concavity therefore gives

    J(q)>=r*alpha*(P+2-r).

Equations(38)--(40) prove

    L_alpha>=2H_cut*A_K,alpha
                   +H_cut*r*(P+2-r)*alpha^3*u^2*p_j^2.

This holds for EVERY permitted slow curvature. Summing over modes
proves the quantitative first inequality in(34). Since B_K=0, the
k=K path in(31) gives2H_cut*delta_cut<=X_all, proving ALL of(34).
Combining the same margin with(32) also gives the explicit bound

    V_min>=E_pos+E_neg+H_cut*(P+2-r)*
                 [(beta*v*z_j)^2+r*sum_q alpha_q^3*u_q^2*p_(j,q)^2]. (41)

For j=0 this recovers the strict initial bound(6) for EVERY finite
delay, without extending the degree-five polynomial calculation.

No number of intervening negative steps, amplitude optimization,
nonseparable choice of values, or choice among the competing crossing
paths can defeat this bound within(28)--(29). It is a complete
redundancy theorem for the entire newly stated family.

### 9.4 Final scope and the original four-step interface

The result includes every initial single reversal followed by an
arbitrarily delayed annihilation: then r<=2 by the global bound, so
b<=r<=2 and(29) is automatic. It also includes a reversal at ANY later
index with killing step b<=2, regardless of the size of r permitted
by r<=P_j+2. It includes further b>2 cases whenever the explicit
standalone negative-block bounds(29) hold.

For n=4, all possible reversal/killing locations satisfy0<=j<K-1<4.
In particular the previously open delayed middle pattern
h=(a,r,c,b), j=1, K=4 is covered whenever a<=b<=2, b<=r<=a+2,
0<=c<=b, together with the already stated global bounds. The genuinely
delayed interior has a<b, r>b and0<c<b. No capacity is held fixed:
C=1+a+r+c+b can lie in the high-capacity range C>=193/25.

Zero steps at any position, repeated fast or full points, earlier
annihilation on a boundary, vanished amplitudes, zero products and
unit curvature remain in the proof. Every ordered pair and all original
formal gradient coordinates are retained; no quotient of the ambient
R^5 certificate space has been taken. The original h/Lambda/exact-flow/
full-PSD problem of04219554e41d9750 is preserved.

The exact gain2H_cut*delta_cut can be positive, as the explicit
three-path interior of Section1 demonstrates. The budget(39)--(40)
shows why it supplies no new exclusion here. Modes nonzero at the
terminal, multiple reversals or multiple changing modes, and suffixes
failing(29) remain outside the final theorem. General full-cone seed
feasibility and the original asymptotic exact-profile target stay open.

All statements in this extension remain UNVERIFIED joint drafts.
No fact has been submitted or represented as verifier-accepted.

# Independent exact tangent audit and a full local capacity theorem

Status: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact submission or formal
gate was allocated or used. The exact arithmetic readback is an independently
implemented finite check, not verifier acceptance. Every new lemma and the local
theorem below belong to this one joint draft; no earlier unverified finding is
used as an accepted premise. The original all-word exact-profile goal is OPEN.

Worker max, round58, under TASK.md and master_guidance d68a7dd9e0669f07.
The later guidance b5768bac207b0736 explicitly preserves this round and confirms
that its sole exact job is consumed; no new allocation is inferred.
Plan: 24d816a4ff465743. Exactly one mathematical job was run. Its five-second
slot was fully reserved before launch; the public ledger is closed.

Part A verdict: the complete frozen first-order object survives the independent
original-variable exact readback. All63 coefficients, all36 strict sign prices,
all14 zero box prices, the svec convention, and the full dual LDL product pass.

Part B verdict at DRAFT level: the full zero-capacity tangent consists only of
the zero direction. This yields a genuine neighborhood theorem for EVERY full
original certificate, with a strict linear capacity loss and a uniform bound
on coefficient displacement. The proof rederives base uniqueness and local
boundedness jointly and controls the complete nonlinear remainder. Neither a
global seven-step capacity ceiling nor the original minimax problem is settled.

## 1. Objects, accepted input, and the proposed theorem

All vectors and matrices below are real unless exact field data are specified.
Write q58=sqrt(2), lambda=1+q58, u=q58-1=1/lambda, and

    hbar=(q58,2,q58,1+lambda,q58,2,q58),  Abar=lambda^3.

For h=(h0,...,h6) in [0,infinity)^7, let s=sum_i h_i and A(h)=1+s.
Let e be the all-ones column in R8, and let e_i be its coordinate columns,
indexed0,...,7. For any zero-diagonal real8-by8 array X define

    L(X)=diag(Xe)-X.

The prefix matrix T(h) has diagonal1, entry h_j at i,j when j<i, and zero
above its diagonal. Put

    omega(h)=(-2h0,...,-2h6,2s)^T,
    D(h)=diag(h0(2-h0),...,h6(2-h6),-s(s+2)),
    M(h,Lambda)=D(h)+sym(L(Lambda)T(h)),
    sym(K)=(K+K^T)/2.

A full certificate means precisely

    Lambda_ii=0, Lambda_ij>=0 for ALL ordered i!=j,
    L(Lambda)^T e=omega(h), M(h,Lambda)>=0 on the ENTIRE R8.       (1)

All56 ordered coefficients and all8 independent formal gradients remain.
No support, symmetry, rank, or equality between formal gradients is imposed
in(1). Nonzero singular PSD remainders are included.

Accepted fact04219554e41d9750, proof Sections10--11, supplies the original
primitive coefficient identity and the full original-coordinate composition
identity. For a normalized convex1-smooth objective F, write g_i=grad F(x_i)
and f_i=F(x_i); Gram(g0,...,g7) has entries <g_i,g_j>. Its Section10 uses

    x_i=x0-sum_(k<i) h_k g_k,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<7)h_i(f_i-f7)
          +sum_(i<7)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g7||^2,

and establishes, under the flow in(1),

    V_h+sum_(i!=j) Lambda_ij Q_ij
       =trace(M(h,Lambda) Gram(g0,...,g7)).                    (2)

This accepted fact is the only non-elementary accepted input used here.
Its original all-function interpretation is retained; no converse about all
primitive schedules is asserted. The exact algebraic statement of its join
identity needed below is recalled in Section5.

Define Lambdabar by the following twenty entries, with every other entry0:

    01:2                    10:2lambda                13:2lambda
    21:2lambda*q58            23:2lambda^2+2lambda*q58    31:2lambda
    32:2lambda^3            37:2lambda^2              43:2lambda*q58
    45:2lambda^2            47:2lambda*q58              53:4lambda
    54:2lambda^3            57:2lambda^3+4lambda      63:2lambda*q58
    65:2lambda^3*q58          67:2lambda^4+2lambda^3*q58+2lambda*q58
    73:2lambda              75:2lambda^3              76:2lambda^5.

These are positive. The independently rebuilt original flow is exact and
M(hbar,Lambdabar)=0. A second algebraic justification is to join two singleton
q58 words at bridge2 and join two resulting three-step words at bridge1+lambda,
using the accepted composition identity; each right scaling is lambda^2 and
each extra interpolation factor is lambda^2-1=2lambda.

PROPOSED LOCAL THEOREM. There exist epsilon>0 and c>0 such that for EVERY
h in[0,infinity)^7 and EVERY array Lambda satisfying(1), if
||h-hbar||_2<epsilon, then

    A(h) <= Abar-c*sqrt(||h-hbar||_2^2+||Lambda-Lambdabar||_F^2). (3)

Here ||.||_F is the Frobenius matrix norm. In particular A(h)<=lambda^3;
equality requires h=hbar and Lambda=Lambdabar. Moreover each such array obeys

    ||Lambda-Lambdabar||_F <= (sqrt(7)/c)*||h-hbar||_2.          (4)

This is a bound relative to the base for all selections, not an assertion of
uniqueness of certificates away from the base or of a Lipschitz selection map
between arbitrary pairs of nearby words. A precise positive definition of c
is given in Section8. The proof establishes existence of epsilon; no numerical
radius is asserted.

## 2. Complete linearization, common null, and exact dual identity

Let E58 be the twenty ordered pairs listed above and Z0 the other36 ordered
distinct pairs. Write Tbal58=T(hbar) and Lbar=L(Lambdabar). Tbal58 is exactly the frozen candidate's prefix matrix T0. The separate name
avoids collision with the benchmark recurrence map called T0 in the glossary.
For p in R7 and a zero-diagonal Y in R^(8-by-8), put

    S=sum_(i=0)^6 p_i,
    Tp_ij=p_j if j<i, and0 otherwise,
    op=(-2p0,...,-2p6,2S)^T,
    D1(p)=diag(2(1-hbar_i)p_i for i<7,-2Abar*S),
    W(p,Y)=D1(p)+sym(Lbar*Tp+L(Y)*Tbal58),
    f(p,Y)=L(Y)^T e-op, n(p,Y)=W(p,Y)e.                     (5)

The FULL linearized cone is

    C58={(p,Y): f(p,Y)=0, Y_ij>=0 on Z0, W(p,Y)>=0 on R8}.    (6)

The twenty Y entries on E58 are free. No p-box occurs in(6).

For any h and any real Lambda with flow, L(Lambda)e=0 and

    omega(h)^T T(h)e=s^2+sum_i h_i^2,
    e^T D(h)e=-s^2-sum_i h_i^2,

so e^T M(h,Lambda)e=0. To check the first equality directly, the entries of
T(h)e are1+sum_(j<i)h_j; expanding the product uses
2sum_(j<i)h_i h_j=s^2-sum_i h_i^2. Differentiation along the exactly flowing
linear families in(5) gives e^T W e=0 when f=0. If W is PSD, expansion of
(e+t z)^T W(e+t z) for arbitrary real t and z forces z^T W e=0, hence n=0.

Let V be the8-by7 matrix whose columns are e_i-e_7 for i<7. Its columns span
e-perp. Put B=V^T W V and G=V^T V=I7+11^T, where1 is the all-ones column
of length7. When We=0, every vector is a multiple of e plus a vector Vz;
therefore W>=0 on R8 if and only if B>=0 on R7. The same decomposition shows
W>=m(I8-ee^T/8) if and only if B>=mG for any real m. Thus the common-null
congruence preserves the original PSD condition without discarding a gradient.

Appendix A contains the complete exact field data for vectors alpha,beta in
Q(q58)^8, a symmetric Z in Q(q58)^(7-by-7), and prices sigma_ij for ij in Z0.
Alpha and beta are respectively the first and last eight equality_prices.
The 28 PSD_svec values are in upper-triangular column-major order. A diagonal
entry is Z_ii; an off-diagonal entry is q58*Z_ij. The precise identity is

    -S = trace(ZB)+sum_(ij in Z0)sigma_ij Y_ij
                  -alpha^T f-beta^T n.                     (7)

It holds for EVERY p and EVERY real zero-diagonal Y, before imposing(6).
All14 box prices are exactly0. Consequently(7) is homogeneous and applies
to arbitrary p; no normalization from the discovery optimization survives.

Here are also the63 coefficient equations in direct algebraic form. Let
H=VZV^T, K=H-sym(beta e^T), let t_i be the transpose of row i of Tbal58, and
let r_ij=t_i-t_j. The unit Y_ij contribution is

    f=e_i-e_j, W=sym(e_i r_ij^T).

Thus the56 equations are

    alpha_i-alpha_j-(K r_ij)_i=0       for ij in E58,
    alpha_i-alpha_j-(K r_ij)_i=sigma_ij for ij in Z0.          (8)

For each i<7 let C_i have entry1 in column i strictly below its diagonal,
and all other entries0. The unit p_i contribution is

    N_i=2(1-hbar_i)e_i e_i^T-2Abar e_7e_7^T+sym(Lbar C_i),
    f=2e_i-2e_7.

The other seven equations are

    2(alpha_i-alpha_7)-trace(K N_i)=1, i=0,...,6.             (9)

The independent executable in Appendix B did NOT use(8)--(9) as a shortcut.
It rebuilt(5) for each of the63 unit inputs from the literal original hbar,
Lambdabar, prefix matrix and row-sum Laplacian. For every input it checked(7),
trace(ZB)=trace(HW), the q58-weighted svec inner product, and

    e^T W e=f^T Tbal58 e.

Its complete result retains all original f,W,We,B arrays. Every coefficient
residual is the rational pair(0,0). Source, input and expected-check hashes
were frozen before the sole charged job. No supplied passed flag or supplied
stationarity residual was used; no author checker was imported.

Field arithmetic uses pairs(r,s) representing r+s*q58, with
(r,s)*(a,b)=(ra+2sb,rb+sa). Inversion multiplies by the conjugate and divides
by r^2-2s^2. Every denominator check is exact. For signs, if r,s have the
same nonzero sign use that sign; if they have opposite signs use
sign(r)*sign(r^2-2s^2). Zero-coordinate cases are immediate. Appendix A's
36 sigma values all have sign+1 by this exact rule.

Appendix A also gives Z=R diag(d0,...,d6) R^T. The independent readback checks
that R is unit lower triangular, every one of the49 product entries is Z's
corresponding entry, and all seven d_i have exact sign+1. Thus Z is positive
definite. No floating eigenvalue is a premise. Let r_k be column k of R, a
vector of length7. Since

    trace(ZB)=sum_k d_k r_k^T B r_k,

this trace is nonnegative for PSD B. If it vanishes, each of the seven
nonnegative quadratic forms vanishes; the PSD argument used for the common
null then makes B annihilate the basis formed by the columns of R. Hence B=0.

For(6), f=n=0, B>=0, and every active Y is nonnegative. Identity(7) proves
S<=0. If S=0, strict positivity of every sigma and positive definiteness of Z
give

    Y_ij=0 for every ij in Z0, B=0, W=0.                    (10)

Up to this point a nonzero equality direction has NOT been excluded. The
next two sections establish its exclusion by original-coordinate algebra.

## 3. A three-step zero-remainder derivative calculation

Use local indices0,1,2,3 and the three-step base word(q58,2,q58), of capacity
lambda^2. In this section L,T,omega,D,M are defined exactly as in Section1
with three steps and four gradients, so the terminal index is3 and e is the
all-ones column of R4. Its base array has seven positive entries

    01=2, 10=13=31=2lambda, 21=2lambda*q58,
    23=2lambda^2+2lambda*q58, 32=2lambda^3,                    (11)

and all other entries0. Its remainder is0 by the accepted singleton join.
Permit derivatives on exactly these seven pairs AND the additional pair03.
The latter derivative is an unrestricted real number. Write r0,r1,r2 for
the physical derivatives, y_ij for the coefficient derivatives, and assume
the four original flow derivatives hold and the full4-by4 remainder
derivative Wloc is0. We prove all r and y are0.

Only the pairs in(11) and03 are used in the following displayed entries.
The entry02 of L(Lambda)T is -h2*Lambda03, whereas its entry20 is0.
Therefore

    (Wloc)_02=-q58*y03/2,

which forces y03=0. The flow at0 and the entries00 and01 now give

    y01-y10=-2r0,
    -2q*r0-u*y01=0,
    -y01+2lambda*r0+u*y10=0.                               (12)

The first two equations give y10=y01+2r0 and y01=-2q*lambda*r0.
Substitution in the last equation yields

    [-2q*lambda*(u-1)+4q]r0=4lambda*r0=0.

Thus r0=y01=y10=0. Entries11 and13 and flow at1 give, in order,

    y13=-2(1+lambda)r1,
    y31=y13-2lambda*r1,
    y21=2(1+lambda)r1.                                     (13)

For clarity, these equations come from

    (Wloc)_11=-2(1+lambda)r1-y13,
    2(Wloc)_13=-y13+y31+2lambda*r1,
    y13-y21-y31=-2r1.

The12 entry, obtained from -h2*Lambda13+(h1-1)*Lambda21, gives

    0=-q58*y13-2lambda*r2+y21+2lambda*q58*r1
      =4lambda^2*r1-2lambda*r2.

Consequently r2=2lambda*r1.

A scalar identity supplies the last physical equation without eliminating
any formal gradient. For a word h of length3 let v=T(h)^(-1)e. Its entries
satisfy v0=1 and v_(i+1)=(1-h_i)v_i. Since L(Lambda)e=0,

    v^T M(h,Lambda)v=v^T D(h)v=1-A(h)^2*v3^2.               (14)

The second equality follows from
h_i(2-h_i)v_i^2=v_i^2-v_(i+1)^2 and -s(s+2)=1-A^2.
At the base, M=0, A=lambda^2 and v3=-u^2. Differentiating(14) therefore
gives the necessary equality

    (r0+r1+r2)/lambda^2+lambda*(r0+r2)+r1=0.               (15)

The moving-vector derivative contributes0 because the base matrix is0.
Substituting r0=0 and r2=2lambda*r1 into(15) gives

    [(1+2lambda)/lambda^2+2lambda^2+1]r1=0.

The bracket is strictly positive, so r1=r2=0. Equations(13) give
y13=y31=y21=0. Entry22 now reads y21-u*y23=0, so y23=0; flow at2 gives
y21+y23-y32=0, so y32=0. Together with(12) and y03=0, this proves that
the full derivative is zero. No sign assumption on these eight derivatives
was needed. This additional03 entry is retained because it arises when the
right child of the seven-step array is extracted.

## 4. Exact central-cut algebra to first order

Assume p,Y satisfy f=0, W=0, and Y=0 on Z0. Thus all coefficient derivatives
are supported on E58. Introduce the REAL, not assumed feasible, families

    h(t)=hbar+t*p, Lambda(t)=Lambdabar+t*Y.

Their flow holds exactly for every real t. Their full remainder is O(t^2),
entry by entry, because its constant and linear coefficients are0. Here
O(t^2) means a function with absolute value bounded by a fixed multiple of
t^2 for sufficiently small |t|; the constants may depend on the fixed p,Y.
All computations in this section retain constant and first-order terms.

Let P58={0,1,2,3}, Bset={4,5,6,7}, and let eP,eB be their indicator columns
in R8. Define capacities and bridge excess from these ACTUAL physical steps:

    a(t)=1+h0+h1+h2, b(t)=1+h4+h5+h6, d(t)=h3-1.

Write h_left=(h0,h1,h2) and h_right=(h4,h5,h6). At t=0, a=b=lambda^2 and
d=lambda. The only P58-to-Bset coefficient is
N(t)=Lambda37. The only Bset-to-P58 coefficients are mu_j(t)=Lambda_(j,3)
for j=4,5,6,7. This support follows from(10) and the displayed E58, rather
than from an ansatz about general feasible certificates.

Let LambdaL be the P58-by-P58 subarray, interpreted as a three-step certificate
array for h0,h1,h2. Its original flow holds exactly: the first three rows
have no crossing edge, and the terminal flow follows by summing all four
row-minus-column sums. Direct matrix multiplication gives

    M(h,Lambda)_(P58,P58)
      =M(h_left,LambdaL)+(a^2-d^2-d*N)diag(0,0,0,1).        (16)

Indeed the only crossing contribution within this principal block is
N*(T_3-T_7) in row3; it changes only entry33, by -d*N. The parent-versus-child
D difference at that entry is (1-d^2)-(1-a^2)=a^2-d^2. The other entries
agree. Thus every derivative entry of M(h_left,LambdaL) except possibly33
is0. The scalar flow identity e^T M e=0 from Section2 also holds for this
child at every t, forcing the remaining derivative entry to vanish. Hence

    M(h_left,LambdaL)=O(t^2), a^2-d^2-d*N=O(t^2).            (17)

For j in Bset, direct multiplication by eP yields

    2(M eP)_j=d*mu_j-N*h_j, j=4,5,6,
    2(M eP)_7=d*mu_7-N.                                  (18)

To see every contribution in(18), T eP equals a+d on Bset and a at index3,
so (L T eP)_j=d*mu_j. On Bset, L^T eP has only the entry -N at7.
Upper triangular multiplication by T^T therefore contributes -N*h_j
for j<7 and -N at7. D eP vanishes on Bset. This proves(18) without
any positivity or remainder-rank hypothesis.

Since M=O(t^2) and d stays positive, (18) gives

    mu_j=(N/d)h_j+O(t^2), j=4,5,6;
    mu_7=N/d+O(t^2); sum_j mu_j=N*b/d+O(t^2).

Summing the exact original flow over P58 gives

    sum_j mu_j-N=2(a+d).

It follows that

    N(b-d)=2d(a+d)+O(t^2).                                (19)

Multiplying(19) by d and using d*N=a^2-d^2+O(t^2) from(17), then dividing
by the positive a+d, gives

    (a-d)(b-d)=2d^2+O(t^2),
    a*b-d(a+b)-d^2=O(t^2).                                (20)

This saturated-bridge relation has been DERIVED for the zero-remainder
derivative; it was not imposed on the full tangent cone.

## 5. Extraction of both complete children and the full equality kernel

For positive a,b near lambda^2 define the positive root

    dhat=(-(a+b)+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,
    Cjoin=a+b+dhat, kappa=(Cjoin/b)^2.

These functions are twice continuously differentiable for a,b in a sufficiently
small positive neighborhood of their base values, so all derivatives below exist.

The identity a*b=dhat*Cjoin is exact. Equation(20) and the factorization

    a*b-d(a+b)-d^2=-(d-dhat)(a+b+d+dhat)

show d-dhat=O(t^2). Consequently (17)--(19) yield

    N=dhat*(kappa-1)+O(t^2),
    mu_j=(kappa-1)h_j+O(t^2), j=4,5,6,
    mu_7=kappa-1+O(t^2).                                  (21)

For the first equality use N=(a^2-d^2)/d+O(t^2), replace d by dhat, and
use a/dhat=Cjoin/b. All divisions have nonzero positive base values.

Define a real child array on Bset, with local indices0,...,3, by

    LambdaR_ij=Lambda_(i,j)/kappa
       -(1-1/kappa)h_i*1_(i<7,j=7), i,j in Bset, i!=j.     (22)

Keep its diagonal0. No sign assumption on LambdaR is made. Its nonterminal
flow is -2h_j+O(t^2): the RR subarray's flow at j<7 equals -2h_j-mu_j;
after division and subtraction in(22) it is

    (-2h_j-mu_j)/kappa-(1-1/kappa)h_j=-2h_j+O(t^2).

The terminal flow then follows by summing the four row-minus-column sums.

Here is the exact full-space composition identity used to extract remainders.
For our two physical child words of capacities a,b and bridge1+dhat, the
bridge index is3 and the terminal index is7. Let j range over4,5,6, the right
child's nonterminal indices. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750, Section11, gives

    V_parent=V_left+kappa*V_right-(kappa-1)*Ejoin,
    Ejoin=sum_j h_j Q_(j,3)+Q_(7,3)+dhat Q_(3,7)
                                             +sum_j h_j Q_(j,7). (23)

Its coefficient proof uses dhat*(a+b+dhat)=ab and kappa=((a+b+dhat)/b)^2.
Thus the joined multiplier array consists of LambdaL on P58, kappa*LambdaR
on Bset plus (kappa-1)h_i into7 from each nonterminal right index, and
the cross multipliers dhat(kappa-1), (kappa-1)h_j, kappa-1. Under child flow
the FULL remainder is the disjoint original-coordinate block sum
M_left plus kappa*M_right. This is an algebraic coefficient identity; its
validity does not require the child arrays to be nonnegative or their
remainders to be PSD. In the current application child flow holds through
first order, which is precisely the order at which we use this identity.
More explicitly, for an arbitrary real child array with flow defect
xi=L(Lambda)^T e-omega, collecting the same coefficients as in(2) gives

    V+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=trace(M Gram)
                           +sum_i xi_i(f_i-||g_i||^2/2).

The function-value coefficients gain xi_i and the gradient diagonal gains
-xi_i/2. This exact formula shows explicitly that an O(t^2) flow defect
cannot alter the constant or linear matrix coefficients in the join identity.

Replace the bridge by1+dhat and crossings by the exact expressions in(23).
By(21) these changes are O(t^2); the internal arrays are unchanged. Combining
the original identity(2) with(23), or just collecting its gradient coefficients,
therefore gives

    M(h,Lambda)=M(h_left,LambdaL) direct-sum
                   kappa*M(h_right,LambdaR)+O(t^2).        (24)

The flow errors in(22) are O(t^2), so cannot alter a constant or linear
coefficient in this equality. Equations(17) and(24) imply the right child
remainder is also O(t^2). Thus both children have exact base flow, zero
base remainder, correct derivative flow, and zero derivative remainder.

The left child's derivative support is the seven pairs in(11). The right
child's support is those same seven pairs plus03. Explicitly its original
internal allowed pairs are45,47,54,57,65,67,75,76; the subtractions in(22)
only affect47,57,67. Hence no other pair is introduced. At the base,
kappa=lambda^2 and1-1/kappa=2/lambda. Substitution in(22) gives

    LambdaR_01=2, LambdaR_03=0,
    LambdaR_10=LambdaR_13=LambdaR_31=2lambda,
    LambdaR_21=2lambda*q58,
    LambdaR_23=2lambda^2+2lambda*q58,
    LambdaR_32=2lambda^3.

For example its03 entry is (2lambda*q58)/lambda^2-(2/lambda)q58=0;
its13 entry is (2lambda^3+4lambda)/lambda^2-(2/lambda)*2=2lambda;
its23 entry is
(2lambda^4+2lambda^3*q58+2lambda*q58)/lambda^2-(2/lambda)q58
=2lambda^2+2lambda*q58.
The other five listed entries are obtained by division by lambda^2.

Apply Section3 separately to these two complete four-gradient derivatives.
It gives

    p0=p1=p2=p4=p5=p6=0,
    LambdaL'(0)=LambdaR'(0)=0.

Differentiating(20) now leaves

    -(2lambda^2+2lambda)*p3=0,

so p3=0. Then a'=b'=dhat'=kappa'=0. Equations(21) give N'=mu_j'=0,
and differentiating(22) gives all original RR coefficient derivatives0.
The PP and crossing derivatives are already0. Consequently

    f=0, W=0, Y|Z0=0  ==>  p=0 and Y=0.                   (25)

This conclusion was obtained by textual exact algebra only, with no rank
computation, machine elimination or extra mathematical job. Together with(10)
it identifies the FULL cone's zero-capacity equality set:

    (p,Y) in C58 and S=0  iff  (p,Y)=(0,0).                (26)

## 6. Uniqueness at the base, rederived from the same joint identity

Let Lambda be ANY full feasible array at hbar. Put p=0 and
Y=Lambda-Lambdabar. The full flow gives f=0. Because p=0, no nonlinear term
occurs: W(0,Y)=M(hbar,Lambda). On each of the36 zero base entries,
Y_ij=Lambda_ij>=0. Thus (0,Y) belongs to C58 and has S=0. Equation(26)
gives Y=0. We have proved

    Lambda feasible at hbar ==> Lambda=Lambdabar.          (27)

This proof covers every base certificate and every PSD rank. It does not
assume the all-base uniqueness claim of the earlier b937bb05ba1645b1 draft.

## 7. Uniform bounds and convergence for arbitrary nearby certificate choices

Work inside the compact cube h_i in[1,4], which contains hbar in its interior.
Consider the ONE scalar function F(x)=k*x^2/2, with k=1/8, minimizer0 and
x0=1. Its actual GD positions satisfy

    x_i=product_(j<i)(1-h_j/8), 1/2<=1-h_j/8<=7/8.

They strictly decrease and stay positive. If0<=i<j<=7, then

    x_i-x_j >= x_i-x_(i+1)=(h_i/8)x_i >=2^-9,

because i<=6 and x_i>=2^-6. Direct expansion of all ordered residuals gives

    Q_ij=-k(1-k)(x_i-x_j)^2/2 <=-7/2^25 for i!=j.           (28)

This checks every original ordered finite pair for the same actual quadratic.
Its gradients are g_i=k*x_i. In V_h, all terms h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2 and the
terminal negative square are nonpositive, whereas f_i-f7<=f_i<=k/2.
Since sum_i h_i<=28, we have V_h<=28k=7/2. Applying(2) to any feasible
Lambda, using full PSD and all its nonnegative entries, gives

    (7/2^25)sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij
       <=sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij(-Q_ij)<=V_h<=7/2.

Hence EVERY certificate in this cube satisfies

    sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij<=2^24.                             (29)

The bound is intentionally coarse and purely analytic. In particular the
entire set of coefficient arrays is bounded independently of their selection.

Now take any sequence h_k->hbar and choose any feasible Lambda_k. Eventually
the words lie in the cube. A subsequence of the arrays converges by(29).
The limit is nonnegative with zero diagonal, has the exact base flow, and has
a PSD base remainder by continuity: each quadratic form is a limit of
nonnegative quadratic forms. Equation(27) identifies the limit as Lambdabar.
If uniform convergence over all selections failed, one could choose such a
sequence with ||Lambda_k-Lambdabar||_F bounded away from0; its convergent
subsequence would contradict(27). Therefore

    h->hbar through feasible words implies Lambda->Lambdabar
    uniformly over ALL feasible choices of Lambda.         (30)

No estimate on ||Lambda-Lambdabar||/||h-hbar|| was used. Such quotients are
allowed to be unbounded at this stage of the argument.

## 8. Exact nonlinear remainder and strict local capacity loss

For arbitrary finite displacements p=h-hbar, Y=Lambda-Lambdabar, the full
polynomial expansion is EXACT:

    M(h,Lambda)=W(p,Y)+R2(p,Y),
    R2(p,Y)=diag(-p0^2,...,-p6^2,-S^2)+sym(L(Y)Tp).          (31)

Flow is exactly f(p,Y)=0. Every original sign is retained:
Y_ij>=0 on Z0 and Lambdabar_ij+Y_ij>=0 on E58. There are no omitted terms in
(31). In particular the mixed coefficient-step term is retained, and higher
orders arising from a nonlinear choice of h,Lambda are already covered by
this exact displacement formula.

Put ||(p,Y)||_* = sqrt(||p||_2^2+||Y||_F^2). Cauchy--Schwarz gives

    ||L(Y)||_F <=(sqrt(7)+1)||Y||_F,
    ||Tp||_F <=sqrt(7)||p||_2,
    ||diag(-p_i^2,-S^2)||_F <=||p||_2^2+S^2<=8||p||_2^2.

The first bound uses seven off-diagonal entries in each row. The second uses
||Tp||_F^2=sum_(j=0)^6(7-j)p_j^2. Symmetrization does not increase Frobenius
norm, and the Frobenius norm of a product is bounded by the product of norms.
Consequently

    ||R2(p,Y)||_F <=8||p||_2^2+(7+sqrt(7))||p||_2||Y||_F
                   <=13||(p,Y)||_*^2.                     (32)

Define the compact set

    K58={(p,Y) in C58: ||(p,Y)||_*=1}.

It is closed because flow and W are linear and the sign and PSD cones are
closed; it lies on a finite-dimensional unit sphere. It is nonempty, as the
following actual feasible path confirms without a new computation. For
0<=t<q58 let x=q58-t. The singleton word x with multipliers2 and2(1+x) has the
full2-by2 remainder(2-x^2)[[1,-1],[-1,1]], which is PSD. This follows by
substituting L=[[2,-2],[-2(1+x),2(1+x)]] and T=[[1,0],[x,1]] into D+sym(LT).
Join two copies at bridge
1+u(1+x), then two resulting children at bridge2+x. The accepted join has
positive coefficients, fixed right scaling lambda^2 at both levels, and
actual word

    (x,1+u(1+x),x,2+x,x,1+u(1+x),x).

Its base array is Lambdabar; all coefficients are differentiable in t.
Its tangent has p=(-1,-u,-1,-1,-1,-u,-1) and S=-lambda^2, so is nonzero
and belongs to C58. Normalize it to obtain a point of K58.

Equations(7),(26) show -S>0 everywhere on K58. Hence

    m58=min_((p,Y) in K58)(-sum_i p_i)>0, c=m58/2>0.        (33)

The minimum is a mathematical definition, not a solver output or a new
computation. Its positivity follows from compactness: a sequence of unit
cone points with -S->0 would have a unit limit in the equality set(26).

We now prove that all sufficiently small FEASIBLE joint displacements obey

    -S >=c||(p,Y)||_*.                                    (34)

If this failed, choose nonzero feasible displacements (p_k,Y_k) with
t_k=||(p_k,Y_k)||_*->0 and -S_k<c*t_k. Normalize
(p_k,Y_k)=t_k*(p_hat_k,Y_hat_k). A subsequence of the unit normalized pairs
converges to a pair(p_hat,Y_hat) of norm1. Exact flow and all36 active signs
pass to the limit. Homogeneity of(31) gives

    M(hbar+p_k,Lambdabar+Y_k)/t_k
       =W(p_hat_k,Y_hat_k)+t_k*R2(p_hat_k,Y_hat_k)>=0.

By(32), the second term tends to0 in norm. Thus W(p_hat,Y_hat)>=0 on the
FULL R8, so the unit limit lies in K58. The assumed failure of(34) gives
-sum_i p_hat_i<=c=m58/2, contradicting(33). This proves the existence of
a joint radius r58>0 on which(34) holds for every feasible pair.

Finally use the uniform convergence(30) and h->hbar to choose epsilon>0
such that EVERY feasible array above a word with ||h-hbar||_2<epsilon
satisfies ||(h-hbar,Lambda-Lambdabar)||_*<r58. The cube condition can be
ensured by making epsilon smaller, since hbar is in its interior. Equation
(34) is then exactly the asserted capacity bound(3). At the base it holds
with both sides0. Cauchy--Schwarz also gives

    c||Lambda-Lambdabar||_F <=Abar-A(h)
                          =-sum_i(h_i-hbar_i)
                          <=sqrt(7)||h-hbar||_2,

which proves(4).

This normalization uses the JOINT displacement, so it never assumes bounded
physical coefficient difference quotients. Singular PSD faces and zero leading
signs cause no gap: PSD is passed to a limit after the full exact quadratic
error vanishes, and equality of capacity at a unit limiting tangent is
excluded by the explicit kernel calculation. No Hessian coercivity, smooth
certificate selection, or sufficiency of a truncated second-order path is
assumed.

## Appendix B. Frozen independent exact-readback source

The input required by this source is exactly Appendix A, saved as
frozen_exact_data.json. Its literal mathematical definitions and arithmetic
operations are included for independent review. The source was run once only.

```python
"""One frozen exact readback. No optimizer, author checker, or rank calculation."""
import json
import sys
import time
import traceback
from fractions import Fraction
from pathlib import Path

START = time.perf_counter()
ROOT = Path('/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/tangent_neighborhood_20261001')
RESULT = {'status': 'running', 'checks': [], 'basis_readbacks': []}


class Q:
    __slots__ = ('a', 'b')

    def __init__(self, a=0, b=0):
        self.a, self.b = Fraction(a), Fraction(b)

    @staticmethod
    def of(x):
        if isinstance(x, Q):
            return x
        if isinstance(x, list):
            return Q(*x)
        return Q(x)

    def __add__(self, other):
        other = Q.of(other)
        return Q(self.a + other.a, self.b + other.b)

    __radd__ = __add__

    def __neg__(self):
        return Q(-self.a, -self.b)

    def __sub__(self, other):
        return self + (-Q.of(other))

    def __rsub__(self, other):
        return Q.of(other) - self

    def __mul__(self, other):
        other = Q.of(other)
        return Q(self.a * other.a + 2 * self.b * other.b,
                 self.a * other.b + self.b * other.a)

    __rmul__ = __mul__

    def __truediv__(self, other):
        other = Q.of(other)
        den = other.a * other.a - 2 * other.b * other.b
        if den == 0:
            raise ZeroDivisionError('exact zero field divisor')
        return Q((self.a * other.a - 2 * self.b * other.b) / den,
                 (self.b * other.a - self.a * other.b) / den)

    def __pow__(self, exponent):
        result = Q(1)
        for _ in range(exponent):
            result = result * self
        return result

    def __bool__(self):
        return bool(self.a or self.b)

    def __eq__(self, other):
        other = Q.of(other)
        return self.a == other.a and self.b == other.b

    def encoded(self):
        return [str(self.a), str(self.b)]

    def sign(self):
        a, b = self.a, self.b
        if a == 0:
            return (b > 0) - (b < 0)
        if b == 0:
            return (a > 0) - (a < 0)
        if (a > 0) == (b > 0):
            return 1 if a > 0 else -1
        difference = a * a - 2 * b * b
        return ((a > 0) - (a < 0)) * ((difference > 0) - (difference < 0))


def encode(x):
    if isinstance(x, Q):
        return x.encoded()
    if isinstance(x, list):
        return [encode(v) for v in x]
    if isinstance(x, dict):
        return {k: encode(v) for k, v in x.items()}
    return x


def matrix(rows, cols):
    return [[Q() for _ in range(cols)] for _ in range(rows)]


def multiply(A, B):
    C = matrix(len(A), len(B[0]))
    for i, row in enumerate(A):
        for k, value in enumerate(row):
            if value:
                for j, other in enumerate(B[k]):
                    if other:
                        C[i][j] = C[i][j] + value * other
    return C


def laplacian(X):
    A = [[-x for x in row] for row in X]
    for i in range(len(X)):
        A[i][i] = sum(X[i], Q())
    return A


def check(name, condition, detail=None):
    RESULT['checks'].append({'name': name, 'passed': bool(condition), 'detail': encode(detail)})
    if not condition:
        raise AssertionError(name + ': ' + json.dumps(encode(detail)))


def main():
    data = json.loads((ROOT / 'frozen_exact_data.json').read_text())
    # Literal definitions from the mathematical manuscript, not from its checker.
    q = Q(0, 1)
    ell = 1 + q
    h = [q, Q(2), q, 1 + ell, q, Q(2), q]
    capacity = ell ** 3
    table = {
        (0, 1): Q(2), (1, 0): 2 * ell, (1, 3): 2 * ell,
        (2, 1): 2 * ell * q, (2, 3): 2 * ell ** 2 + 2 * ell * q,
        (3, 1): 2 * ell, (3, 2): 2 * ell ** 3, (3, 7): 2 * ell ** 2,
        (4, 3): 2 * ell * q, (4, 5): 2 * ell ** 2, (4, 7): 2 * ell * q,
        (5, 3): 4 * ell, (5, 4): 2 * ell ** 3, (5, 7): 2 * ell ** 3 + 4 * ell,
        (6, 3): 2 * ell * q, (6, 5): 2 * ell ** 3 * q,
        (6, 7): 2 * ell ** 4 + 2 * ell ** 3 * q + 2 * ell * q,
        (7, 3): 2 * ell, (7, 5): 2 * ell ** 3, (7, 6): 2 * ell ** 5,
    }
    pairs = [(i, j) for i in range(8) for j in range(8) if i != j]
    zeros = [ij for ij in pairs if ij not in table]
    base = matrix(8, 8)
    for (i, j), value in table.items():
        base[i][j] = value
    L0 = laplacian(base)
    T0 = [[Q(1) if i == j else (h[j] if j < i else Q()) for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]
    s = sum(h, Q())
    omega = [-2 * x for x in h] + [2 * s]
    base_flow = [sum((L0[i][j] for i in range(8)), Q()) - omega[j] for j in range(8)]
    LT = multiply(L0, T0)
    D0 = [x * (2 - x) for x in h] + [-s * (s + 2)]
    M0 = [[(LT[i][j] + LT[j][i]) / 2 + (D0[i] if i == j else Q()) for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]
    check('seven steps and exact base capacity', capacity == 1 + s, h)
    check('all twenty base entries positive', len(table) == 20 and all(v.sign() == 1 for v in table.values()))
    check('base original flow', all(not x for x in base_flow), base_flow)
    check('base full remainder is zero', all(not x for row in M0 for x in row), M0)

    equalities = [Q.of(v) for v in data['equality_prices']]
    check('sixteen equality prices', len(equalities) == 16)
    a, b = equalities[:8], equalities[8:]
    svec_prices = [Q.of(v) for v in data['PSD_svec']]
    check('twenty-eight svec prices', len(svec_prices) == 28)
    Z = matrix(7, 7)
    index = 0
    for j in range(7):
        for i in range(j + 1):
            value = svec_prices[index] if i == j else svec_prices[index] / q
            Z[i][j] = Z[j][i] = value
            index += 1
    stored_Z = [[Q.of(v) for v in row] for row in data['PSD_matrix']]
    check('sqrt2 column-major svec decodes the supplied matrix', Z == stored_Z, Z)
    sigma = {(i, j): Q.of(v) for i, j, v in data['all36_sign_prices']}
    check('all thirty-six original zero-pair prices, no duplicates', len(data['all36_sign_prices']) == 36 and set(sigma) == set(zeros))
    signs = [{'pair': list(ij), 'price': sigma[ij], 'sign': sigma[ij].sign()} for ij in zeros]
    check('all sign prices strictly positive in the real embedding', all(sigma[ij].sign() == 1 for ij in zeros), signs)
    box_prices = [Q.of(v) for v in data['all14_box_prices']]
    check('all fourteen box prices exactly zero', len(box_prices) == 14 and all(not v for v in box_prices), box_prices)

    lower = [[Q.of(v) for v in row] for row in data['LDL']['L']]
    pivots = [Q.of(v) for v in data['LDL']['pivots']]
    check('LDL dimensions', len(lower) == 7 and all(len(row) == 7 for row in lower) and len(pivots) == 7)
    check('LDL unit lower triangular', all(lower[i][i] == 1 for i in range(7)) and all(not lower[i][j] for i in range(7) for j in range(i + 1, 7)))
    check('all seven LDL pivots strictly positive', all(d.sign() == 1 for d in pivots), [{'value': d, 'sign': d.sign()} for d in pivots])
    ldl_residual = [[sum((lower[i][k] * pivots[k] * lower[j][k] for k in range(7)), Q()) - Z[i][j] for j in range(7)] for i in range(7)]
    check('every supplied LDL product entry equals Z', all(not v for row in ldl_residual for v in row), ldl_residual)

    # Independent V Z V^T, where V has columns e_i-e_7.
    H = matrix(8, 8)
    for i in range(7):
        for j in range(7):
            H[i][j] = Z[i][j]
        H[i][7] = -sum(Z[i], Q())
        H[7][i] = H[i][7]
    H[7][7] = sum((sum(row, Q()) for row in Z), Q())
    t0e = [sum(row, Q()) for row in T0]
    labels = [('p', i, -1) for i in range(7)] + [('Y', i, j) for i, j in pairs]
    residuals = []
    for kind, row, col in labels:
        p = [Q() for _ in range(7)]
        Y = matrix(8, 8)
        if kind == 'p':
            p[row] = Q(1)
        else:
            Y[row][col] = Q(1)
        S = sum(p, Q())
        LY = laplacian(Y)
        Tp = [[p[j] if j < i else Q() for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]
        term1, term2 = multiply(L0, Tp), multiply(LY, T0)
        D1 = [2 * (1 - h[i]) * p[i] for i in range(7)] + [-2 * capacity * S]
        W = [[(term1[i][j] + term2[i][j] + term1[j][i] + term2[j][i]) / 2 + (D1[i] if i == j else Q()) for j in range(8)] for i in range(8)]
        op = [-2 * x for x in p] + [2 * S]
        f = [sum((LY[i][j] for i in range(8)), Q()) - op[j] for j in range(8)]
        n = [sum(W[i], Q()) for i in range(8)]
        B = [[W[i][j] - W[i][7] - W[7][j] + W[7][7] for j in range(7)] for i in range(7)]
        trace = sum((Z[i][j] * B[i][j] for i in range(7) for j in range(7)), Q())
        original_trace = sum((H[i][j] * W[i][j] for i in range(8) for j in range(8)), Q())
        svec_B = [B[i][j] if i == j else q * B[i][j] for j in range(7) for i in range(j + 1)]
        vector_trace = sum((x * y for x, y in zip(svec_prices, svec_B)), Q())
        price_sign_term = sigma.get((row, col), Q()) if kind == 'Y' else Q()
        value = trace + price_sign_term - sum((a[i] * f[i] + b[i] * n[i] for i in range(8)), Q())
        residual = value + S
        label = kind + str(row) + (',' + str(col) if kind == 'Y' else '')
        common_null_residual = sum(n, Q()) - sum((f[i] * t0e[i] for i in range(8)), Q())
        RESULT['basis_readbacks'].append(encode({'variable': label, 'S': S, 'flow': f, 'W': W, 'We': n, 'B': B, 'PSD_price': trace, 'sign_price': price_sign_term, 'stationarity_residual': residual, 'congruence_price_residual': original_trace - trace, 'svec_price_residual': vector_trace - trace, 'common_null_scalar_residual': common_null_residual}))
        check('original-variable coefficient ' + label, not residual and original_trace == trace and vector_trace == trace and not common_null_residual, residual)
        residuals.append(residual)
    RESULT['independently_rebuilt_model'] = encode({'hbar': h, 'capacity': capacity, 'Lambdabar': base, 'T0': T0, 'original_ordered_pairs': [list(ij) for ij in pairs], 'zero_pairs': [list(ij) for ij in zeros], 'Z': Z, 'H': H})
    RESULT['all_63_stationarity_residuals'] = encode(residuals)
    RESULT['stored_passed_flags_used'] = False
    RESULT['stored_stationarity_residuals_used'] = False
    RESULT['author_checker_imported'] = False
    RESULT['status'] = 'passed_exact_readback_not_formal_verification'


try:
    main()
except BaseException as error:
    RESULT['status'] = 'failed_no_retry'
    RESULT['exception'] = repr(error)
    RESULT['traceback'] = traceback.format_exc()
    traceback.print_exc()
finally:
    RESULT['internal_seconds_before_result_write'] = time.perf_counter() - START
    (ROOT / 'exact_readback_result.json').write_text(json.dumps(encode(RESULT), indent=2) + '\n')
    print(json.dumps({'status': RESULT['status'], 'checks': len(RESULT['checks']), 'basis_readbacks': len(RESULT['basis_readbacks']), 'internal_seconds_through_result_write': time.perf_counter() - START}))
sys.exit(0 if RESULT['status'] == 'passed_exact_readback_not_formal_verification' else 1)
```
