# A quantitative nonperiodic policy with exact signed-history closure

Status: UNVERIFIED joint candidate proof, worker max, 2026-09-26. No new
verifier submission. This answers the quantitative-state assignment with an
explicit horizon-dependent family and a local invariant that controls all
remote planes. It does not prove the unrestricted exact-profile target.

All geometry and estimates used below are proved in this document. Earlier
shared Markov examples and the recursive family are motivation, not premises.
The accepted results invoked are the exact benchmark recursions in fact
0be8b54e0a33381c and the maximal unqueried-terminal extension in fact
1252b8823bb5a290, for the final scoped comparison in Section 7. No external
paper is a black box.

## 1. An exact adjacent criterion for a structured profile class

Write an actual prescribed schedule in chronological order as

    (block_0,b_0,block_1,...,b_(m-1),block_m),

where every block is a finite list of nonnegative steps, s_i is its sum,
and the b_i are checkpoints. Put

    T_i=s_i+1,    A_i=b_i-1,

and assume A_i>=T_(i+1) for 0<=i<m. Thus this part covers the explicit
regime b_i>=s_(i+1)+2; it is not a condition on the unrestricted problem.

Choose positive parameters beta_i with

    beta_i<=A_i/T_(i+1),
    R_i=A_i-T_(i+1) beta_i>=0,                            (1)
    beta_i beta_(i+1)<=1,
    R_i>=beta_i R_(i+1),             0<=i<m-1.            (2)

The last two inequalities are absent when m=1. Define

    q_i=sqrt(A_i beta_i/T_(i+1)),
    a_i=sqrt(T_(i+1) beta_i/A_i),
    alpha_0=1/T_0, alpha_(i+1)=q_i alpha_i,
    zeta_0=e_0,
    zeta_(i+1)=-a_i zeta_i+sqrt(1-a_i^2)e_(i+1),
    omega_i=alpha_i zeta_i.                               (3)

The e_i are an orthonormal basis of R^(m+1). Equations (1) ensure
0<a_i<=1, so every zeta_i is unit and these are genuine Euclidean data.
Fix the SINGLE polyhedral function and its SINGLE Moreau envelope

    J(z)=max(0,<omega_0,z>,...,<omega_m,z>),
    f(x)=min_z[J(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                         (4)

Start at X_0=T_0 omega_0=e_0, and set

    X_(i+1)=X_i-(s_i+b_i)omega_i,
    Z_i=X_i-T_i omega_i.                                 (5)

Then every query within ordinary block i, and the following checkpoint
query, has gradient omega_i. In particular all individual entries of each
ordinary block are executed; their order and their size need not be bounded.
At the endpoint after the final ordinary block, the exact normalized gap is

    V(h,beta)=1/T_0^2 product_(i=0)^(m-1)
                         [A_i beta_i/T_(i+1)].           (6)

The origin is an actual closest minimizer, with ||X_0||=1. Therefore (6)
is a lower bound for the true W_n(h), where n is the full supplied length.

If s_i>0 for 0<=i<=m-2, conditions (2) are also NECESSARY for the
prescribed data (3)--(5) to be supported by (4). This exactness concerns
this Markov, homogeneous-plane class, with A_i>=T_(i+1). It does not
claim that the three-profile state for general affine histories collapses.

### Full proof of all old and future comparisons

The Markov formula gives

    <zeta_i,zeta_j>=(-1)^(j-i) product_(l=i)^(j-1) a_l,
                                                        i<j.

Since T_(i+1)alpha_(i+1)=A_i alpha_i a_i, (5) yields

    Z_0=0,
    Z_(i+1)=Z_i-A_i alpha_i(zeta_i+a_i zeta_(i+1)).        (7)

Every summand at index l in (7) is orthogonal to zeta_j for j>=l+1.
Hence <Z_i,zeta_j>=0 for every j>=i. For j<i, taking the other
inner products in (7), and using a_l q_l=beta_l, gives exactly

    <omega_j,Z_i>=-alpha_j^2 H_(j,i),
    H_(j,i)=sum_(l=j)^(i-1) (-1)^(l-j) R_l
                              product_(v=j)^(l-1) beta_v. (8)

Every adjacent pair of summands, starting at an even offset from j, is
its nonnegative product weight times R_l-beta_l R_(l+1). If the number
of summands is odd, its last summand is nonnegative. Thus (2) proves
H_(j,i)>=0 for EVERY lag. No inherited plane has been discarded.

At a query after an ordinary cumulative sum u in [0,s_i], the proposed
proximal point is

    X_i-(u+1)omega_i=Z_i+d omega_i,  d=s_i-u>=0.

The current plane has value d alpha_i^2. For a future plane j>i its
value is

    d alpha_i^2 (-1)^(j-i) product_(l=i)^(j-1) beta_l.

At odd separation it is nonpositive; at even separation the product
is at most one by grouping the adjacent factors in (2). Thus EVERY
future plane is at most the current one, including arbitrarily distant
ones and the final plane.

For a past plane j<i, its residual (8) is nonpositive, and

    <omega_j,omega_i>/alpha_i^2
       =(-1)^(i-j) product_(l=j)^(i-1) [T_(l+1)/A_l].

At odd separation it is nonpositive; at even separation the product is
at most one because A_l>=T_(l+1). Consequently every past plane is
also at most the current plane. The zero plane is dominated since d>=0.

It follows that omega_i is an active-plane subgradient at the proposed
proximal point. Adding ||x-z||^2/2 to its subgradient inequality proves
that this point uniquely minimizes the proximal objective. This proves
every actual gradient and GD update in the supplied chronological schedule.

For completeness, the envelope in (4) is convex, finite, differentiable,
and 1-smooth. Convexity follows by taking the infimum over z of the jointly
convex defining expression. Existence and uniqueness of the proximal point
follow from coercivity and strict convexity. Adding two proximal
subgradient inequalities gives
||g(x)-g(y)||^2<=<g(x)-g(y),x-y>, with g=x-prox_J(x).
The two minimizing inequalities then identify the derivative as g, and
the displayed bound gives its 1-Lipschitz property.

At the final endpoint u=s_m, the proximal point is Z_m and J(Z_m)=0;
thus 2f(x_n)=alpha_m^2, yielding (6). The minimum of f is zero and its
minimizer set is exactly {J=0}. For any such minimizer z,
<omega_0,z><=0. Since X_0=T_0 omega_0=e_0,

    ||X_0-z||^2=1+||z||^2-2<X_0,z>>=1.

Origin itself is a minimizer, proving the exact normalization.

For necessity of (2), at the checkpoint proximal point Z_(i+2) the
current height is zero. Formula (8) at lag two forces
R_i-beta_i R_(i+1)>=0. At the beginning of ordinary block i, with
s_i>0, the future plane i+2 has value
s_i alpha_i^2 beta_i beta_(i+1), whereas the current value is
s_i alpha_i^2. This forces beta_i beta_(i+1)<=1. Conditions (1) are
the requirement a_i<=1 in the specified Euclidean realization. Together
these prove the asserted exact local criterion.

### A completely explicit policy for every schedule in this regime

One need not assume parameters satisfying (2) exist. Set a temporary
R_m=0, and work backward by

    beta_i=min(1,A_i/[T_(i+1)+R_(i+1)]),
    R_i=A_i-T_(i+1) beta_i.                              (9)

This gives 0<beta_i<=1<=A_i/T_(i+1), all neighboring products are
at most one, and R_i>=beta_i R_(i+1) by the displayed choice. Formula
(6) is its exact computable payoff in the supplied steps. There is no
claim that (9) attains the benchmark or optimizes the complete criterion.

In fact imposing beta_i<=1 loses useful constructions. The quantitative
policy below has beta_i=3/2 at every other checkpoint. Future comparisons
require the PAIR products in (2), not the unjustified individual cap.

## 2. Robust nonperiodic transmission at fixed ordinary sum

Fix any common ordinary sum S>0 and put T=S+1. The ordinary lists may
be completely nonidentical as long as each has that sum. Choose 2k
checkpoints, in pairs indexed by j=0,...,k-1, with

    b_(2j)=T+1,
    b_(2j+1)=rho_j T+1,
    9/4<=rho_j<=5/2.                                    (10)

All rho_j may be chosen independently from this interval after the whole
schedule is prescribed. Set

    beta_(2j)=1/2,  beta_(2j+1)=3/2.

Then the residual coefficients in (1) are

    R_(2j)=T/2,
    R_(2j+1)=T(rho_j-3/2).

The critical-to-large inequality in (2) is exactly rho_j<=5/2. The
large-to-next-critical inequality is exactly rho_j>=9/4. Every adjacent
beta product is 3/4. The final residual is nonnegative too. Hence all
conditions are checked for arbitrary nonperiodic variation of the rho_j,
without a perturbative assumption or a discarded remote comparison.

Each pair has the squared-scale multiplier

    q_(2j)^2 q_(2j+1)^2=3rho_j/4>=27/16.

The precise whole-schedule payoff is consequently

    W_n(h)>=T^(-2) product_(j=0)^(k-1)(3rho_j/4)
                            >=(27/16)^k/T^2.             (11)

The cost T^2 is the actual initial-distance normalization at gradient
scale 1/T. It cannot be silently omitted when S grows with the horizon.
The next section keeps that cost explicitly while making both the total
ordinary complexity and the checkpoint amplitudes grow.

## 3. A prescribed growing family and the COMPLETE benchmark comparison

Define c=13/5, a=c^(-17), and finite words

    H_0=(), H_(r+1)=(H_r,a c^r,H_r).

Let s_r=sum H_r. The exact length and sum are

    |H_r|=2^r-1,
    s_r=a(c^r-2^r)/(c-2).                                (12)

For each integer k>=1, set

    r=floor(k/4)+18,
    block=16 concatenated copies of H_r,
    S=16s_r, T=S+1,
    rho_j=9/4+1/[4(j+1)],   0<=j<k.                     (13)

Take 2k+1 copies of this block with the checkpoints (10) between them.
The rho_j in (13) make an explicitly nonperiodic schedule. More generally,
all conclusions about the lower bound hold for ANY choices in the interval
(10). The length of the complete prescribed schedule is

    N_k=16(2k+1)(2^r-1)+2k.                              (14)

The local criterion constructs one global function on R^(2k+1), with
every gradient and query fixed and with origin its actual closest minimizer.
From (12), a=c^(-17), and r<=k/4+18,

    T<=1+(80/3)a c^r<=(211/3)c^(k/4).

Thus (11) gives the explicit bound

    W_(N_k)(h)>= (9/44521)[27/(16 sqrt(c))]^k
                 >=(9/44521)(27/26)^k.                  (15)

The last comparison uses c<169/64, so sqrt(c)<13/8. It does not hide a
parameter-dependent loss. The right side tends to infinity.

For clarity this provides a comparison to the WHOLE exact B_n sequence,
not just its exponent. In the benchmark recursions of accepted fact
0be8b54e0a33381c, B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1>=1, hence U_n^ZJ=1/B_n<=1.
The binomial expansion gives (27/26)^26>2. For k>=338=13*26,

    (9/44521)(27/26)^k > (9/44521)2^13 >1.

Consequently this explicit nonperiodic family satisfies

    W_(N_k)(h)>1>=U_(N_k)^ZJ    for every k>=338.          (16)

The result is confined to the specified family; it says nothing about the
infimum over all schedules at N_k or other horizons.

## 4. The simple benchmark witnesses are weak on the SAME family

Define the full quadratic channel and the sum/single-cut channel by

    Q_n(h)=max_(0<=lambda<=1) lambda product_(t<n)(1-lambda h_t)^2,

    K_n(h)=max{1/[1+2sum h_t],
        max_j (h_j-1)_+^2/
           [(1+sum_(t<j)h_t)^2(1+2sum_(t>j)h_t)]}.

These are the usual quadratic and single-cut Huber lower-bound functionals.
We will prove, on the SAME schedules (13)--(14),

    K_(N_k)<=c^2/(2S),
    Q_(N_k)<=max{1/(4 e k S),(2/3)^(4k)},                (17)

where e=exp(1), and

    max(K_(N_k),Q_(N_k))/U_(N_k)^ZJ ->0.                (18)

This assertion bounds those channels, not the actual worst case, which
grows by (15). It does not assert an upper bound on all scalar functions
or on the verified multi-cut alternating-ray channel.

### Quadratic product estimate for H_r, with all constants

Write b_u=a c^u, and set

    P_r(lambda)=product_(u=0)^(r-1)(1-b_u lambda)^(2^(r-u-1)).

For lambda in (0,1], put z=a lambda and choose the integer v>=17 with
c^v z<=1<c^(v+1)z. Let y=c^v z, so 1/c<y<=1. If a product factor
is zero all the desired upper bounds hold. Otherwise, for r>v,

    log|P_r|/2^(r-v-1)
        =sum_(j=-v)^(r-v-1)2^(-j)log|1-c^j y|.

For j<0 use log(1-c^j y)<=-c^j y. For j>=1 use
log|1-c^j y|<=j log c+log y; these upper bounds are nonnegative,
so extending just their sum to infinity increases it. The resulting bound is

    -2y[1-(2/c)^v]/(c-2)+log[y(1-y)]+2log c
      <=-(13/4)y+log(1/4)+2log c
      <=-5/4+log(169/100)
      <=-5/4+69/100=-14/25<-1/2.                        (19)

To justify the numerical coefficient in the first inequality, since
2/c=10/13 and v>=17, one has (10/13)^v<1/40. For example
(10/13)^5<27/100 and (10/13)^2<3/5, hence its seventeenth power
is less than (27/100)^3(3/5)<1/40. Substitution into
2[1-(2/c)^v]/(c-2) makes it at least 13/4. Also y>1/c=5/13
and y(1-y)<=1/4. Finally log t<=t-1 at t=169/100 proves the last
inequality. These prove (19) without a decimal estimate.

It follows that

    |P_r(lambda)|<=exp(-2^(r-v-2)) for r>v.

For r<=v all factors lie in [0,1], so |P_r|<=1. In particular
|P_r|<=1 always, including lambda=0.

### Quadratic estimate for every complete ordinary/checkpoint pair

First S>35 for r>=18. Indeed the three highest levels of H_18 give
s_18>=1+10/13+100/169=399/169, and S>=16s_18>35. Every checkpoint B
in (10) therefore satisfies

    B<=(5/2)(S+1)+1<=(13/5)S=cS.

If lambda b_(r-1)<=1, all factors of P_r are nonnegative and
|P_r|^16<=exp(-S lambda). For B lambda<=1 the entire macro factor
|P_r|^16|1-B lambda| is at most exp(-S lambda). Otherwise it is at
most (cS lambda-1)exp(-S lambda), whose maximum for nonnegative
S lambda is c exp(-1-1/c)<2/3. The latter exact inequality follows
from

    exp(18/13) > sum_(j=0)^4 (18/13)^j/j!
               =112495/28561 >39/10=(3/2)c.

If lambda b_(r-1)>1, put d=r-v>=1. The preceding polynomial estimate,
lambda S<=(80/3)c^d, and B<=3S give

    |P_r|^16|1-B lambda|
       <=80 c^d exp(-16*2^(d-2))
       <=80 exp(-7d)<5/8<2/3.                           (20)

Here c<e, as e>1+1+1/2+1/6=8/3>c; also 2^d>=2d and e>2.
These justify each inequality in (20). Therefore every ordinary/checkpoint
macro, including either type and every supplied rho_j, has scalar factor

    <=max(exp(-S lambda),2/3).

There are 2k such macros. The terminal ordinary block has factor at most
one. After squaring, multiplying by lambda<=1, and maximizing, this proves
the Q bound in (17), since max_(lambda>=0)lambda exp(-4kS lambda)
=1/(4 e k S). Orthogonal diagonalization of a positive semidefinite
quadratic Hessian shows that its normalized objective is a weighted
average of these scalar objectives, so this controls all convex quadratics
in every dimension.

### Every single-cut term

For all levels u, b_u-1<=s_u. When u<=17, b_u<=1 proves it. For
u>=18, (2/c)^u<=2/5, so s_u/b_u=[1-(2/c)^u]/(c-2)>=1.
At a level-u step within a copy of H_r, both its local prefix and its
local suffix contain a whole H_u, so both have sum at least s_u.

For a checkpoint cut, its size is at most cS, and its complete prefix and
suffix sums are each at least S. Its term is at most c^2/(2S).
For an ordinary cut outside the final ordinary block, its complete suffix
contains that final block, hence has sum at least S. Its own H_u prefix
and b_u-1<=s_u bound the other ratio by one; its term is at most 1/(2S).
For an ordinary cut inside the final block, its complete prefix sum is at
least S, while the H_u suffix gives

    (b_u-1)_+^2/(1+2 suffix_sum)
          <=s_u^2/(1+2s_u)<=s_u/2<=S/32.

Its term is at most 1/(32S). The global sum Huber term is at most
1/(2S). This proves the K bound in (17), covering every cut.

### Comparison to the exact DP profile

Here is a direct algebraic bound on the same exact recursions, included
to avoid using an unverified asymptotic formula. Put C_n=S_circle(n)+1.
The shifted primitive and dominant joins from fact 0be8b54e0a33381c are

    Omega(A,D)=(A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD))/2,
    Gmass(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.

They join C_k,C_(n-k-1) and C_k,B_(n-k-1), respectively, and are
increasing in their positive arguments. For t in [0,1],

    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1.              (21)

One proof sets x=t^(1/3), y=(1-t)^(1/3) and uses x^3+y^3=1 to write

    1-x^4-y^4-x^4y^4
      =x^3y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0.

The two reciprocals are each at least 1/3, whereas xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3.
This also covers the endpoints. Since Omega is the positive root of
X^2-(A+D)X-AD=0, substituting
t=A^(3/4)/(A^(3/4)+D^(3/4)) into (21) proves

    Omega(A,D)<=(A^(3/4)+D^(3/4))^(4/3).

Induction in the exact primitive recursion gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3).
Also Gmass(A,D)<=D+4A. Induction in the exact dominant recursion,
using C_0=B_0=1, now gives

    B_n<=1+4n^(4/3).                                    (22)

For a split at k, the inductive upper bound is
1+4[(n-k-1)^(4/3)+(k+1)^(4/3)]<=1+4n^(4/3), proving (22)
for its maximum as well.

Finally N_k<=48k 2^r and, for r>=18,
S>= (40/3)a c^r. Multiplying (17) by (22) bounds the K ratio by
a constant times k^(4/3)(2^(4/3)/c)^r and the first Q ratio by a
constant times k^(1/3)(2^(4/3)/c)^r, plus smaller terms. These tend
to zero since r=floor(k/4)+18 and c^3=2197/125>16. The remaining
Q ratio is bounded by a constant times

    k^(4/3)[2^(1/3)(2/3)^4]^k,

which tends to zero because its bracket is less than one. This proves
(18) with the normalization, horizon dependence, and full B_n sequence
kept throughout.

## 5. Exact scope and the next unresolved interface

The new reusable invariant is (1)--(2), with the exact residual formula
(8) and payoff (6). It checks both cross-plane directions for all lags
and computes the genuine closest-minimizer normalization. Formula (9)
is a prescribed feasible policy for every supplied schedule satisfying
the next-block condition. Equations (10)--(18) give a substantially
stronger explicit nonperiodic policy and a growing example beyond the
quadratic and single-cut channels.

This is a structured subclass of the geometric profile state, with
homogeneous planes and zero-height checkpoint proximal points. It does
not reinstate the disproved compression of general positive-level affine
histories. Those still require Phi,Psi, the actual boundary gradient,
and their distance/sublevel profiles.

The condition b_i>=s_(i+1)+2 is a real remaining restriction. When it
fails, the old-plane correlation product T_(i+1)/A_i can exceed one;
the zero residual sign alone no longer dominates old planes at new
positive-height queries. One must use actual retained height/slack data
there. The current proof supplies neither a partition satisfying this
condition for arbitrary near-optimal schedules nor a uniform B_n
comparison outside it. All results here remain unverified research
evidence until the verifier accepts an allocated submission.

## 6. Exact retained loss when the next-block condition fails

The proof above also identifies the complete additional constraint for
arbitrary A_i=b_i-1>0, including A_i<T_(i+1). Keep definitions (1),
(3)--(8), the Euclidean constraint 0<beta_i<=A_i/T_(i+1), and
T_i=s_i+1. Define the empty products to be one and, for j<i, put

    P_(j,i)=product_(l=j)^(i-1) beta_l,
    Q_(j,i)=product_(l=j)^(i-1) [A_l/T_(l+1)].

The exact old-plane comparison on the whole of ordinary block i is

    H_(j,i)>=s_i[(-1)^(i-j) P_(j,i)
                              -P_(j,i)Q_(j,i)]_+,
    H_(j,i)>=0.                                         (23)

Indeed at its proximal point Z_i+d omega_i the old plane minus the
current plane, divided by alpha_j^2, equals

    -H_(j,i)+d[(-1)^(i-j)P_(j,i)-P_(j,i)Q_(j,i)].

This is affine in d in [0,s_i], whose two endpoints are actual samples.
Thus (23) is necessary and sufficient. Future comparisons are still the
even-lag product constraints P_(i,j)<=1 whenever s_i>0. At odd lag the
sign already makes the future plane nonpositive. These are exact all-plane
conditions; in particular the positive-part term in (23) must be kept
when a product of A_l/T_(l+1) is less than one.

They give a concrete interval for appending a new block to a legal prefix.
Suppose block i has already been constructed and A=A_i>0 and the entire
next block, with T=T_(i+1)=s_(i+1)+1, are prescribed. Its only new scalar
is beta=beta_i. For every j<i with i-j odd, use the already retained
quantities

    p_j=P_(j,i)>0,  q_j=Q_(j,i)>0,
    c_j=H_(j,i)/p_j>=0.

The exact admissible interval is

    0<beta<=Upper,
    beta>=Lower,

    Upper=min{A/T,
                  min_(j<i, i-j odd, s_j>0) 1/p_j},
    Lower=max{0,
                  max_(j<i, i-j odd)
                 (A-c_j)/[T-s_(i+1)(1-A q_j/T)_+]}.     (24)

An empty inner minimum is +infinity and an empty inner maximum contributes
zero. Each denominator is at least T-s_(i+1)=1. A nonpositive candidate
for Lower is harmless. These formulas keep every old query and do not
assert that the interval is nonempty.

To check them, (8) gives, at the new even lag from j,

    H_(j,i+1)/p_j=c_j-A+T beta.

Substituting this and the new products p_j beta and q_j A/T into (23)
is exactly the lower bound in (24). If its numerator is nonpositive the
new residual is nonnegative automatically; otherwise the same bound also
implies that nonnegativity. At a new odd lag the old nonnegative residual
is increased by a nonnegative summand and the bracket in (23) is negative,
so there is no extra condition. The immediately preceding plane likewise
has residual A-T beta>=0. A future new plane at an old positive-height
query gives beta p_j<=1 precisely for the indicated even separations;
this proves Upper. Its comparisons at zero-height old proximal points
hold by the exact forward orthogonality in (7). This proves both necessity
and sufficiency of (24) for appending the supplied complete block.

For any sequence of accepted intervals, the same fixed-origin normalization
and product payoff (6) remain exact, with all directions fixed before the
actual GD run. Choosing the largest allowed beta maximizes the immediate
new squared gradient for that retained prefix; no greedy optimality for
the entire remaining schedule is claimed.

### A precise test of the omitted positive-height term

Take three one-step ordinary blocks, each (1), and two bridges each 2.
Thus the complete schedule is (1,2,1,2,1), T_0=T_1=T_2=2 and
A_0=A_1=1. The tempting parameters beta_0=beta_1=1/2 have
R_0=R_1=0, beta_0 beta_1=1/4, so every zero-residual test and
every future-plane test passes. But (23) at (j,i)=(0,2) requires

    0 >= 1*(1/4-1/16)=3/16,

which fails. Concretely their gradients are omega_0=e_0/2,
omega_1=-e_0/4, omega_2=e_0/8. At the initial query e_0/4 of the
last ordinary block, the intended proximal point is e_0/8. The old
plane omega_0 has value 1/16 there, while the proposed current plane
omega_2 has value 1/64. This is the exact old-at-new violation.

With beta_0=1/2 retained, (24) gives Lower=4/5>Upper=1/2,
so no choice of the next correlation fixes that prefix within this
specified geometry. Re-selecting the earlier parameter does: take

    beta_0=8/19, beta_1=1/2.

Now H_(0,2)=3/19 and the right side of (23) is also 3/19;
all the other comparisons hold. Formula (6) gives payoff 1/76.
This is even the maximum in this two-parameter Markov class: its only
nonautomatic old comparison is

    beta_0(3-5 beta_1/4)<=1,

with 0<beta_0,beta_1<=1/2. The objective beta_0 beta_1/16 increases
up to beta_1=1/2, where beta_0=8/19 is its largest feasible value.
This restricted calculation is not the true W_5 of that schedule.

Thus the arbitrary-schedule obstacle has an explicit additional term and
a concrete failed extension, rather than a presumed missing feasibility
theorem. The nonperiodic family of Sections 2--4 avoids that term by an
actual prescribed mass condition. A uniform policy that controls (23)--(24)
while attaining the complete B_n benchmark remains unresolved.

## 7. A positive terminal level removes an entire growing loss factor

This refinement is useful precisely when the zero-level score (11) is
asymptotically too small. It changes the terminal cell of the globally
fixed construction; it is not an unauthorized deletion of queried-prefix
constraints or an application of terminal completion to earlier samples.

Keep the schedule, ordinary blocks, and all parameters up to index 2k-1
from Section 2. Write rho=rho_(k-1) and A=rho T for the coefficient
at the last checkpoint. The queried prefix through that checkpoint is
kept exactly. Its gradient scale is

    alpha_(2k-1)^2=(1/[2T^2])
                       product_(j=0)^(k-2)(3rho_j/4).    (25)

For the final ordinary block, choose instead a terminal margin parameter

    T_terminal=2S+1=2T-1,
    beta_last=A/T_terminal,
    a_last=1,
    q_last=A/T_terminal.                                (26)

Thus the last direction is the negative of the preceding direction and
the final gradient scale is alpha_(2k)=q_last alpha_(2k-1). Define the
final residual point with the actual new margin,

    Z_(2k)=X_(2k)-T_terminal omega_(2k).

The same bridge update now gives Z_(2k)=Z_(2k-1), since the last
direction is exactly reversed and T_terminal alpha_(2k)=A alpha_(2k-1).
The last residual coefficient is zero.

All old planes and all old queries are still checked. The preceding
residual inequality is automatic because its right-hand side is zero.
The last adjacent future product is

    beta_(2k-2) beta_last=(rho T)/[2(2T-1)]<=1

when T>=4/3 and rho<=5/2. The last old-correlation factor is
T_terminal/A=(2T-1)/(rho T)<=1 because rho>=9/4. All earlier
factors and inequalities are unchanged. Consequently the pairing and
product arguments in the full proof of Section 1 still check every
old/future comparison. In the terminal block the proximal points are
Z_(2k)+(T_terminal-u-1)omega_(2k), 0<=u<=S, whose coefficients are
nonnegative. This directly checks its entire sequence of supplied queries.

At the final query the proximal value is now

    F_terminal=S alpha_(2k)^2>0,

and the exact objective numerator is

    2f_end=(2S+1)alpha_(2k)^2
           =A^2 alpha_(2k-1)^2/(2T-1).

The global function is again (4) with the revised final normal. The origin
remains its closest minimizer by the same omega_0 halfspace argument.
Thus the EXACT normalized score of this new instance is

    V_positive= [rho_(k-1)^2/(2(2T-1))]
                         product_(j=0)^(k-2)(3rho_j/4).  (27)

For comparison, the earlier zero-proximal-level instance on the IDENTICAL
schedule had exact score V_zero=T^(-2)product_(j<k)(3rho_j/4).
Their exact ratio is

    V_positive/V_zero=2rho_(k-1)T^2/[3(2T-1)],           (28)

which grows proportionally to T. This is a change in the actual normalized
objective of a legal instance, not just in an unnormalized gradient scale.

The scalar choice (26) has a simple exact optimization interpretation.
For an arbitrary terminal margin M>=S+1, keep the last direction reversed
and choose alpha_terminal=A alpha_(2k-1)/M. Whenever the old/future
checks hold, its score is

    A^2 alpha_(2k-1)^2 [2(M-S)-1]/M^2.

Its derivative has the sign of 2S+1-M, so its maximum occurs at
M=2S+1. We checked that this value satisfies the actual old/future tests;
no global optimality beyond this terminal family is asserted.

### A family for which the positive-level score reaches B_n and the old
### score does not

Now change only the prescribed horizon-dependent depth from (13) to

    r=k+18,
    N_k^+=16(2k+1)(2^(k+18)-1)+2k.                       (29)

All rho_j, ordinary blocks, and checkpoints remain exactly as specified
there. The old all-plane policy is legal at this depth too, but its
normalization cost T^2 is now substantial. Formula (27), using
rho_j>=9/4 and T<=(211/3)c^k, gives

    V_positive >= [3/(2(2T-1))](27/16)^k
                >=(3/(4T))(27/16)^k
                >=(9/844)(135/208)^k.                  (30)

Here all constants are independent of the horizon. Unlike (15), this
displayed lower bound tends to zero; comparison to the exact B_n sequence
is therefore substantive.

Put lambda_silver=1+sqrt(2). Its use in the following elementary bound
does not replace B_n by an asymptotic model. From the exact shifted
primitive join in Section 4,

    Omega(A,A)=lambda_silver A.

Induction over the actual balanced splits gives
C_(2^r-1)>=lambda_silver^r. A dominant split with this primitive left
part and empty right part gives

    B_(2^r)>=Gmass(lambda_silver^r,1)>=2lambda_silver^r+1.

Moreover B_n is increasing: the split with empty left part has
Gmass(1,B_(n-1))>=B_(n-1)+2. Since N_k^+>=2^r,

    B_(N_k^+)>=2lambda_silver^(k+18)+1.

Multiplying (30) by this bound yields

    V_positive B_(N_k^+)
       >=(9/422)lambda_silver^18
                         [135lambda_silver/208]^k>1    (31)

for EVERY k>=1. To check the strict inequality without a decimal value,
lambda_silver>12/5 implies 135lambda_silver/208>81/52>1,
and lambda_silver>2 makes (9/422)lambda_silver^18>1.
Thus this specified positive-level construction satisfies the complete
finite-horizon benchmark comparison

    W_(N_k^+)(h)>=V_positive>U_(N_k^+)^ZJ,
                                      for every k>=1.   (32)

On this SAME family the former zero-level score is below the benchmark:

    V_zero<= (15/8)^k/T^2,
    V_zero/U_(N_k^+)^ZJ ->0.                            (33)

Indeed T>=(40/3)a c^r, N_k^+<=48k2^r, and (22) bound the ratio by a
constant times k^(4/3)[(15/8)2^(4/3)/c^2]^k, plus a smaller term.
Since 2^(4/3)<c and (15/8)/c=75/104<1, it tends to zero.

The K and Q estimates (17) were proved for every r>=18, not just the
first depth choice. With r=k+18 the same calculation gives

    max(K_(N_k^+),Q_(N_k^+))/U_(N_k^+)^ZJ ->0.            (34)

For the last Q term use 2^(4/3)(2/3)^4<c*(16/81)<1;
the other terms contain (2^(4/3)/c)^r times polynomial factors in k.

Equations (28)--(34) identify a horizon-growing quantitative gain from
retaining a positive terminal level, with every old plane, every actual
prefix query, and the same closest-minimizer normalization preserved.
This closes the selected nonperiodic test family only. The arbitrary
schedule interval constraints (23)--(24), positive-level gluing beyond
this structured family, and the unrestricted uniform B_n comparison
remain open.

### Completing only the unqueried endpoint cannot supply this gain

This gives a quantitative use of accepted fact 1252b8823bb5a290, whose
maximal extension retains all genuinely queried prefix jets and a minimizer
sample. Apply it to the ZERO-level instance on (29), with the optimum
sample at origin. Its last actual step is a=c^(-17), since H_r begins
and ends with this step. Write omega for its terminal-block gradient and
v=||omega||^2=V_zero. The last queried jet has

    x_(n-1)=Z+(1+a)omega,
    g_(n-1)=omega,
    f_(n-1)=(a+1/2)v,
    x_n=Z+omega.

For ANY convex 1-smooth extension of those prefix jets, the descent
inequality at x_(n-1), obtained by integrating the 1-Lipschitz gradient
along the segment of length a||omega||, gives

    f(x_n)<=f_(n-1)-a v+(a^2/2)v=(1+a^2)v/2.            (35)

In particular it holds for the pointwise maximal prefix extension in
fact 1252b8823bb5a290. Its normalized error is at least V_zero because
the original function is one compatible extension, and (35) gives

    V_zero<=V_maximal_completion<=(1+a^2)V_zero.         (36)

The normalization has not changed. Indeed the retained initial jet is
x_0=e_0, g_0=e_0/T, f_0=(T-1/2)/T^2, and the extension has minimum
zero at origin. For any of its minimizers z, the necessary smooth-convex
interpolation inequality (proved in fact 1252b8823bb5a290) is

    f_0+<g_0,z-x_0>+||g_0||^2/2<=0.

Substitution gives <e_0,z><=0. Therefore every minimizer has squared
distance at least one from e_0, and origin attains one. This proves that
the actual closest-minimizer denominator in (36) is exactly one too.

By (33) and the fixed finite factor 1+a^2, the maximal-completion score
divided by U_(N_k^+)^ZJ still tends to zero. Thus the gain (28) comes
from constructing a different entire terminal block, with a positive
proximal endpoint value and rechecked old/new constraints. It cannot be
obtained merely by completing the genuinely unqueried last sample of the
old zero-level transcript. Every parameter is chosen before the GD run.

# Exact survivor of the complete current scalar necessary-test system

Status: self-contained joint proof DRAFT, not a verifier-accepted fact.
This file establishes a limitation of a specified collection of necessary
conditions. It does NOT supply nonnegative multipliers or a full PSD seed.
No optimizer, numerical parameter search, grid, verifier, or installation
was used. The two tiny exact rational calculations expand the fixed
polynomial identities printed below; they do not search for a schedule.

## 1. Exact schedule and variable-capacity family

For 0<=t<=1/100 define

 h(t)=(7/5,9/4,33/25+t,7/4),
 a=h0-1=2/5, b=h1-1=5/4, c=h2-1=8/25+t, d=h3-1=3/4,
 C=1+sum h=193/25+t,
 P=2+a=12/5, Q=3+a+b=93/20,
 D=2+d=11/4, H=3+c+d=407/100+t.

All previous max2 scalar tests, and the complete C82 triangle family,
are STRICTLY satisfied throughout 0<=t<=1/100. In addition, every
quadratic-curvature test and every C83 cut-repair test is STRICTLY
satisfied for every 0<k<=1 throughout 0<=t<=10^(-12).

Thus there is an exact survivor at total sum168/25 and a concrete
interval of survivors with strictly larger sums. In particular an
attempt to prove emptiness from these scalar tests cannot succeed.
This is not a restriction or a reduction of the original feasible set
onto a fixed-capacity slice; it is a constructed family inside the
necessary-test region.

The original full interface remains twenty Lambda_ij>=0, i!=j in0..4,
Lambda_ii=0, L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, T_ii=1,T_it=h_t for t<i,
L^T e=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2sum h)^T, and

 diag(h0(2-h0),...,h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)) + (LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0,
 s=sum h.

No Lambda or matrix remainder is claimed in this file. Accepted
04219554e41d9750 gives the conditional asymptotic implication only
if an actual full certificate is subsequently constructed.

## 2. Compact box, every cut, and the elementary nonlinear tests

On our interval, 193/25<=C<=773/100<87/10, 8/25<=c<=33/100,
and all four shifts are positive. The endpoint bounds a,d<77/97 and
middle bounds b,c<1552559/1081453 hold: b=5/4 gives
4*1552559-5*1081453=802971>0, and c< b. Thus the strict step box
from the earlier joint draft is satisfied.

The four cut inequalities, in order, are

 Ca <= C-1-a,
 Cb <= P H,
 Cc <= Q D,
 Cd <= C-1-d.                                           (1)

The first and fourth have margins respectively
(3/5)C-7/5>0 and C/4-7/4>0. The second has margin

 PH-Cb=59/500+(23/20)t>0.                                (2)

For the third, Cc<=25509/10000<64/25, whereas QD=1023/80>12.
These bounds follow by multiplying the upper endpoints of C and c.
Every prefix/reversed-prefix cut recurrence is merely a rearrangement
of (1), so no extra cut has been dropped. The concavity-derived endpoint
sum condition follows from the two middle cuts: if E=4+a+d, adding
b<=P(C-P)/(C+P), c<=D(C-D)/(C+D), and using the negative second
derivative of z(C-z)/(C+z), gives E>=2C(C-1)/(3C+1).
The pointwise endpoint and middle cut bounds follow directly from (1).

The actual unit-curvature product test, suffix product test, and
chronological clipped-tail test are, respectively,

 C a b c d<=1,   C c d<=Q,
 C |bc-(1+d)min(bc,1)|<=P.                              (3)

Here ab=1/2, bc<=33/80<1, and Cc<64/25. Consequently their left
sides are less than

 (3/8)(64/25)=24/25<1,
 (3/4)(64/25)=48/25<Q,
 (15/16)(64/25)=12/5=P.

All comparisons are strict because 25509<25600.
At t=0 the exact full product is579/625 and the exact clipped
left side is579/250, whose gap from P is21/250.

The scalar mixed first-prefix test has u=7/5 and eta=b/H. Its expression
is

 u^2+u-1+u(2-u^2)eta-a C^2 eta^2
   =59/25+(7/125)eta-(2/5)(C eta)^2.

By (1), C eta<P=12/5. Therefore this expression exceeds7/125>0.
Its domain a eta<1 also follows since a eta<ab/4=1/8.

For the two unit-curvature cut repairs, the base energy is
V0=(1+prefix sum)^2-C^2 eta^2>0 by (1). The auxiliary terminal
value q is q=1/(2H) at cut1, and q=-c/(2D) at cut2. Hence

 C|q|=C/(2H)<1 at cut1, since 2H-C=C-73/10>0,
 C|q|=Cc/(2D)<(64/25)/(11/2)<1 at cut2.

The auxiliary energy1-C^2 q^2 is strictly positive. Thus every repair
expression beta V0+eta(1+eta)(1-C^2 q^2) is positive for beta>=0,
including the beta=0 case. No denominator with an unproved sign is used.

## 3. All three adjacent triangles and the first backward lift

The adjacent-triangle tests, with the selected consecutive shifts
B,E, preceding capacity P0 and following capacity D0, read

 B E [1+B(P0+C)/P0^2] <= D0.                            (4)

For the first pair, its left side is
ab[1+a(1+C)] < (1/2)[1+(2/5)*9]=23/10<H.
For the middle pair, bc<=33/80, P+C<11, and P^2>5. Therefore its
left side is less than

 (33/80)[1+(5/4)*11/5]=99/64<11/4=D.

For the last pair, cd<=99/400, Q+C<13 and Q^2>21. Its left side
is less than

 (99/400)[1+(33/100)*13/21]
 <(99/400)(5/4)=99/320<1.

The strengthened middle triangle has the additional nonnegative
parameter max((1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,0). In this family

 a^2+2a-1=-1/25,

so this parameter equals zero and the strengthened test is exactly
the already strict middle inequality. This is an actual branch check,
not deletion of an active max term.

## 4. Both last-pair lifts and the earliest-normal guard

Define the last-triangle quantities

 eta=c/D,
 K=c^2 d+(c+d+2)(1-cd)=c+d+2-cd(d+2),
 r=c^2 d/K,
 Hpre=2+a+b=73/20, Apre=1+a+b=53/20,
 Z=(c+r)/(1+d),
 qlate=(1+(1-b)eta)/b,
 qearly=[eta+(Apre eta-1)Z]/Apre,
 Jpre=1-2Hpre eta+Hpre^2 max(qlate,qearly).

The required inequalities are C^2 r<=min(Jpre,Q^2) and

 C^2 max(eta^2,r)<=Jquad,
 Jquad=[(1-b eta)Q^2+(1+eta)/(a^2 b)]/(1+b).             (5)

All denominators are positive: eta<3/25, cd<1/4, c+d+2>3, so
K>3-(1/4)(11/4)=37/16>2. Also

 r < ((33/100)^2*(3/4))/2 <1/20,
 eta^2<9/625<1/20, C^2<64.

Thus every left side in (5) and C^2 r is less than16/5.
On the right, qlate>(1-3/100)/(5/4)=97/125>3/4,
Hpre>7/2,Hpre<4, and eta<3/25. Therefore

 Jpre >=1-2Hpre eta+Hpre^2 qlate
       >1-24/25+147/16>9.

Also Q^2>21. For Jquad use b eta<3/20, a^2 b=1/5 and1+b=9/4:

 Jquad >[(17/20)*21+5]/(9/4)=457/45>10.

These bounds already retain the full max in Jpre. In fact the earliest
normal is inactive here for a proved reason: Apre eta<(53/20)(3/25)<1.
Since Z>0, the explicit formula gives
qearly<eta/Apre<eta<3/25<3/4<qlate. No shortcut based only on
cd<1 or b eta<1 has been made.
The other max is also correctly determined: d=3/4>sqrt(2)-1, so
r>eta^2. This follows from

d(d+2)^2-K=(c+d+2)(d(d+2)-1)>0.

At t=0, K=241/100,r=192/6025,eta=32/275,
qlate=1068/1375, and Jquad=529303/49500.

## 5. The entire C82 interior-edge triangle family survives

For clarity, the interface is rederived here in this family's notation.
Take U>0,z>0, with

 U>=b,
 z^2 <= (U+1)(a U-1),
 z^2 >= c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2.                              (6)

Use vertices p=(U,0), q=(-1,z), w=(-bc/D,0), and the convex
function F(x)=max_{v in conv(p,q,w)}(<x,v>-||v||^2/2).
Its gradient is projection onto that triangle. The original jets are

 x0=((1+a)U-1,z), g0=(U,0),
 x1=(-1,z), g1=(-1,z),
 x2=(b,-bz), g2=(b,0),
 x3=(-bc,-bz), g3=(-bc/D,0),
 x4=(-bc/D,-bz), g4=(-bc/D,0).

The initial normal comparison with q is z^2-(U+1)(aU-1)<=0;
the other initial comparison is nonpositive since aU-1>=0.
At1 the normal is0. At2 its normal is(0,-bz), and U>=b places
g2 on the base. At3 the comparison with q is
b[c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2-z^2]<=0, and the base comparison is nonpositive.
At4 the normal is again vertical downward. These checks include every
triangle vertex and the optimum0 in its base, hence all original
interpolation pairs. The four GD identities follow by subtraction.
Saturated values f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 telescope, giving energy

 E(U,z)=((1+a)U-1)^2+(1-b^2)z^2-C^2(bc/D)^2.           (7)

The complete parameter-eliminated C82 test is the infimum of (7)
over (6), including zero-height limits. We bound the whole domain,
so no unproved formula for that infimum is needed.
Since a=2/5, (6) forces U>=5/2. Also 1-b^2=-9/16<0, so
E(U,z)>=q(U), where

 q(U)=((7/5)U-1)^2-(9/16)(U+1)((2/5)U-1)-C^2(bc/D)^2.

Its quadratic coefficient is347/200>0 and
q'(5/2)=(7/5)(71/16)>0. Thus q is strictly increasing for U>=5/2.
Using Cc<64/25 and b/D=5/11 gives

 E(U,z)>=q(5/2)=25/4-(5 Cc/11)^2
              >25/4-25/16=75/16>0.

This includes all branches, boundary limits and the exact infimum.
It proves survival of C82, not sufficiency of any scalar test.

## 6. All curvatures in the full quadratic and C83 families

This section first fixes t=0. For 0<k<=1 and j in{0,1,2,3}, put

 R_j=sum_(i>j)h_i, H_j=sum_(i<=j)h_i, E_j=1+k R_j,
 p_0=1, p_i=product_(v<i)(1-k h_v),
 q_j=k p_(j+1)/E_j.

The auxiliary trajectory follows the curvature-k quadratic through
query j, and subsequently has constant gradient q_j, ending at
x4=q_j/k. Its values before or at j are k p_i^2/2 and afterwards
q_j^2/(2k)+q_j^2 sum_(v>=i)h_v. This array is only an auxiliary
array; it need not be legal before a repair. Direct substitution in

 V=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2
   -s(s+1)g4^2

gives

 V_j(k)=k sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+k(1-h_i)]p_i^2
        -H_j[1/k+s+R_j+1]q_j^2
       =k P_j(k)/E_j^2,
 P_j=E_j^2 sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+k(1-h_i)]p_i^2
       -H_j[1+k(s+R_j+1)]p_(j+1)^2.                   (8)

For the substitution, the suffix values give
2sum_(i>j)h_i sum_(v>=i)h_v=R_j^2+sum_(i>j)h_i^2;
combining with the suffix gradient diagonals leaves R_j(R_j+1)q_j^2.
Subtracting s(s+1)q_j^2 and the prefix terminal values yields (8).
In particular j=3 is the actual quadratic F(x)=k x^2/2 for all queries.

The constant and linear coefficients of P_j vanish for every schedule.
The constant is H_j-H_j. Write T_j=sum_(i<=j)h_i sum_(v<i)h_v;
then 2T_j=H_j^2-sum_(i<=j)h_i^2. The coefficient of k in the prefix
sum is H_j-H_j^2. Hence the coefficient of k in P_j is
2R_j H_j+H_j-H_j^2-H_j(s+R_j+1-2H_j)=0,
since s=H_j+R_j. Thus P_j=k^2 Q_j, where Q_j is a polynomial.

For j=0, Q_0 is linear and its endpoint values are

 Q_0(0)=256368/3125, Q_0(1)=475104/15625,

both positive. For j=1,2,3, all coefficients in the exact Bernstein
expansions on [0,1/2] and [1/2,1] are positive, as printed below.
Since the Bernstein basis polynomials are nonnegative and sum to1,
Q_j(k)>0 throughout [0,1], and V_j(k)>0 throughout 0<k<=1.

For the C83 repair at a selected cut j=0,1,2, its base cut energy is
V0_j=(1+sum_(i<j)h_i)^2-C^2 eta_j^2>0, eta_j=(h_j-1)/(1+R_j).
The entire curvature test has form

 beta_j(k) V0_j + eta_j(1+eta_j) V_j(k)>=0,

where beta_j(k) is the maximum of0 and the finite residual-repair
ratios. In particular beta_j(k)>=0, including all changing maximizer
branches and beta=0. Both energies have just been proved positive,
so the expression is strictly positive for every curvature and every
branch. No active-residual formula or denominator sign is discarded.
For j=3 the auxiliary is already the full legal quadratic and its
positive energy has been checked directly. The curvature-zero limit
is obtained by continuity; no division by k=0 is asserted.

## 7. Exact polynomial identity certificate

For a degree m polynomial Q(k)=sum_i A_i k^i, its Bernstein coefficients
on [0,1] are B_v=sum_(i<=v) A_i binom(v,i)/binom(m,i).
This identity follows by expanding k^i in the degree-m Bernstein basis.
To obtain the halves, repeatedly replace consecutive coefficients by
their average. The first entries down this triangular array give the
coefficients on [0,1/2], and the last entries in reverse row order give
those on [1/2,1]. This follows from splitting each degree-m Bernstein
basis at its midpoint, or directly from the binomial theorem.

The following exact integer arrays specify Q and the two expansions.
Each array is divided by the stated positive denominator. These are
small, fixed polynomial identities, checked with rational arithmetic;
no sample of k or optimization result is used as a premise.

j=1; power denominator=250000; power numerators=[50793540, -177528981, 237836151, -110684385]
left: denominator=2000000; numerators=[406348320, 169643012, 91495138, 61220313]; all positive=True
right: denominator=2000000; numerators=[61220313, 30945488, 48543712, 3330600]; all positive=True
j=2; power denominator=2500000000; power numerators=[633255870000, -3594456911400, 8982042049995, -11659202708349, 7719844643973, -2066390991819]
left: denominator=400000000000; numerators=[101320939200000, 43809628617600, 22226486235180, 13253106636042, 9010773691434, 6728505601455]; all positive=True
right: denominator=400000000000; numerators=[6728505601455, 4446237511476, 4124034276126, 2990914095504, 3383629498560, 2414712384000]; all positive=True
j=3; power denominator=2500000000; power numerators=[738069932500, -6435214284525, 25550374460520, -58327870129494, 81650300956812, -69585624017973, 33277390306776, -6867072767016]
left: denominator=2800000000000; numerators=[826638324400000, 311821181638000, 137675698349600, 70890394016824, 41454390035320, 26681369684405, 18427013864050, 13413025887415]; all positive=True
right: denominator=2800000000000; numerators=[13413025887415, 8399037910780, 6625417777865, 4813868801830, 4552116709024, 2617934431520, 4409755955200, 396992512000]; all positive=True

## 8. A concrete interval above the threshold also survives all curvatures

Every Bernstein coefficient in Section7 and each endpoint of Q_0
exceeds1/10. This is an exact comparison of the displayed integers.
We retain the same degree for Q_j under variation of t.

For a polynomial A(k), let ||A||_1 be the sum of the absolute values
of its coefficients. This norm is submultiplicative by convolution.
Throughout 0<=t<=1/100, all h_i<3, s<7,R_j<7,H_j<7.
Only h2 depends on t, with derivative1. Thus for i<=4,

 ||p_i||_1<=256, ||partial_t p_i||_1<=64,
 ||p_i^2||_1<=65536, ||partial_t(p_i^2)||_1<=32768.

In the prefix sum Z_j=sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+k(1-h_i)]p_i^2,
i<=3, so ||p_i^2||_1<=4096. The linear coefficient polynomial
has norm h_i^2<9, and its derivative has norm at most6. Therefore

 ||Z_j||_1<=147456,
 ||partial_t Z_j||_1<=4(6*4096+9*32768)=1277952.

Also ||E_j||_1<=8, ||partial_t E_j||_1<=1,
||1+k(s+R_j+1)||_1<=16, and its derivative norm is at most2.
Differentiating (8), and using H_j<=7 and partial_t H_j<=1, yields

 ||partial_t P_j||_1
 <=16*147456+64*1277952+30*65536+112*32768
 =89784320 <10^8.

Division by k^2 just deletes the two identically zero coefficients,
so the same bound holds for partial_t Q_j. Each Bernstein coefficient
has derivative magnitude bounded by this norm, because
0<=binom(v,i)/binom(m,i)<=1; midpoint subdivision takes convex
combinations and preserves the bound. Consequently for
0<=t<=10^(-12), every displayed positive coefficient changes by
less than10^(-4). It remains greater than1/10-1/10000>0.
This proves the all-curvature assertions on that exact interval.

Sections2--5 already proved every other scalar test throughout the
larger interval 0<=t<=1/100. Thus, for example, the exact rational word

 (7/5,9/4,33/25+10^(-12),7/4)

has sum168/25+10^(-12) and passes the ENTIRE specified scalar system,
including the continuum of curvatures and all C82 parameters.

## 9. Exact failed implication and what remains open

The failed implication is:

 'All the cut, quadratic, clipped-tail, adjacent-triangle, backward-lift,
 repaired-last-triangle, C82 and C83 inequalities together exclude every
 four-step schedule with sum at least168/25.'

The displayed rational family disproves that implication. It does not
prove existence of full Lambda/M certificates, and no converse from
these witnesses is supplied. Even an explicit full-cone obstruction for
this family would not eliminate all other schedules.

All cut slacks are genuinely positive. At t=0 the critical second cut
has slack59/500 and the last cut has slack9/50. Neither generic
cut saturation nor an automatic tail restriction is a legal inference.
The remaining exact constructive question is the original full
20-multiplier/five-gradient feasibility problem for an explicit word;
the remaining global exclusion question needs a witness or coefficient
argument beyond the now-refuted scalar coverage claim.

# One exposed triangle: coupled side transport, failed capture, and a paid full-word face switch

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. No new fact ID. Original minimax target OPEN.**

Worker max4, project gd-exact-profile-20260926, invocation88 after ended87.
Guidance5c716c2407472849; plan229ca5c7b9b482af. Exactly one authorized
invocation, cap1. This report is a joint mathematical draft, not an accepted
fact and not a proof of the full triangle profile. Every new ingredient used
here is proved here. The old scalar report5960de36af8e17d5 is awareness only;
none of its unverified lemmas is an accepted premise of this report.

Exactly TWO substantive mechanisms were opened. Mechanism1 derives the
actual fixed-triangle projection cells, a coupled exact complete-child side
identity, and legal failures of the scalar tube/capture migration. Only
after those decisive failures, Mechanism2 treats ONE face-switch pattern:
the first ORIGINAL leaf changes upper side to lower side, and every remaining
original sample, including the terminal, is on that lower side. Its full
parameter domain is paid analytically, uniformly over the angle and cap.
An explicit finite off-axis full-word member proves the pattern is legal.

No mathematical programs, arithmetic/numerical/trajectory/recurrence replays,
solvers, optimizers, benchmark DP, formal calls/fact_submit, installations,
consults, subagents, external publication, controller/service actions,
finalization, revocation or Goal completion were performed. Tool uses were
administrative text reads/writes and shared-memory/literature retrieval.

## 1. Exact scope and outcome

For all the parameters below, the candidate conclusions established jointly
in this report are:

1. Every sloping-side complete child, with all its original queries AND its
   terminal in that same side cell, obeys the sharp coupled identity(12).
